# South Sudan — Mathematics (Ministry of General Education and Instruction)

READ COMPLETE. Files in hand (reconciled against the record):
**24 files / 24 distinct works — 12 student books + 12 teacher guides**,
Grades Primary 1–8 + Secondary 1, 2, 4, **2,237 PDF pages** in total
(Band A, Primary 1–4: 667; Band B, Primary 5–8: 708;
Band C, Secondary 1, 2, 4: 862; 174,399,531 bytes, ~166 MB).
Anchors: **503 (475 passed, 27 corrected, 1 not found)** —
Band A 158 (150 passed, 7 corrected, 1 not found),
Band B 176 (170 passed, 6 corrected, 0 not found),
Band C 169 (155 passed, 14 corrected, 0 not found).
Recorded 22 in hand of 26 — reconciled **24 in hand**: the inventory
missed two in-hand files (Primary 1 Teacher's Guide, Primary 3 Pupil's
Book). 4 link-only: the whole Secondary 3 (Grade 11) set.

## The set at a glance

- **One series, one spine.** All 24 books are the Republic of South Sudan
  Ministry of General Education and Instruction mathematics series, ©2018,
  GPE-funded, written by Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers (Kenya),
  approved by Minister Deng Deng Hoc Yai with effect from 4 February 2019.
  All in English. **No ISBN is printed in any book; no individual authors
  are named anywhere.**
- **Grades covered:** Primary 1–8 (16 files) and Secondary 1, 2 and 4
  (8 files). There is no Secondary 3 (Grade 11) book in hand at all — the
  whole S3 set (4 books) is link-only.
- **Primary arc.** P1: numbers to 99, odd/even, ordinals, place value,
  two-digit addition/subtraction, non-standard measure, SSP notes, o'clock
  time, days/months, lines and shapes. P2: three-digit numbers, number-line
  addition/subtraction, place value, column addition with carrying,
  subtraction without borrowing, rounding, multiplication and division
  tables, fractions as parts of a whole, metres, kg comparisons, half-litres,
  giving change, time to quarters, triangle/square/rectangle properties.
  P3: four-digit numbers, divisibility for 2/5/10, equivalent fractions,
  cm/m conversions, litres/ml/cl/dl, kg/g, time conversions, a.m./p.m.,
  symmetry of shapes, first inequalities, pictographs and block graphs.
  P4: five-digit numbers, rounding to thousands, multiples/factors,
  same-denominator fractions, ratio, percentages, rectangle/square area,
  volume by counting cubes, profit/loss, 12/24-hour clock, angles with a
  protractor, like terms, bar/line/pie graphs. P5: six-digit numbers,
  divisibility for 3/4/6/9, primes, Roman numerals to 50, HCF/LCM, fractions
  with LCM, decimals, m/km, cube/cuboid volume, temperature conversion,
  parallel-line and angle construction, one-step equations, bar/line/pie
  graphs. P6: numbers to a million, divisibility by 8/11, squares and roots,
  decimal–fraction–percent conversions, ratio/proportion, π and circle area,
  acres/hectares, triangle area, capacity, tonnes, profit/loss, angle
  bisection, vertically opposite/supplementary angles, circle construction,
  nets, scale, two-step equations, expanding brackets, tables and graphs,
  coordinates. P7: squares/roots of fractions and decimals, cubes, unitary
  method, percentage change, circumference, quadrilateral areas, surface
  area, speed, weight, temperature, transversals, triangle construction,
  Pythagoras, parallelogram/trapezium construction, linear scale,
  simplifying/substituting/forming equations, sets and Venn diagrams,
  mean/median/mode, scale drawings, probability, business accounting
  (profit/loss, discount, simple interest, commission, hire purchase,
  bills). P8: factors/multiples including negatives, squares/roots by prime
  factors and by averaging, decimal/fraction roots, fraction–decimal–percent
  conversions, perimeter/area of all plane shapes, surface area and volume
  of cubes/cuboids/cylinders, time–speed–distance, inscribed/circumscribed
  circles, Pythagoras, pyramids/prisms, scale, coordinates, simplifying
  expressions, sets, finite/infinite sets, reading tables/graphs,
  probability, commissions, discounts, hire purchase, profit/loss, simple
  and compound interest, cash accounts.
- **Secondary arc.** S1: natural numbers/factors/proportion,
  fractions/decimals/percentages, standard form, profit/loss, measurement
  (perimeter, area, surface area, volume, capacity), geometry/trigonometry
  (angles, triangles, polygons, quadrilaterals, solids, transformations,
  bearings, scale, trig ratios, elevation/depression), algebra (simplify,
  substitute, expand, factorise, equations, simultaneous equations,
  inequalities), statistics and probability (mean/mode/median, probability,
  tree diagrams). S2: indices, surds, commercial arithmetic (forex,
  simple/compound interest, tax, hire purchase), coordinate geometry,
  Pythagoras (proofs left as learner tasks), unit circle, quadratics by
  factorisation, vectors, sets/Venn, 2×2 matrices, functions
  (composite/inverse), assumed mean, cumulative frequency/ogive. S4
  regular: complex numbers (basic), area estimation (mid-ordinate and
  trapezium rules), circle equations, trig graphs, linear
  inequalities/programming, permutations/combinations, vectors (2D/3D),
  calculus (differentiation from first principles, tangents/normals,
  stationary points, kinematics, integration as area). S4 Additional:
  limits, trig equations, calculus 3 (product/quotient/implicit,
  exponential/log/trig derivatives and integrals, kinematics), partial
  fractions, vectors (dot product, line equations), complex numbers (polar
  form, De Moivre, roots).
- **Regular vs Additional at S4 proven NOT a subset.** Only the regular
  book teaches area estimation, circle equations, trig graphs, linear
  programming and permutations/combinations; only the Additional book
  teaches limits, trig equations, advanced differentiation/integration,
  partial fractions and De Moivre. The overlap is complex numbers, vectors,
  trigonometry and calculus at different depths.
- **Guide verdicts.** P1 guide has NO answer key at all (its introduction
  promises one); P2 guide is the strongest companion in the primary band;
  P3 guide is weak, with error-prone keys that repeat the pupil's errors
  verbatim; P4 guide keys most of Units 1, 2 and 4 with fresh errors of its
  own. P5–P8 guides promise topics the student books never teach
  (litres↔ml, acute/obtuse/reflex angles, scale drawing, coordinates,
  rotational symmetry, protractor use, translation, grouped-data statistics,
  business plans/spreadsheets, break-even points) and never flag them as
  extras. S1 guide never covers inequalities though its front-matter
  promises them. S2 guide ends with no answers for the closing
  cumulative-frequency exercise. S4 Maths guide reproduces the 30
  permutation multiple-choice questions with NO answer key. S4 Additional
  guide adds a "Limits and series" section with no student-book counterpart.
- **Series-wide template errors.** 7 of the 8 secondary books say "primary
  series" / "primary school syllabus" and "equiping" on the blurb page and
  carry a stray "PRI" token on the title page. The S4 Additional Mathematics
  guide is the exception (smaller page size, no blurb page, no such wording).
- **The books are error-prone — every filing carries a slips list, and
  several guides add fresh errors of their own.** Worst, never to be taught
  as fact: P3 student p. 17 counts 1/4 among the equivalents of 1/2; P8
  student p. 37 prints a cuboid surface-area formula repeating LW three
  times and p. 39 says "divide by 10,000" for cm³→m³; S4 Maths student
  pp. 95–96 area-by-integration examples (wrong integrand, "64/3" copied
  as "64/8", negative area −2/3); S4 Additional student p. 194 cube roots
  of 8 (√8 for ∛8, wrong exponents, k = 2 root repeats k = 1); P7 guide
  p. 25 temperature formulas without the 32 offset; P8 guide Exercise 10
  speed key 6 of 10 wrong; S2 guide p. 11 sterling rate wrong by a factor
  of ten (£1 = SSP 17.50 for 175).
- **The one unverified anchor.** P3 student equivalent fractions
  1/2 = 2/4 = 4/8 — the chain is not printed anywhere in the book;
  Activity 27 claims 1/2, 1/4, 3/6, 4/8, 5/10 "all indicate the same
  part", which is wrong for 1/4. Marked UNVERIFIED BY CHECK, never to be
  cited.

## How this filing was made — read this first

- **Grab.** All 24 PDFs pulled from the secured GitHub library via the
  authenticated pusher/API route (20 tree files via the Contents API, 4
  release assets), each verified byte-exact against the API-recorded size
  with %%EOF present and a pdfinfo page count. Total 174,399,531 bytes.
- **Cover/title-page proof.** Every file's grade and kind proved from its
  own cover and title page; neighbouring grades swap-checked (no G4/G5
  swap in maths, unlike the English phase, where the G4/G5 Pupil's Books
  were swapped); SHA-256 all distinct — no duplicate groups.
- **Reads.** Every distinct work read in full, page by page, in three
  parallel bands (P1–P4, P5–P8, S1/S2/S4). Filings are the readers' own
  summaries and structural notes, only very short quoted fragments.
  Methods are stated honestly per book: the P1 student book is a
  scanned-image book (114 of 118 pages with no text layer), read by OCR of
  all 118 rendered pages plus visual reads; P7 student passages extract as
  Caesar-shifted gibberish, decoded by the consistent +3 shift and verified
  visually; the degree symbol renders as "0" in text layers throughout
  (an extraction artefact, not a print error).
- **Printed-page convention.** Every citation uses the book's PRINTED page;
  the PDF→printed offset is stated at the head of each book section
  (printed = PDF − offset). Two guides have folio errors (S1 guide prints
  p. 16 twice and skips p. 86; S4 Maths guide prints a solutions page as
  folio "2" and repeats folio "14") — citations use the guide's own
  pagination as stated in its section.
- **Citation checks.** A separate checker verified 15–22 anchors per work
  on the exact PDF pages (503 anchors total); failures were corrected in
  the filings in place; counts are written at the top of each book section.
  The one not-found anchor is marked UNVERIFIED BY CHECK in the P3 student
  section. One further slip (P1 student p. 35 number chart) was found by
  the Band A checker and folded into the P1 student slips list below.

## Editions as printed (per file — never borrow a year across files)

All 24 books: ©2018, The Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General
Education and Instruction — **except five files where no © line is printed
or extractable**: P3 student, P3 teacher, P5 teacher, P7 student, P7
teacher (same 2018 series by foreword and approval date, but the year is
not printed on the pages read). **No ISBN is printed in any book. No
individual authors are named in any book.** Publisher: Mountain Top
Publishers (named in the imprint of most books; Longhorn and Mountain Top
thanked in the forewords). Funded by the Global Partnership for Education;
foreword by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, approval effective 4 February 2019.

# BAND A — Primary 1–4 (8 files, 667 PDF pages)

All 8 books are in English; all 8 filings are in English. One series throughout: Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, ©2018 (except the two Grade 3 books, where no © line is extractable), GPE-funded, Longhorn / Mountain Top (Kenya).


## Grade 1, student — Pupil's Book 1 (© 2018; 118 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 5)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 20 anchors — 18 passed, 2 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Pupil's Book 1 (cover: "Primary Mathematics 1"; title page: "Mathematics — Pupil's Book 1", "PRIMARY 1", "South Sudan")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Copyright line (title page, verified visually):** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved. No part of this book may be reproduced by any means graphic, electronic, mechanical, photocopying, taping, storage and retrieval system without prior written permission of the Copyright Holder." No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named anywhere. No publisher name is printed in the book itself; the foreword thanks "Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya" (commissioned by the Curriculum Foundation UK to write the new textbooks). "Funded by: GLOBAL PARTNERSHIP for EDUCATION — quality education for all children". "This book is the property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE."
- **Foreword:** by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Hon. Minister of General Education and Instruction; approves the textbook for use in all schools of South Sudan "with effect from 4th February, 2019". Thanks the Curriculum Development Centre staff, the Curriculum Foundation (UK) under Dr Brian Male, UNICEF South Sudan (GPE-funded project management), the British Council's Tony Calderbank, DfID's Sir Richard Arden, and former Ministers.
- **File:** `g01-student.pdf` — 118 PDF pages, 22,561,266 bytes. Printed pagination: front matter unnumbered (PDF pp. 1–6), then printed pp. 1–113 (PDF pp. 6–118). **Printed page = PDF page − 5** throughout (PDF p. 7 = printed p. 2; PDF p. 118 = printed p. 113). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
This is a **scanned-image book: 114 of its 118 PDF pages have no extractable text layer at all** (only the inside-cover/imprint text extracts). I read it by rendering all 118 pages at 200 dpi and running OCR (RapidOCR, ~45 s/page, all 118 pages) over the renders, then reading the full OCR output page by page, and **visually reading the rendered pages wherever the OCR was ambiguous** — the title page (© line), the number-words table (printed p. 29), the ordering activity (printed p. 44), the place-value example (printed p. 37), the days-of-the-week page (printed p. 99) and the shop price list (printed p. 93). Where OCR column order scrambled a table, I checked the image and report the printed layout, not the OCR order. Picture-led pages are described from what the pictures show; I do not invent numbers the pictures do not carry. No section was sampled: every page was read (OCR + visual check where needed). The companion Teacher's Guide 1 was read separately (see g01-teacher.md).

**Font quirk (affects reading, not content):** the book's font renders the digit "1" like a capital "I" — e.g. "I0 SSP" for 10 SSP, "I ten and 2 ones = I2", "Idividually". This is a typeface choice, not a misprint, but it is easy to misread.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit

#### Front matter (PDF pp. 1–6, unnumbered)
- Inside cover: "Primary Mathematics has been written and developed by Ministry of General Education and Instruction… in conjunction with Subjects experts… The book comprehensively covers the Primary 1 syllabus… Each year comprises of a Pupil's Book and teacher's Guide."
- "How to take care of your books" — 8 do's, 8 don'ts.
- Title page (PDF p. 3) with the ©2018 line, ministry seal, GPE funding mark, "THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE".
- Foreword (PDF p. 4).
- Table of Contents (PDF p. 5) — see the slips section: its page numbers are systematically 2–4 pages lower than the printed pages.

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 2–70)
Content map (PDF p. 6, unnumbered): count, read and write numbers 0–99; even and odd numbers; ordinal numbers 1st–10th; sort, match and arrange groups; add and subtract whole numbers up to two digits.

- **1.1 Sorting, Grouping and Matching (pp. 2–19), Activities 1–12.** By colour (market pictures, "Colours" song, favourite colour), by shape (cut-outs, "which ones look the same as the green ones?"), by size ("Elephant, Elephant" song; a school bus with plate "KAU 1004"; bigger/smaller comparisons), by texture (rough/smooth, classroom hunt).
- **1.2 Counting Numbers (pp. 20–31), Activities 13–22.** "A Fish Alive" song; counting chart 1–99 (Act 13); missing numbers in sequences (Act 14); counting in tens — 10 Ten, 20 Twenty … 100 Hundred (Act 15a/b); counting objects and writing the answer (Act 16); "How many?" (Act 17); smaller/bigger number (Act 18); number after / before / in between (Act 19 A/B/C — answers 13, 55, 20, 27, 68, 62, 32, 74, 5); writing numbers in words 1–15 with a matchstick table — 1 One, 2 Two, 3 Three, 4 Four, 5 Five (p. 29), then 6–15 (p. 30) (Act 20); write the number in words or the numeral — 20, 56, 35, 22, 83, 43; ninety-nine, fifty-four, seventy-seven, ninety-seven, sixty-five, sixty, ninety-one, seventy, forty-two, twelve, seventy-six, eighty-six, forty-nine, thirty-four (Act 21); number-words blackboard game (Act 22).
- **1.3 Odd and Even Numbers (pp. 32–35), Activities 23–26.** Clap "even and odd" (Act 23); odd or even, with reasons (Act 24); count in 2s and in 5s to 40 (Act 25a/b); copy the 1–100 number chart, circle evens, cross out odds (Act 26).
- **1.4 Place Value (pp. 36–45), Activities 27–35.** "Who is on the ones side?" (Act 27); "Place value is where a digit is in a number" — 12 = 1 ten, 2 ones; 20 = two bundles of 10 matchsticks = 2 tens, 0 is ones (p. 37); collecting and grouping objects (Act 28); ones counting (Act 29); tens/ones tables — 11 Eleven, 12 Twelve, 13 Thirteen, 14 Fourteen, 20 Twenty (Act 30); "say how many tens, and how many ones" — 21, 23, 37, 25, 50, 60, 75, 92, 48, 73, 67 (Act 31); "1 ten and 2 ones = 12" (Act 32); "Ten Little Ducks" song (Act 33); ascending and descending order — cards 1–10 arranged, with printed answer rows (Act 34, p. 44 — see slips); ordering sets ascending and descending (Act 35, p. 45).
- **1.5 Addition (pp. 46–54), Activities 36–43.** "Addition is putting together two or more things or numbers" (p. 46); collecting sticks (Act 36, Deng, Taban, Kiden and Nyandeng); put-together pictures (Act 37); finger addition (Act 38); sums in the exercise book (Acts 39–40); two-digit sums — 2+2, 16+13, 16+10, 12+25, 16+3, 22+14, 23+11, 22+6, 12+7, 23+13 (Act 41, p. 52); column addition (Act 42, p. 53); missing addends — "What do you add to make sum total of the number in green?" e.g. 9 = 4 + ?, 18 = 9 + ?, 13 = ? + 7, 12 = 8 + ?, 10 = ? + 6, 14 = 6 + ?, 15 = ? + 8, 7 = ? + 4 — plus tens/ones column sums (Act 43, p. 54).
- **1.6 Subtraction / Take Away (pp. 55–60), Activities 44–51.** "Subtraction is taking one number or amount away from another number" (p. 55); take-away with fingers (Acts 44–45); dot-picture subtractions — 5−2, 3−3, 6−3, 8−4, 10−5 (Act 46, p. 56); worked examples — "90 − 10 = 80 (Without regrouping)" and "188 − 69 = 119 (With regrouping)" (p. 57); subtraction without regrouping (3−2, 17−4, 16−12, 6−3, 16−6, 15−10, 14−2, 18−6, 18−8, 18−5, 23−13, 20−10, 15−12, 17−6, 15−10) and with regrouping (13−6, 11−5, 10−8, 18−9, 16−4, 16−7, 32−25, 13−5, 10−3, 14−7, 17−9, 13−9, 10−3, 13−9, 11−7) (Act 47, p. 57); picture subtractions (Acts 48, 50); board problem in groups (Act 49); 26 missing-number subtractions, e.g. 6 − ? = ?, 10 − ? = ?, ? − 3 = 6, 7 − 1 = ?, ? − 5 = 3, 9 − ? = ?, 8 − 6 = ?, 15 − 12 = ?, 18 − ? = 10, ? − 3 = 13, ? − 14 = 4, 13 − 2 = ?, 20 − ? = ?, 12 − 7 = ? (Act 51, p. 60).
- **1.7 Ordinal Numbers (pp. 61–67), Activities 52–59.** First/second/third — who got into the classroom first (Acts 52–53); race positions, Paul and John (Act 54a/b); "Who do you think will touch the water first?" (Act 55); 1st First … 10th Tenth with words (pp. 63–65); "Who will be first to step on to the road?" (Act 56); position in pictures — 1st first, 2nd second, 3rd third, 4th fourth, 5th fifth, 6th sixth, 7th seventh, 8th eighth, 9th ninth, 10th tenth (Act 57, p. 66); draw and colour ordinals (Act 58); "Say which position the shape is in" — circles and stars (Act 59, p. 68).
- **"Let us discuss" (pp. 69–70).** A sharing problem with four named children (Achal, Poni, Wani and Lado) and a set of SSP prices — 40, 30, 70, 80, 60 — the unit's closing discussion page (no answers given).

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 71–102)
Content map (p. 71): days of the week and months of the year; common notes in local currency; estimate and compare length, capacity and weights using arbitrary units; simple money problems; tell time on the clock face in hours and half hours.

- **2.1 Length (pp. 72–77), Activities 1–5.** Estimate walking time home→school (Act 1); count steps to the headteacher's office, toilet block and school gate (p. 73); "Which is longer?" — crayons (Act 2, p. 74); taller than / shorter than (Act 3, p. 75); measure desk, play field, teacher's table and a classroom wall with hand spans, strides, arms and feet, and compare groups' different results (Act 4, p. 76); bottle tops, books and other non-standard units (Act 5, p. 77). All measurement is non-standard; no ruler, no cm/m.
- **2.2 Mass (pp. 78–83), Activities 6–11.** "Can you lift these things?" (Act 6); heavy or light (Act 7); visit shops/markets to ask what they use to weigh things (Act 8); "Who do you think is heavier? Kenyi in blue dress or Nyandeng in green dress?" (Act 9, p. 81); "Which one can you lift?" (Act 10); heavier comparisons (Act 11). No balance scale is built in the book (the guide adds a shoebox balance); no kg/g.
- **2.3 Capacity (pp. 84–87), Activities 12–15.** Pouring and comparing containers (Act 12); "How many glasses will fill the bucket? How many full cups do you get from a full jug? How many small buckets will fill the big bucket?" — plus the same at home with cups, bottles and jugs (Act 13, p. 85); comparing containers (Act 14); "Which one can hold more? Why do you say so?" (Act 15, p. 87). No litres.
- **2.4 Currency (pp. 88–93), Activities 16–20.** Market pictures (Act 16); "Discuss what is happening in the picture. What do you think Kenyi and Taban are saying?" (Act 17); "Grandmother Pussycat" song (p. 90); Bank of South Sudan notes shown — 1, 5, 10, 20, 50 and 100 SSP (Act 18, p. 91; the 50 and 100 notes carry 2015 dates); shop price list (Act 20, p. 93): Sweet Bread 25 SSP, pen 15 SSP, New Exercise book 30 SSP, eraser 10 SSP, ink (Pilot) 20 SSP, pencil 10 SSP, Toned Milk 40 SSP, socks 50 SSP. No adding of prices and no change-giving is taught in the book itself.
- **2.5 Time (pp. 94–97), Activities 21–24.** "What time do you think it is?" — school-activity pictures (Act 21, p. 94); o'clock faces (Act 23, p. 96); times of day — "Good morning", "Goodnight" (Act 22); "Look at the clocks below. Each group to present to the class what they normally do at these times" (Act 24). Only o'clock/half-hour by picture; no minutes, no a.m./p.m. labels.
- **2.6 Days of the Week (pp. 98–100), Activities 25–27.** Weekday pictures (Act 25); all seven days named — Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday (Act 26, p. 99); days ordering game (Act 27, p. 100).
- **2.7 Months of the Year (pp. 101–102), Activities 28–29.** All twelve months with ordinals — January 1st, February 2nd, March 3rd, April 4th, May 5th, June 6th, July 7th, August 8th, September 9th, October 10th, November 11th, December 12th (Act 28, p. 101); make month cards (Act 29, p. 102).

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 103–113)
Content map (p. 103): recognise simple geometric shapes; observe shapes and patterns; draw geometrical lines and shapes; count sides and angles and sort by these properties; distinguish straight and curved lines; observe shapes made when lines meet and intersect; explore how 2-D shapes fit together; "Investigate 3-D shapes and make models using clay".

- **3.1 Lines (pp. 104–105), Activities 1–3.** Lines in groups of five (Act 1); "Draw and colour" (Acts 2–3, p. 105). Straight and curved lines are named in the content map but no line-type labels appear in the activities themselves.
- **3.2 Shapes (pp. 106–113), Activities 4–12.** Shape pictures (Act 4, p. 106); "Draw these shapes in your exercise book" / "Draw and colour these shapes" (Acts 5–9, pp. 107–110); cut-and-glue shapes (Act 10, p. 111); group shape work (Act 11, p. 112); "How many different shapes can you see in the picture?" (Act 12, p. 113). The book ends here — no revision, no index, no back cover content.

### Companion note — against Teacher's Guide 1
The guide companions this book in structure and order (same 3 units, same sub-units, same activity numbering 1–59, same songs and named children). The guide's activity walkthrough matches the pupil's pages throughout; the guide adds teaching method (sorting games, abacus-making, shoebox balance, paper-plate clock) and the "Let us discuss" answers. Differences: the guide gives **no answer key at all** (its introduction promises answers, but none appear), and the guide's Unit 3 overview promises 3-D shapes (cube, cylinder, pyramid, clay modelling) that neither the guide nor the pupil's book covers. The guide's Unit 1 overview (p. 6) also lists pupil's Activities 18–22 and 32–33, which its own unit walkthrough never returns to. See g01-teacher.md for the full guide filing.

### What it does NOT teach (honest negatives)
- No multiplication, no division, no fractions, no decimals.
- No standard units anywhere: no centimetres/metres, no kilograms/grams, no litres — length, mass and capacity are all estimated and compared with non-standard units (hand spans, strides, steps, cups, glasses, buckets).
- No money arithmetic: prices are shown (Act 20) but the book never adds prices together or works out change; that is left to the teacher (the guide's role-play).
- No clock minutes, no a.m./p.m., no calendar use (days and months are named and ordered only).
- No 3-D shapes, despite the Unit 3 content map promising them ("Investigate 3-D shapes and make models using clay") and the guide's overview promising cube, cylinder and pyramid.
- No data, graphs, patterns beyond shape sorting, symmetry, or angles.
- Numbers stay within 0–99 (counting to 100 only in tens) — except the single 3-digit subtraction example on p. 57.
- No answers anywhere: every activity is "do it in your exercise book" with nothing to check against.

### Held-copy defects
- The file is a **scan, not a typeset PDF**: 114 of 118 pages are images with no text layer. The scan itself is complete and legible — all 118 pages present, in order, none blurred beyond reading.
- **Table of Contents page numbers are systematically wrong** (see slips): every listed page number is 2–4 pages lower than the printed page where the section actually starts.
- The digit "1" is set in a font where it looks like a capital "I" throughout (e.g. "I0 SSP", "I ten") — easy to misread, and it defeated the OCR on several pages.
- Two pages carry upside-down text fragments in the content maps (Unit 1 map, PDF p. 6; Unit 3 map, PDF p. 108) — layout artefacts, not content.

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Table of Contents (PDF p. 5) — every page number is wrong.** Listed vs actual printed page: 1.2 Counting "18" (actual p. 20); 1.3 Odd and even "28" (actual p. 32); 1.4 Place Value "32" (actual p. 36); 1.5 Addition "43" (actual p. 46); 1.6 Subtraction "52" (actual p. 55); 1.7 Ordinal Numbers "58" (actual p. 61); UNIT 2 "68" (actual p. 71); 2.1 Length "69" (actual p. 72); 2.2 Mass "75" (actual p. 78); 2.3 Capacity "81" (actual p. 84); 2.4 Currency "85" (actual p. 88); 2.5 Time "90" (actual p. 94); 2.6 Days of the Week "94" (actual p. 98); 2.7 Months "97" (actual p. 101); UNIT 3 "100" (actual p. 103); 3.1 Lines "101" (actual p. 104); 3.2 Shapes "104" (actual p. 106).
2. **Printed p. 29 (Activity 19C, "Which number comes in between?"):** the items are numbered 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11 — items 4 and 8 are missing from the numbering.
3. **Printed p. 29 (Activity 20 header):** "Idividually" for "Individually".
4. **Printed p. 44 (Activity 34, ascending/descending order):** the cards to arrange are 1–10, but the printed answer rows run only 1–9 ascending (1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9) and 9–1 descending (9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2 1) — **10 is omitted from both rows**. The question on the same page also reads "What do yo notice?" for "you".
5. **Printed p. 57 (Activity 47, worked examples):** "188 − 69 = 119 (With regrouping)" — the arithmetic is correct, but a 3-digit subtraction sits in a Primary 1 book whose stated scope is "Add whole numbers up to two digits" / "Subtract whole numbers up to two digit-numbers" (Unit 1 content map). It is out of scope for the grade, not a wrong answer.
6. **Printed p. 103 (Unit 3 content map) and the guide's Unit 3 overview:** 3-D shapes are promised ("Investigate 3-D shapes and make models using clay"; cube, cylinder, pyramid) but no activity in the book covers any 3-D shape.
7. **Reused foreign imagery (not a maths error, but a content note):** the school bus in Activity 9 (p. 15) carries a Kenyan plate "KAU 1004"; the shop pictures (pp. 92–93) show a Kenyan-style "Sweet Bread" wholemeal loaf and an Indian-style "Toned Milk" packet — not South Sudanese products. Same pattern as the G02/G03 books' Kenyan retail imagery.
8. **Printed p. 35 (Activity 26 number chart):** row 7 reads "61 62 62 64 …" — 63 is misprinted as a second 62. (Found by the citation checker, 2026-11-05; recorded in band-a/check.md, not in the reader's original filing.)

## Grade 1, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 1 (no © line extractable; 79 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 5)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 20 anchors — 20 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Teacher's Guide 1 (cover: "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 1", "PRIMARY 1")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Imprint (PDF p. 1, inside cover):** "MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS" named as publisher; "Primary Mathematics has been written and developed by Ministry of General Education and Instruction, Government of South Sudan in conjunction with Subjects experts." "This Book is the Property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. This Book is not for sale." Funded by the Global Partnership for Education (logo). No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named. No © line is extractable from this file's text layer (the © line, if present, sits in the cover artwork).
- **Front matter:** "How to take care of your books" (PDF p. 2); title page (PDF p. 3); contents (PDF p. 5). No ministerial foreword in this file.
- **File:** `g01-teacher.pdf` — 79 PDF pages, 10,522,583 bytes. Printed pagination: printed pp. 1–74. **Printed page = PDF page − 5** throughout (PDF p. 6 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 79 = printed p. 74). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete). This guide is text-rich (79 pages, ~82,000 characters of text) with almost no picture-only pages. No section was sampled: every page's text was read. The companion Pupil's Book 1 was read separately (its text layer is scanned images; see g01-student.md) — companion verdicts below rest on both books.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit
The guide has a 5-page introduction (printed pp. 1–5) then three units. The introduction covers: purpose; teaching strategies (problem-based learning, open-ended questions — the worked example is a rectangle of perimeter 160 cm, "50 cm × 2 + 30 cm × 2" — group work, peer teaching); classroom assessment by observation / conversation / product ("triangulation"). The same introduction, word for word, appears in the Primary 2, 3 and 4 guides (including the same 160 cm rectangle example and the same "multiply three-digit numbers by two-digit numbers" peer-teaching example, which is far above Primary 1 level).

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (guide printed pp. 6–43)
- **Unit overview (pp. 6–7):** "Learn about" table, key inquiry questions, learning outcomes (count/read/write 0–99; even/odd; ordinals 1st–10th; add/subtract two-digit numbers), competencies and links to other subjects.
- **1.1 Sorting and grouping (pp. 8–17).** Long teaching notes on sorting by colour/texture/size/shape (beans, forks, buttons, bowls); songs ("Colours"; "Elephant, Elephant… too big"); Activities 1–12 of the pupil's book: sorting/matching objects, colours, shapes (triangle 3 sides, circle, square), size (bigger/smaller), texture (smooth/rough). Note: the guide's Activity 12 is texture; the pupil's book TOC lists 1.1 as "Sorting, Grouping and Matching".
- **1.2 Counting numbers (pp. 18–23).** Counting 1–99 (ball toss, snap/clap/foot-tap, blocks, jumping, balloon taps); songs ("A Fish Alive", 1–10); number recognition; Activities 13–16: counting, missing numbers, counting in 2s/5s/10s, counting backwards from 100/90/80…
- **1.3 Odd and even numbers (pp. 24–25).** Odd = 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11… ("cannot be divided evenly into two groups"); even = 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12… ("divisible by 2"); clapping evens/odds; Activities 17, 23–25 (counting in 2s and 5s; "skip a number… skip four numbers and count the fifth").
- **1.4 Place value (pp. 26–29).** Tens and ones with sticks/bottle tops; Activities 26–31 (circle evens/cross odds; ones/tens roles; arranging sticks in tens; counting balls; "how many tens and ones"); ordering ascending/descending defined (p. 29).
- **1.5 Addition (pp. 30–34).** Activities 34–35 (ordering 1–10; smallest/biggest) then Activities 36–43: addition with objects (blocks, buttons, coins, dice, bodies as manipulatives); "+" and "=" symbols; horizontal then vertical number sentences; addition vocabulary (altogether, put together, total, sum); skip counting, doubles (3 + 3 = 6; 8 + 8 = 16), flashcards; "Addition is calculating the total of two or more numbers".
- **1.6 Subtraction / taking away (pp. 35–37).** Activities 44–51: removing objects from a pile, flashcards (5 take away 2 = 3), fingers; "Subtraction is taking one number or amount away from another number" (p. 36).
- **1.7 Ordinal numbers (pp. 38–43).** Ordinal forms table (first 1st, second 2nd, third 3rd, fourth 4th … twenty-sixth 26th, 101st; compounds 421st, 5,111th); Activities 52–59: lining up and saying positions; who was first/second/third into class; groups of 10 in a line; counting mangoes/boys/girls; tens and ones in prices. The unit ends with two "Let us discuss" picture prompts (mangoes on a tree; boys/girls heights) on p. 43.

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (guide printed pp. 44–67)
- **Unit overview (pp. 44–45):** length/capacity/weight by estimation and arbitrary units (hand span, strides, foot length); local currency (1, 5, 10, 20, 50 SSP); time of day, days of the week, months of the year; telling time in hours and half hours.
- **2.1 Length (pp. 46–49).** Activities 1–5: steps between places; longer/shorter; taller/shorter buildings; hand-span/arm/feet measuring of desk, table, wall, playfield; bottle tops covering the maths book.
- **2.2 Mass (pp. 50–53).** Activities 6–11: comparing weights by hand and with a "shoebox balance" (can + lid + playdoh; detailed build instructions, pp. 51–53); size vs weight (feather, marble, ball — see slips); objects weighing the same as a rubber/eraser.
- **2.3 Capacity (pp. 54–56).** Activities 12–15: volume/capacity taught together; estimating jar contents; pouring/comparing ("greater than / less than / equal to"); jug = 8 glasses; kettle = 5 cups; bucket = 16 mugs; milk can = 25 bottles.
- **2.4 Currency/Money (pp. 57–61).** Activities 16–20: counting money as a functional skill; role-play shopping with coloured chips as money and price cards; saving activity (make a coin bank from a cylinder); needs vs wants; criticising adverts; price questions (loaf of bread, socks, rubber, bottle of ink; rubber + pen; what can be bought with 15 SSP).
- **2.5 Time — daily routines (pp. 62–64).** Activities 21–25: morning/noon/evening/night; yesterday/today/tomorrow; making a paper-plate clock; measuring shadows at different times of day.
- **2.6 Days of the week (pp. 65–66).** Activities 26–27: "What day comes after Monday? … before Saturday?" etc.; today/tomorrow/yesterday for Monday, Saturday, Sunday.
- **2.7 Months of the year (p. 66).** Activities 28–29: covering month cards; arranging 12 month cards in order.

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (guide printed pp. 68–74)
- **Unit overview (pp. 68–69):** shapes (square, rectangle, triangle, circle), straight/curved lines, intersecting lines, 2-D shapes fitting together, 3-D shapes (cube, cylinder, pyramid) modelled in clay.
- **3.1 Lines (p. 69).** Activities 1–2: forming lines with bodies; copying top-bottom, left-right, curved and circular strokes.
- **3.2 Shapes (pp. 70–74).** Activities 3–11: collecting materials and describing shapes/patterns; naming classroom objects by shape; drawing as many circles/squares/triangles/rectangles as possible (not ovals, not rectangles when drawing squares, etc.); constructing an object (Activity 10); naming shapes and their sides/corners. The guide ends at printed p. 74 with no answer-key section, no revision and no index.

### Companion verdict — does it companion Pupil's Book 1?
**Yes, in structure and order; with the caveats below.** The guide covers the same 3 units in the same order as the pupil's book contents (Unit 1 Numbers pp. 2–70 of the pupil's book; Unit 2 Measurement pp. 71–102; Unit 3 Geometry pp. 103–113) [corrected 2026-11-05: the filing had copied the contents page's wrong numbers], with the same sub-unit titles and the same activity numbering (Activities 1–59 in Unit 1, 1–29 in Unit 2, 1–11 in Unit 3). Every sub-unit in the pupil's book has a matching guide section, and no guide section lacks a pupil's-book counterpart.
- **Page keys:** the guide's contents page numbers are the GUIDE's own pages, not the pupil's-book pages (e.g. guide lists 1.2 Counting at guide p. 18; the pupil's book has counting at pupil's p. 18 by coincidence for 1.2, but 1.3 odd/even is guide p. 24 vs pupil's p. 28; 1.5 addition guide p. 30 vs pupil's p. 43; 1.7 ordinals guide p. 38 vs pupil's p. 58). Where the guide cites a pupil's-book page it is usually right in substance ("(Page 2) of the learner's book" for the colours song, guide p. 10) but the guide never gives a systematic pupil's-page key.
- **Answers:** the introduction promises "a series of exercises that come at the very end of each sub-topic and their answers are provided in this teachers guide" (guide p. 1). That promise is NOT kept: this guide gives teaching notes and activity guidance but essentially no answer lists — no worked answers for the pupil's counting, addition, subtraction or ordinal exercises. A teacher cannot check pupils' answers from this guide.
- **Scope mismatches:** (a) the Unit 3 overview promises 3-D shapes (cube, cylinder, pyramid) and clay modelling, but neither the guide's Unit 3 activities nor the pupil's book's Unit 3 (shapes pp. 104–110) actually covers 3-D shapes; (b) the Unit 1 overview promises counting "to 99" and two-digit addition/subtraction, which matches the pupil's book; (c) the introduction's worked examples (perimeter 160 cm; multiplying 3-digit by 2-digit numbers) are far above Primary 1 and are copied unchanged into the P2/P3/P4 guides.
- **Activity-number gaps:** the guide jumps from Activity 17 (odd/even) to Activity 23 (clapping evens/odds) with no mention of Activities 18–22; and from Activity 31 to Activity 34 with no mention of Activities 32–33. Those pupil's-book activities exist (counting/place-value work) but the guide gives no guidance for them.

### What it does NOT teach / does not provide (honest negatives)
- No answer key (see above) — the only one of the four guides in this band without worked answers.
- No assessment items, tests, or revision material; no schemes of work or lesson plans with timings (only activity notes).
- No coverage of 3-D shapes despite the Unit 3 overview promising them; no pattern-making guidance beyond shapes (the pupil's book Unit 3 is lines + shapes only).
- No guidance for pupil's-book Activities 18–22 and 32–33.

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 79 PDF pages present, in order, legible. PDF p. 4 is blank in the text layer (a full-page illustration/title verso).
- The Table of Contents lists "1.1 Sorting and Grouping" at guide p. 8, but the guide's own Unit 1 overview table (p. 6) describes Unit 1 only as "Numbers: Sorting and matching" — the contents and the overview use slightly different wording.

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 1 (PDF p. 6), introduction:** "This Primary 3 Mathematics teacher's guide will be used alongside the learner's book." Wrong grade — this is the Primary 1 guide. (The Primary 3 guide says "Primary 3" correctly; the Primary 1 guide's text was evidently copied from the Primary 3 file.)
2. **Printed p. 30 (PDF p. 35), Activity 34–35:** "Ascending order 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9 / Descending order 9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2 1" — the activity says "Write numbers 1 to 10"; 10 is missing from both lists.
3. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 56), 2.2 Mass:** "Which ones are bigger? Both the feather and the ball are bigger. … The marble is heavier than the feather, even though it is bigger." The marble is NOT bigger than the feather — the sentence as printed is muddled (the intended point is that the smaller marble is heavier than the bigger feather).
4. **Printed p. 10 (PDF p. 15), 1.1:** "Define to learners the meaning sorting and marching" — "marching" for "matching".
5. **Printed p. 38 (PDF p. 43), 1.7 ordinal table:** gives 421st and 5,111th as examples — correct, but far beyond Primary 1 scope (ordinals to 10th); harmless but a scope oddity.
6. The introduction's peer-teaching example ("one learner decides to multiply three-digit numbers by two-digit numbers", guide p. 4) is not Primary 1 content.

### Citation anchors (checked 2026-11-05 — see the check line at the top of this section)
1. Guide's Unit 1 learning outcomes: count/read/write numbers 0–99; ordinals 1st–10th — printed pp. 6–7.
2. Sorting defined: arranging in groups by colour, texture, size and shape — printed p. 10.
3. Matching defined: putting together objects by colour, shape, texture and size — printed p. 10.
4. Counting 1–99: ball toss, snap/clap/foot-tap, counting in 2s/5s/10s — printed pp. 18–19.

## Grade 2, student — Pupil's Book 2 (© 2018; 89 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 3)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 20 anchors — 20 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Pupil's Book 2 (cover: "Mathematics Pupil's Book 2", "PRIMARY 2")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Copyright line (p. 1):** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named anywhere. No publisher name is printed inside this file (the foreword thanks "Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya").
- **Foreword:** by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Hon. Minister of General Education and Instruction; approves the book for use in all schools except international schools "with effect from 4th February, 2019". Thanks UNICEF South Sudan (managing the Global Partnership for Education-funded project), the Curriculum Foundation (UK), the British Council and DfID.
- **File:** `g02-student.pdf` — 89 PDF pages, 5,820,629 bytes. Printed pagination: front matter unnumbered (cover, foreword, contents), then printed pp. 1–86. **Printed page = PDF page − 3** throughout (PDF p. 4 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 89 = printed p. 86). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout. Currency is the South Sudanese Pound (SSP) in word problems, but Activity 1 (printed p. 1) reuses Kenyan retail imagery (Ariel soap "Buy Ariel 500gms @ 130/-", "SAVE 30/-", a chicken-shop menu priced 350/550/1300/1600) — prices in Kenyan-shilling style, not SSP.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean, complete: only 2 of 89 pages have near-zero text, both picture pages). Unit titles are set as graphics and are absent from the text layer; I confirmed them by rendering the three unit-opener pages and reading them visually: Unit 1 NUMBERS, Unit 2 MEASUREMENT, Unit 3 GEOMETRY. Picture-only activities (clock faces, money pictures, shape-counting, pattern pictures) were read from the text layer's captions and the teacher's guide's keys where they exist; the actual pictured numbers/clock times in those activities could not be verified from text alone and are flagged as picture-dependent. No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 1–40)
- **1.1 Reading 3-digit numbers (pp. 1–3).** Reading numbers from shop pictures and number lists; reading number names (twelve, thirteen … ninety-seven). Numbers up to 3 digits.
- **1.2 Writing 3-digit numbers (pp. 4–5).** Writing numbers in words ("Two hundred and fifty-four") and numerals; matching number names to symbols (one hundred ↔ 100 … nine hundred ↔ 900).
- **1.3 Ordering numbers (pp. 6–8).** Ordering 3-digit numbers smallest→largest and largest→smallest; missing numbers in sequences (including counting in 50s: 300, 350, 400…); smallest/greatest in a set; odd/even identification from a number table (p. 8).
- **1.4 Addition & subtraction by using a number line (pp. 8–11).** Adding/subtracting by counting steps right/left on a number line, first 1-digit then 2- and 3-digit numbers (e.g. 120 + 4, 142 − 7, 310 − 8).
- **1.5 Place value (pp. 12–15).** Hundreds/tens/ones with an abacus and bottle tops; place value of 734 worked out (7 hundreds = 700, 3 tens = 30, 4 ones = 4); tables of place values; place value of a named digit (incl. digit 5 in ten numbers).
- **1.6 Addition of numbers up to 3 digits (pp. 16–19).** Column addition without carrying (13 + 24) and with carrying (26 + 37 = 63; 384 + 208 = 592); practice sums; 6 word problems (mangoes, litres, SSP, exercise books, pupils).
- **1.7 Subtraction of numbers up to 3 digits without borrowing (p. 20).** 12 bare subtractions and 8 word problems. Borrowing is explicitly NOT taught here.
- **1.8 Rounding off (pp. 21–22).** A 0–100 rounding chart; rounding to the nearest ten (324 → 320; 236 → 240) and nearest hundred (361 → 400; 532 → 500); practice to nearest tens and hundreds.
- **1.9 Multiplication (pp. 23–28).** Multiplication as repeated addition / groups of objects (4 × 3 = 12 from 4 groups of 3 sticks); a full 1–10 multiplication table to fill in (some products pre-filled: 3 × 3 = 9, 5 × 5 = 25, 8 × 10 = 80, 9 × 5 = 45, 10 × 10 = 100); multiplication facts drills; 12 word problems (mangoes, desks, cabbages, mats, dogs' legs, car wheels, meals per week, pens, books, birds' legs, father's age, holiday days).
- **1.10 Division (pp. 29–35).** Division as equal sharing (15 pineapples among 3 people = 5 each; 15 ÷ 3); division sentences from sharing stories; division facts to 50 ÷ 10; long-division layout drills; a division game/table; 8 word problems (oranges, pencils, notebooks at SSP 80 for 4, sweets).
- **1.11 Fractions (pp. 36–40).** A fraction as part of a whole: halves, thirds, quarters from sharing a loaf; folding paper into halves/quarters/thirds; 1/2 + 1/2 = 1; 2/4, 3/4; colouring shapes to show a given fraction (including 9/15, 2/4, 3/4).

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT — "Length, mass and weight" (printed pp. 41–70)
- **2.1 Length (pp. 41–46).** Measuring with sticks, string, metre rules and tape measures; measuring classroom/door/chair distances; route-distance word problems from a school map (dorm/clinic/office; home–school–river); adding lengths in metres (426 m + 51 m etc.).
- **2.2 Mass (pp. 47–50).** Heavier/lighter/same comparisons against a 1 kg container of soil (stone, textbook, carton of books, shoes, ball, metal, cotton, eraser); "which is heavier, 2 kg maize or 4 kg beans?" etc. No balance-scale weighing in numbers; no grams/kilograms arithmetic beyond comparison.
- **2.3 Capacity (pp. 51–55).** Filling buckets with bottles/cups/calabashes; half-litres (how many half-litres in 2/3 litres; 8 half-litres = 4 litres); how many small containers fill big ones (1 L → 5 L, 2 L → 10 L … 6 L → 24 L); adding/subtracting litres and half-litres; word problems (milk, water pots, half-litre bottles).
- **2.4 Money (pp. 56–57).** South Sudanese banknotes front/back (pictures only — denominations not legible in the text layer).
- **2.5 Giving change (pp. 58–62).** Picture-based: "What do you see?", true/false, "How much?" (amounts picture-dependent); how many 1-pound notes in a 20-pound note / in four 10-pound notes; how many 50s and 5s in a 100-pound note.
- **2.6 Shop Price List (p. 63).** A priced list (trousers SSP 650, box 370, blanket 460, school bag 245, school shoes 815, dress 340, mattress 500) with 5 change problems.
- **2.7 Time (pp. 64–65).** Reading clock faces (o'clock times, picture-dependent); daily-routine times.
- **2.8 Half past the hour (p. 66).** "Half past 5" = 30 minutes past; make/draw clocks for half past 4/6/11/8/5.
- **2.9 Quarter past / quarter to the hour (pp. 67–70).** Quarter = 15 minutes (60 ÷ 4); minute hand at 3 (past) or 9 (to); clock-face drawing; daily-routine times; reading digital times (4:00, 3:30, 6:45, 7:00, 5:15, 9:45, 2:30, 12:00, 8:00, 1:30, 4:15, 2:30).

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 71–86)
- **3.1 Shapes (pp. 71–77).** Recognising rectangle, triangle, circle, square (and oval) in photos and the classroom; drawing and naming shapes; colouring rectangles red and squares blue; counting triangles/rectangles/ovals/circles/squares in a picture; making crafts from shapes.
- **3.2 Patterns (pp. 78–83).** Making patterns from cut-out circles, squares, ovals, triangles, rectangles; counting the shapes in a pattern; copying patterns and drawing the missing/next shape.
- **3.3 Properties of triangles (p. 84).** Triangle = 3 straight sides, 3 angles. Types: equilateral (3 equal sides, 3 equal angles, always 60°), isosceles (2 equal sides, 2 equal angles), scalene (no equal sides, no equal angles).
- **3.4 Properties of a square (p. 85).** Right-angle and equal-side marks on a diagram; learners infer properties from the diagram (no properties listed in words).
- **3.5 Properties of a rectangle (pp. 85–86).** Same diagram-led approach as 3.4; final activity: count squares, rectangles, triangles, circles in pictures (p. 86). The book ends at printed p. 86 with no revision, glossary or index.

### What it does NOT teach (honest negatives)
- No subtraction WITH borrowing (1.7 is explicitly "without borrowing"); no multiplication beyond 10 × 10; no division with remainders; no multiplication/division of 2-digit by 1-digit numbers.
- Fractions stop at halves, thirds, quarters (and 2/3, 3/4, 9/15 as colouring tasks): no adding/subtracting fractions, no equivalent fractions, no fraction of a quantity.
- No decimals, no percentages, no ratio, no area/perimeter/volume, no angles, no data/graphs.
- Money: no coin denominations are named or pictured in text; giving change is picture-led with no worked method (no counting-up method taught).
- Time: only o'clock, half past, quarter past/to; no minutes past/to (e.g. 10 past), no a.m./p.m., no 24-hour clock, no duration/elapsed-time problems.
- Mass: no weighing in grams/kilograms with numbers, no balance reading, no kg↔g conversion. Length: no cm/m conversion (that waits for Primary 3). Capacity: no millilitres.
- No 4-digit numbers (ceiling is 3 digits, to 999).

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 89 PDF pages present, in order, legible; contents page matches the body (all sections present at the listed pages). No missing or duplicated pages found.
- The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles (the words NUMBERS / MEASUREMENT / GEOMETRY appear only as graphics on the unit openers, not in the contents list or the text layer).
- Several activity items exist only as pictures with no text (clock faces pp. 64–65, money pp. 56–62, shape-counting pp. 75–77, pattern pictures pp. 78–83); a text-only reader cannot recover their numbers.
- The digit "1" is occasionally rendered as capital "I" in the text layer (font artefact, e.g. "I kg soil"); not a print defect.

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 17 (PDF p. 20), 1.6 worked example 384 + 208:** step 4 reads "Add 1+8+0=0" — it should be 9. (The final answer 592 is correct; only the step is wrong.) The teacher's guide repeats this slip verbatim.
2. **Printed p. 34 (PDF p. 37), 1.10 Activity 9:** the worked line reads "18 divide by 6 is 3" under the item "18÷3 =". The divisor has changed: 18 ÷ 3 = 6, not 3. (18 ÷ 6 = 3 is true, but it does not answer the item as set.) The teacher's guide repeats it.
3. **Printed p. 32 (PDF p. 35), 1.10 Activity 4 Q1:** "How many books will each pupil gate?" — "gate" for "get". (Teacher's guide repeats "kooks"/"gate".)
4. **Printed p. 27 (PDF p. 30), 1.9 Activity 5 Q4:** "A floor is covered with 4 marts in a row" — "marts" for "mats".
5. **Printed p. 50 (PDF p. 53), 2.2 Q5:** "2 kg maize floor?" — "floor" for "flour".
6. **Printed p. 53 (PDF p. 56), 2.3 Activity 3 diagram:** litre/half-litre bottle diagrams are labelled "1 kg" in places — kilogram labels inside a capacity (litres) diagram.
7. **Printed p. 66 (PDF p. 69), 2.8 Activity 3:** items are numbered 4–8 ("4. It is 4 o'clock … 8. It is 11 o'clock") instead of 1–5.
8. **Printed p. 70 (PDF p. 73), 2.9 Activity 3:** the time "2:30" appears twice in the list (items 7 and 12).
9. **Printed p. 21 (PDF p. 24), 1.8 rounding chart:** the chart's "Round up / Round up / Round down / Round down" labels sit under the chart with no clear referent; and the 236 example explains "Add one to the tens digit to get 1+3=4" (written backwards; the value 240 is right).
10. **Printed p. 28 (PDF p. 31), 1.9 Activity 6:** the multiplication grids print as "3   1 =" with the × sign missing (probably a font/layout gap; the teacher's guide reproduces the same gap).

## Grade 2, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 2 (© 2018; 68 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 4)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 18 anchors — 18 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Teacher's Guide 2 (cover: "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 2", "PRIMARY 2")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Copyright line (p. 1):** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named anywhere. No publisher name is printed inside this file (the foreword thanks "Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya").
- **Foreword:** by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Hon. Minister of General Education and Instruction; approves the guide for use in all schools except international schools "with effect from 4th February, 2019". Thanks UNICEF South Sudan (Global Partnership for Education funding), the Curriculum Foundation (UK), the British Council and DfID.
- **File:** `g02-teacher.pdf` — 68 PDF pages, 4,808,930 bytes. Printed pagination: printed pp. 1–64. **Printed page = PDF page − 4** throughout (PDF p. 5 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 68 = printed p. 64). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete). Unit titles in the guide are text (unlike the pupil's book, where they are graphics). The companion Pupil's Book 2 was read separately (see g02-student.md) — companion verdicts below rest on both books. Picture-only pupil's-book activities (clock faces, money pictures, shape counting) have guide answers that are themselves picture-dependent; I checked the guide's numeric keys against the pupil's book's text items wherever both exist. No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit
The guide has a 5-page introduction (printed pp. 1–5, identical in substance to the P1/P3/P4 guides: problem-based learning, open-ended questions with the same 160 cm rectangle example, group work, peer teaching, assessment by observation/conversation/product) then three units.

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (guide printed pp. 6–33)
- **Unit overview (pp. 6–7):** "Learn about" table, key inquiry questions, objectives and learning outcomes. Note the overview says learners will "read, write and order numbers up to three digits to 100" (garbled) and objective (a) says "Read and write numbers up to four digits" — both wrong for a 3-digit unit (see slips).
- **1.1 Reading 3-digit numbers (pp. 8–9).** Teaching notes (bundles of sticks, number cards); full key for pupil's Activity 4 (Twelve = 12 … Eighty-two = 82).
- **1.2 Writing 3-digit numbers (pp. 10–11).** Keys for Activities 1–3 (254, 341, 513, 671, 123, 466, 732, 912, 834; twenty-seven … eighty-five; two hundred and sixteen … seven hundred and forty-one). Activity 4 (matching names to symbols) is described but its key is not given — the matching itself is picture-dependent.
- **1.3 Ordering numbers (pp. 12–13).** Full key for Activity 1: orderings (2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 23 etc.), missing numbers (101, 104, 106; 211, 213, 214; 352, 353, 355; 445, 448, 449; 597, 599, 602), next five (974–978; 780–784; 642–646; 890–894; 450–650 in 50s), smallest→largest (4, 16, 23, 271, 427, 932), largest→smallest (871, 402, 316, 204, 112, 13, 9), smallest in each set (12; 130; 220; 147; 492), greater of each pair (900; 915; 500; 660; 250; 700). All checked correct against the pupil's book. The pupil's Activity 1 Q8 (the 12-number table: even/odd/less than 200/above 500) has NO key in the guide.
- **1.4 Addition & subtraction by number line (pp. 13–15).** Full keys: 3 + 2 = 5; 4 + 1 = 5; 2 + 3 = 5; 6 + 2 = 8; 5 + 2 = 7; 10 + 4 = 14; 21 + 3 = 24; 30 + 5 = 35; 52 + 6 = 58; 46 + 2 = 48; 120 + 4 = 124; 130 + 3 = 133; 145 + 5 = 150; 160 + 7 = 167; subtractions 9 − 3 = 6; 10 − 6 = 4; 7 − 2 = 5; 5 − 4 = 1; 20 − 5 = 15; 37 − 6 = 31; 90 − 4 = 86; 16 − 2 = 14; 40 − 3 = 37; 77 − 7 = 70; 51 − 5 = 46; 44 − 4 = 40; 142 − 7 = 135; 100 − 10 = 90; 115 − 5 = 110; 230 − 6 = 224; 310 − 8 = 302. All correct.
- **1.5 Place value (pp. 15–18).** Materials (number cards, locally made abacus); full keys for Activities 1–5, including the bold/underlined-digit answers (321 tens; 632 ones; 423 hundreds; 216 tens; 572 ones; 736 tens; 914 hundreds; Activity 5: 164 tens; 270 ones; 912 tens; 791 tens; 100 ones; 288 tens; 179 ones; 219 ones; 812 hundreds). All correct.
- **1.6 Addition up to 3 digits (pp. 18–20).** Repeats the pupil's worked examples (26 + 37 = 63; 384 + 208 = 592 — including the pupil's "1+8+0=0" slip, see below); keys for Activity 2 (241 + 12 = 253; 319 + 23 = 342; 472 + 42 = 514; 615 + 236 = 851; 927 + 26 = 953; 629 + 241 = 870; 328 + 207 = 535; 417 + 234 = 651; 632 + 194 = 826; 184 + 341 = 525; 376 + 293 = 669) and Activity 3 word problems (715 mangoes; 686 litres; 781; 471 books; 917 pupils; 635 boys and girls). All correct. Note: the guide calls the money "shillings" in Q3 ("Deng has 512 shillings") where the pupil's book says SSP 512.
- **1.7 Subtraction without borrowing (p. 21).** Full key: 74 − 42 = 32 … 972 − 341 = 631; word problems (46; 347; 312; 252; 314; 268; 150 chickens; 430 girls). All correct.
- **1.8 Rounding off (pp. 22–23).** Repeats the pupil's rounding chart; keys: nearest tens 314 → 310; 327 → 330; 476 → 480; 512 → 510; 638 → 640; 761 → 760; nearest hundreds 365 → 400; 413 → 400; 271 → 300; 738 → 700; 619 → 600; 534 → 500; 473 → 500; 657 → 700. All correct.
- **1.9 Multiplication (pp. 23–26).** Teaching notes; full 1–10 multiplication table as key; Activity 4 keys (1 × 5 … 10 × 10, all correct); Activity 5 word problems — only 7 of the pupil's 12 answered (car wheels 20; meals 21; pens 100; books SSP 60; bird legs 18; Dorothy's father 30; holidays 28 days). The seller-mangoes, desks, cabbages, mats and dogs'-legs problems have NO key. Activity 6 (multiplication grids) is reproduced without answers.
- **1.10 Division (pp. 27–31).** Teaching notes; Activity 3 keys (15 ÷ 5 = 3; 12 ÷ 6 = 2; 18 ÷ 3 = 6; 25 ÷ 5 = 5; 24 ÷ 4 = 6); Activity 5 keys (all 18 divisions correct); Activity 6 (no numeric key — group work); Activity 7 keys (6 ÷ 3 = 2; 9 ÷ 3 = 3; 8 ÷ 4 = 4 — the pupil's book sets "8 ÷ 4 =" blank and the guide keys it as 4, which is wrong: 8 ÷ 4 = 2 — see slips); Activity 8 keys (2 pens; 5 mangoes; 4 books; 5 brooms); Activity 9 repeats the pupil's "18 divide by 6 is 3" line; Activity 10 keys (5; 10 pencils; 4 pounds; SSP 20 per notebook; 4 oranges; 5 mangoes; 5 sweets; 5 pencils). Note Activity 10 Q1's key says "5 mangoes" for a question about oranges — see slips.
- **1.11 Fractions (pp. 31–33).** Repeats the pupil's loaf-sharing story (whole = 1; half = 1/2; third = 1/3; fourth = 1/4) and paper-folding (1/2 + 1/2 = 1; three quarters = 3/4); Activity 3 (colouring) has no key.

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (guide printed pp. 34–55)
- **Unit overview (pp. 34–35):** length/capacity/weight estimation, shopping role-play, telling time in hours/half past/quarter past.
- **2.1 Length (pp. 36–41).** Activity 5 route answers: Jane dorm→clinic→office 300 m; nurse office→dorm→clinic 342 m; John's height 1 m; farm perimeter 364 m (all consistent with the pupil's map distances 128 m / 86 m / 120 m / 62 m); Activity 6 sums (477; 233; 662; 613; 796; 124 m); Activity 7 (Janet home→Ali's home 1047 m; Ali school→river→home 626 m; Janet school and back 832 m). All correct.
- **2.2 Mass (pp. 41–42).** Guidance only; no numeric key (the pupil's comparisons are picture-led).
- **2.3 Capacity (pp. 43–47).** Activity 3: Q1 mis-copied as "in 3 litres" (duplicating Q2; the pupil's Q1 is "in 2 litres" — see slips); Activity 4 table completed (5, 5, 5, 5, 3, 4); Activity 5 keys — items numbered 5–18 instead of 1–14 (see slips) and item 14 is WRONG as a key: the guide prints "50 litres + 27 litres = 77 litres" for the pupil's subtraction item "50 litres − 27 litres" (correct: 23 litres). All other Activity 5 keys correct (100; 123; 29; 24; 114; 18 half-litres; 50; 71; 71; 67 litres; 4 litres left; 9-litre pot; 10 half-litre bottles).
- **2.4 Money (p. 47).** Guidance only ("Avail real money in class…").
- **2.5 Giving change (pp. 47–51).** Activity 1: "work out how many of the smaller denomination make SSP 100" (no key); Activity 2 true/false and Activity 3 "How much?" have picture-only keys (SSP 75, 26, 300; then 150, 130, 120 — not verifiable from text); Activity 4 keys (20 one-pound notes in a 20; 40 in four 10s; 2 fifties in 100; 20 fives in 100).
- **2.6 Shop Price List (p. 52).** Keys: Mary 450 − dress 340 = 110; 500 − box 370 = 130; Kambo 950 − shoes 815 = 135; box + blanket + shoes = 370 + 460 + 815 = 1645; Keru 2500 − (mattress 500 + box 370 + bag 245) = 1385. All correct.
- **2.7 Time (p. 53).** Activity 2 clock answers: 10, 2, 3, 5, 8, 7, 12, 11, 8 o'clock (two 8 o'clocks — see slips). Activity 3 renumbers the pupil's items 4–8 as 1–5 (4, 7, midday, 3, 11 o'clock).
- **2.8 Half past the hour (p. 54).** Activity 1 listed (half past 4/6/11/8/5), no key needed.
- **2.9 Quarter past/to (pp. 54–55).** Activity 1 listed; Activity 3 key: 4:00 four o'clock; 3:30 half past three; 6:45 quarter to seven; 7:00 seven o'clock; 5:15 quarter past five; 9:45 quarter to ten; 2:30 half past two; 12:00 noon; 8:00 eight o'clock; 1:30 half past one; 4:15 quarter past four; 2:30 half past two. All correct (note 12:00 called "noon").

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (guide printed pp. 56–64)
- **Unit overview (pp. 56–57):** shapes, patterns, tessellation, types/properties of triangles, rectangles and squares. Note: calls them "these three regular geometrical shapes" — a rectangle is not a regular shape (see slips).
- **3.1 Shapes (pp. 59–60).** Teaching notes only (draw shapes on the board, manila cut-outs, match names).
- **3.2 Patterns (p. 60).** Teaching notes only.
- **3.3–3.5 Properties of triangles / squares / rectangles (pp. 63–64).** Teaching notes only ("Explain to them the properties as in the pupil's book by practically showing them using the cut outs"). No property lists and no keys for the pupil's counting activities (Activities 5–6, shape counts) are given.

### Companion verdict — does it companion Pupil's Book 2?
**Yes — closely, and it is the strongest companion in this band.** Same 3 units in the same order, same sub-unit titles and numbering (1.1–1.11, 2.1–2.9, 3.1–3.5), same activity numbering throughout. It gives worked keys for most numeric exercises, and where I could check them against the pupil's book they agree (with the exceptions listed under slips). Gaps:
- No key for pupil's 1.2 Activity 4 (matching), 1.3 Activity 1 Q8 (number table), 1.9 Activity 5 Q1–5 and Activity 6, 1.11 Activity 3, 2.2 (mass), 2.4 (money), 2.5 Activities 1–3 (picture-dependent), 3.1–3.5 counting/pattern activities.
- The guide's contents page numbers are the GUIDE's own pages, not the pupil's-book pages (e.g. guide 1.6 addition at guide p. 18 vs pupil's p. 16; guide 1.10 division at guide p. 27 vs pupil's p. 29).
- The introduction promises answers "provided in this teachers guide" — for this guide the promise is largely kept for arithmetic, not for picture-led work.
- No unit in the guide is missing from the pupil's book and vice versa.

### What it does NOT teach / does not provide (honest negatives)
- No keys for picture-led work (money, clocks, shape counting, patterns) — the teacher must judge from the pictures.
- No tests, revision material, schemes of work or timings.
- No additional content beyond the pupil's book (no extension topics).

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 68 PDF pages present, in order, legible. The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles (the words NUMBERS / MEASUREMENT / GEOMETRY appear only as graphics in the pupil's book and are absent from the guide's contents list too).
- Guide p. 30 (printed 26) reproduces the pupil's multiplication grids with the × signs missing, without answers.

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 19 (PDF p. 23), 1.6:** repeats the pupil's slip verbatim — "Add 1+8+0=0" in the 384 + 208 worked example (should be 9; final answer 592 is right).
2. **Printed p. 29 (PDF p. 33), 1.10 Activity 7:** "8 ÷ 4 = 4" — wrong; 8 ÷ 4 = 2. The pupil's book sets the same item (printed p. 33) and the guide keys it as 4. (Neighbouring items are right: 6 ÷ 3 = 2; 9 ÷ 3 = 3; 12 ÷ 4 = 3; 24 ÷ 6 = 4; 21 ÷ 3 = 7; 12 ÷ 3 = 4; 18 ÷ 3 = 6; 8 ÷ 2 = 4; 10 ÷ 5 = 5.)
3. **Printed p. 30 (PDF p. 34), 1.10 Activity 9:** repeats the pupil's "18 divide by 6 is 3" under the item "18 ÷ 3 =" (18 ÷ 3 = 6).
4. **Printed p. 30 (PDF p. 34), 1.10 Activity 10 Q1:** "A mother shared 20 oranges equally among her 4 children. How many did each get? 5 mangoes" — "mangoes" for "oranges".
5. **Printed p. 47 (PDF p. 51), 2.3 Activity 5 Q14 (guide numbering):** the key prints "50 litres + 27 litres = 77 litres" — it has silently changed the pupil's subtraction item (pupil's Activity 5 Q10: "50 litres − 27 litres") into an addition. As an answer to the pupil's item it is wrong (correct: 23 litres). [corrected 2026-11-05: the filing had misquoted the guide as printing "−"]
6. **Printed p. 45 (PDF p. 49), 2.3 Activity 3:** Q1 is printed as "How many half litres are there in 3 litres" — a duplicate of Q2; the pupil's book Q1 is "in 2 litres".
7. **Printed p. 47 (PDF p. 51), 2.3 Activity 5:** items are numbered 5–18 instead of 1–14.
8. **Printed p. 28 (PDF p. 32), 1.10 Activity 4:** "How many kooks will each pupil gate?" — repeats the pupil's "kooks"/"gate" typos.
9. **Printed p. 53 (PDF p. 57), 2.7 Activity 2:** two of the nine clock answers are "8 o'clock" (items 5 and 9) — one of the two pictured clocks is presumably a different hour; not verifiable from text, flagged as a likely misread.
10. **Printed p. 20 (PDF p. 24), 1.6 Activity 3 Q3:** "Deng has 512 shillings" — the pupil's book says SSP 512 (pounds); the guide changes the currency word to shillings.
11. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 55), 2.5 Activity 4 Q1b:** "Four 10 pound coins = 40" — the pupil's book asks about "four 10 pound notes"; the guide writes "coins". (The value 40 is right.)
12. **Printed p. 6 (PDF p. 10), Unit 1 overview:** "read, write and order numbers up to three digits to 100" (garbled) and objective (a) "Read and write numbers up to four digits" — the unit teaches 3-digit numbers only.
13. **Printed p. 56 (PDF p. 60), Unit 3 overview:** "differentiate these three regular geometrical shapes" (triangle, rectangle, square) — a rectangle is not a regular shape.
