## Grade 3, student — Pupil's Book 3 (no © line extractable; 82 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 5)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 20 passed, 1 corrected, 1 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Pupil's Book 3 (cover: "Mathematics Pupil's Book 3", "PRIMARY 3")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Imprint (PDF p. 1, inside cover):** "MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS" named as publisher; "Primary Mathematics has been written and developed by Ministry of General Education and Instruction, Government of South Sudan in conjunction with Subjects experts." Funded by the Global Partnership for Education (logo). "This Book is the Property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. This Book is not for sale." No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named. No © line is extractable from this file's text layer (unlike Pupil's Books 2 and 4, which print "©2018…" on p. 1) — the © line, if present, sits in the cover artwork.
- **Front matter:** "How to take care of your books" (do's and don'ts, PDF p. 2); title page (PDF p. 3); contents (PDF p. 5). There is NO ministerial foreword in this file (Books 2 and 4 both carry one).
- **File:** `g03-student.pdf` — 82 PDF pages, 3,659,008 bytes. Printed pagination: printed pp. 1–77. **Printed page = PDF page − 5** throughout (PDF p. 6 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 82 = printed p. 77). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete for all text pages). Unit titles are set as graphics and are absent from the text layer; I confirmed them by rendering the five unit-opener pages and reading them visually: Unit 1 NUMBERS, Unit 2 MEASUREMENT, Unit 3 GEOMETRY, Unit 4 ALGEBRA, Unit 5 STATISTICS. Picture-only activities (number chart, fraction shading diagrams, clock/shapes/pictograph pictures) were read from captions and the teacher's guide's keys where they exist; pictured values that exist only as images are flagged picture-dependent. The digit "1" is frequently rendered as capital "I" in the text layer (e.g. "432I", "3I79") — a font artefact, not a print error; I treat those as 1s. No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 1–21)
- **1.1 Reading numbers (pp. 1–3).** Reading numbers from a supermarket poster picture (Kenyan retail imagery: Blue Band, Weetabix, Colgate, prices "20.00", "194.00"); counting to 100 forwards/backwards from a number chart (picture); mental maths: 10/100 less and more (430, 128, 904, 327, 999, 254, 929); number names to 4 digits (e.g. 2456 = "two thousand, four hundred and fifty-six"; 7121).
- **1.2 Writing numbers (pp. 3–5).** Writing numbers in words and in numerals to 4 digits (including 1060, 3610, 4001, 4100, 1040; "One thousand, one hundred and sixty-six" etc.); ordering a set of numbers highest/lowest.
- **1.3 Place value (pp. 6–8).** Place-value game with digit cards; place-value table to thousands (8901, 154, 236, 1873); expanded form (319 = 300 + 10 + 9); building numbers from "4 hundreds 6 tens 3 ones" etc. (Activities 9–11; Activity 10 items d/e are garbled in the text layer — see slips — but the teacher's guide gives the intended items).
- **1.4 Comparing numbers (pp. 9–12).** Ascending/descending order defined; worked ordering by hundreds then tens (548/351/411/269; 374/356/329/381); ordering sets to 4 digits; place value of digit 7 in ten numbers (Activity 17); two worked additions in columns (4321 + 3178 = 7499; 5114 + 2798 = 7912).
- **1.5 Addition & subtraction (pp. 12–14).** (Contents title misspelt "Addition & Substation".) Bare addition/subtraction to 4 digits (26 + 42 … 6981 − 2221; 4794 − 794); word problems with 3 addends (cows, books, mango trees, pupils, vehicles). Activity 19 Q4 is worded "If 275 schools joined the two schools" (should be pupils — see slips).
- **1.6 Divisibility test for 2, 5 and 10 (pp. 14–16).** Rule for 2 (ends 0/2/4/6/8), for 5 (ends 5/0), for 10 (ends 0; "even and divisible by 5"); divisor vs factor explained (8 ÷ 2 = 4; 10 ÷ 3 = 3 rem 1).
- **1.7 Comparing simple equivalent fractions (pp. 16–21).** Halves from folding/cutting paper and fruit; unit fractions 1/2, 1/4, 1/6, 1/8, 1/10 as strips; equivalent fractions defined ("equal value": 1/2 = 2/4 = 4/8 etc.); numerator/denominator named (3 = numerator, 5 = denominator); matching shaded pictures to equivalent fractions (Activities 28–29, picture-dependent).

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 22–47)
- **2.1 Estimate and measure (p. 22).** Estimation defined ("a rough idea that we make without measuring"); estimating arm length, height, desk, classroom, car, playground in metres.
- **2.2 Introducing centimetres and metres (pp. 22–25).** 1 cm as a line AB; measuring lines with a 15 cm ruler to the nearest cm; drawing lines of given lengths (3–15 cm); measuring classroom objects; strides for hall/field/classroom.
- **2.3 Measuring lengths and distances in metres (pp. 25–26).** Estimating then measuring door, desk, blackboard, teacher's table.
- **2.4 Conversion of cm to m and m to cm (p. 27).** 1 m = 100 cm; 485 cm = 4 m 85 cm; 7 m 35 cm = 735 cm.
- **2.5 Addition and subtraction involving metres and centimetres (pp. 28–32).** Adding cm (60 + 60 = 1 m 20 cm); adding/subtracting m + cm with carrying/borrowing (2 m 36 cm + 1 m 36 cm = 3 m 72 cm; 7 m 54 cm − 5 m 63 cm); word problems (sticks, tables, ribbon, routes Anne/Ali/Mary, heights, fence, pipes).
- **2.6 Capacity (pp. 33–36).** Litre as standard unit; graduated cylinder made from a jar; 1 litre = 2 half-litres = 4 quarter-litres; adding/subtracting litres; conversion table ml/cl/dl/l (10 ml = 1 cl; 10 cl = 1 dl; 10 dl = 1 l; ml→l ÷1000; cl→l ÷100); converting 2000 ml, 4900 ml, 10000 ml to litres and 340 cl, 550 cl, 800 cl to litres.
- **2.7 Weight (pp. 37–40).** Kilogram as standard unit; gram for lighter objects; "the weight of 1 litre of water is 1 kilogram"; 1 kg = 1000 g; 1/2 kg = 500 g; 1/4 kg = 250 g; estimating weights (stones, textbooks, own weight); market visit; adding/subtracting kg (4 kg + 6 kg; 104 kg + 9 kg; 98 kg − 43 kg — the worked line is garbled, see slips); mixed litre questions under the weight heading (Activity 29).
- **2.8 Units of time (pp. 41–44).** 1 week = 7 days; 1 day = 24 hours; 1 hour = 60 minutes; 1 minute = 60 seconds; days in 3 weeks (21); weeks in 63 days (9); hotel/holiday duration problems; clock hands (long = minutes, short = hours, long thin = seconds); seconds in 10 minutes (600) / half an hour (1800); minutes in 3 hours (180); hours in 2 days (48); days in 5 weeks (35); weeks in 2 years (104); months in 1½ years (18); converting hours/minutes/seconds (Activity 34 — items worded as "1 hours 24 seconds" etc., see slips); a.m./p.m. (Activity 35); "time before 6 hours" / "time after 4 hours" (Activities 36–37).
- **2.9 Money (pp. 45–47).** Shop and market visits to record prices; money word problems in SSP (Willie SSP 8 + SSP 2 left; Bonnie SSP 1600 − 400; Carlos 700 vs Karen 600; Thomas 1700 vs Sandra 1300; Donna 1300 vs Jack 200; Susan 6700 vs Joshua 300; Arthur 9000 + 700 left; Linda 5500 + 900 left).

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 48–60)
- **3.1 Properties of geometric shapes (pp. 48–60).** Recap of rectangle, square, triangle, oval, circle from Primary 2; drawing a 5 cm × 5 cm square step by step; drawing a 6 cm × 3 cm rectangle; drawing an equilateral triangle 3 cm × 3 cm × 3 cm (60° angles); drawing circles with round objects; counting shapes in a diagram; "guess my shape" game; fact book of shape properties; lines of symmetry by paper folding: square 4, rectangle 2, equilateral triangle 3, isosceles 1, right-angled 0, scalene 0, circle — all diameters.

#### UNIT 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 61–63)
- **4.1 Inequalities (pp. 61–63).** Inequality defined; symbols > < ≥ ≤ with words and examples (x > 2, x < 4, x ≥ 10, x ≤ 3); 50 > 15, 15 < 50; filling in the correct sign (3 __ 2 … 24 __ 17); true/false with sums (4 > 2 + 1; 2 + 3 < 4; 3 + 5 < 6; 1 + 3 > 2; 6 > 5 + 4); comparing sums (2 + 2 < 3 + 3 etc.); which number is greater (100 vs 22; 569 vs 920; 17 vs 77; 718 vs 19; 28 vs 16; 1000 vs 918).

#### UNIT 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 64–77)
- **5.1 Pictographs (pp. 64–70).** Pictorial representation defined; attendance table (Mon 20, Tue 30, Wed 10, Thu 30, Fri 10) as a pictograph with one picture = 10 learners; loaves baked in 6 days; mangoes sold in a week (one picture = 10 mangoes); shirts sold in a week (one picture = 5 shirts); boys/girls pictograph (10 boys / 10 girls per picture); drawing a pictograph for houses built in Juba 2013–2017 (160, 330, 550, 720, 890); class sports survey.
- **5.2 Block graphs (pp. 70–77).** Pictograph → block graph (loaves data); pupils per class graph; countries-to-visit votes (Uganda 6, Rwanda 8, Kenya ?, Tanzania 12, Ethiopia 10, Egypt 16, Nigeria ?); favourite subjects block graph (scale 0–55); classroom-object survey; favourite-colour survey ("block paragraph" — typo, see slips); pets survey (cat 8, dog 14, goat 12, rabbit 4, sheep 18). The book ends at printed p. 77 with no revision, glossary or index.

### What it does NOT teach (honest negatives)
- No multiplication or division at all in this book — despite the teacher's guide introduction calling Unit 1 "Numbers: multiplication, division and fractions". No times tables, no long multiplication/division.
- No decimals (except decimal litres 7.2 L / 9.4 L in a conversion table, without teaching decimals); no percentages, no ratio.
- No area, perimeter or volume; no angles (beyond 60° in the equilateral-triangle drawing and right angles in squares/rectangles); no coordinates.
- Fractions stop at comparing simple equivalent fractions: no addition/subtraction of fractions, no fractions of quantities, no mixed numbers.
- Time: no 24-hour clock, no elapsed-time word problems beyond "before/after N hours", no reading minutes past the hour beyond whole/half/quarter (that was Primary 2).
- Money: no banknote/coin identification in this book (that was Primary 2); no giving-change method; the money section is word problems only.
- Statistics: pictographs and block graphs only — no bar charts with axes labelled by the learner beyond the given examples, no tally charts, no mode/mean/median.

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 82 PDF pages present, in order, legible. PDF p. 4 is blank in the text layer (a full-page illustration/title verso — no text to extract); it is not a missing page.
- The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles (NUMBERS / MEASUREMENT / GEOMETRY / ALGEBRA / STATISTICS appear only as graphics on unit openers). Contents lists 1.5 as "Addition & Substation".
- The digit 1 is rendered as capital I throughout the text layer (font artefact).
- Fraction diagrams in Activities 28–29 (pp. 18–21) are pictures with no text equivalents; their fractions can only be read from the images (the teacher's guide supplies them: 2/7 = 6/21, 3/4 = 6/8, 1/2 = 3/6, 2/4 = 7/14, 2/3 = 4/6, 6/7 = 18/21, 3/4 = 6/8).

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 17 (PDF p. 22), 1.7 Activity 27:** "Since fractions 1/2, 1/4, 3/6, 4/8, 5/10 all indicate the same part of the rectangle" — 1/4 does NOT equal 1/2. (3/6, 4/8, 5/10 do; 1/4 does not.) The teacher's guide repeats the claim ("it is half of the whole").
2. **Printed p. 29 (PDF p. 34), 2.5 Example 3:** "Add 4 m 76 cm and 3 m 34 cm" but the column shows "4 m 76 cm + 3 m 24 cm = 8 m 10 cm". The second addend changes from 34 cm to 24 cm mid-example. (76 + 34 = 110 cm → 8 m 10 cm is right for 34; the printed column with 24 is inconsistent.)
3. **Printed p. 31 (PDF p. 36), 2.5 Example 2:** "Subtract 5 m 63 cm from 7 m 54 cm" is given as "1 m 92 cm". The correct answer is 1 m 91 cm (6 m 154 cm − 5 m 63 cm = 1 m 91 cm). The method note (borrow 1 m = 100 cm) is right; the final number is wrong.
4. **Printed p. 38 (PDF p. 43), 2.7:** "There are 100 grams in 1 kilogram" printed on the same page as the correct "1 kg = 1000 g". Direct contradiction; 100 is wrong. The teacher's guide repeats both lines.
5. **Printed p. 16 (PDF p. 21), 1.6:** "10 ÷ 3 = 3 rem 1" is followed by "2 is a factor or divisor of 8 and 3 is the divisor of 10" — muddled: it presents 3 as a factor-like divisor of 10 immediately after showing 10 ÷ 3 leaves a remainder.
6. **Printed p. 13 (PDF p. 18), 1.5 Activity 19 Q4:** "If 275 schools joined the two schools how many pupils are there" — "schools" should be "pupils".
7. **Printed p. 40 (PDF p. 45), 2.7 worked examples:** the line "104 kg + 9 kg = 98 kg – 43 kg" jumbles two separate examples (104 + 9 = 113; 98 − 43 = 55) into one nonsensical equation.
8. **Printed p. 40 (PDF p. 45), 2.7 Activity 29:** litre questions ("105 litres – 45 litres") sit inside the weight (kilograms) section.
9. **Printed p. 43 (PDF p. 48), 2.8 Activity 34:** items are worded "1 hours 24 seconds", "2 hours 12 seconds", "15 hours 19 seconds" for conversion into seconds — the intended unit was almost certainly minutes (the guide's key treats items 2–6 as minutes + seconds, e.g. 20 min 45 s = 1245 s).
10. **Printed p. 44 (PDF p. 49), 2.8 Activity 35 Q3:** "2:20 midnight" — 2:20 is not midnight (the guide's key gives 2:20 a.m.).
11. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 56), 3.1 rectangle steps:** step 4 says "Join the points c and d to make the square complete" inside the rectangle activity (should say rectangle); step 6 says "A rectangle has 2 of its sides equal" (should be two pairs of opposite sides equal).
12. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 56), 3.1 Activity 8 (guess my shape):** "it has 43 right angles" — should be "4 right angles".
13. **Printed p. 52 (PDF p. 57) and pp. 58–59:** "two limes of symmetry" / "3 limes of symmetry" — "limes" for "lines" (also in the teacher's guide).
14. **Printed p. 76 (PDF p. 81), 5.2 Activity 12:** "Display your findings in a block paragraph" — should be "block graph".
15. **Printed p. 5 (contents) and p. 12:** "Addition & Substation" for "Subtraction"; p. 7: "We start nding place values" (missing "fi" in "finding"); p. 34: "paste a strip of pare" ("paper"); p. 38: "For smaller or litres objects" ("lighter"); p. 8 Activity 10 items d/e are garbled in print ("2 hundreds 2 hundreds 2 tens 2 hundreds") — the guide resolves them as "2 hundreds 2 tens 2 ones = 222" and "1 thousands 4 hundreds 3 tens 1 ones = 1431".

## Grade 3, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 3 (no © line extractable; 77 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 6)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 18 anchors — 17 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Teacher's Guide 3 (cover: "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 3", "PRIMARY 3")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Imprint (PDF p. 1, inside cover):** "MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS" named as publisher; "Primary Mathematics has been written and developed by Ministry of General Education and Instruction, Government of South Sudan in conjunction with Subjects experts." "This Book is the Property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. This Book is not for sale." Funded by the Global Partnership for Education (logo). No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named. No © line is extractable from this file's text layer (the © line, if present, sits in the cover artwork).
- **Front matter:** "How to take care of your books" (PDF p. 2); title page (PDF p. 3); contents (PDF pp. 5–6). No ministerial foreword in this file.
- **File:** `g03-teacher.pdf` — 77 PDF pages, 7,869,223 bytes. Printed pagination: printed pp. 1–71. **Printed page = PDF page − 6** throughout (PDF p. 7 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 77 = printed p. 71). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete). The companion Pupil's Book 3 was read separately (see g03-student.md) — companion verdicts below rest on both books. I checked the guide's numeric keys against the pupil's book's items wherever both exist. The digit "1" is frequently rendered as capital "I" in the text layer (font artefact). No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit
The guide has a 5-page introduction (printed pp. 1–5, identical in substance to the P1/P2/P4 guides: problem-based learning, open-ended questions with the same 160 cm rectangle example, group work, peer teaching, assessment by observation/conversation/product) then five units.

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (guide printed pp. 6–25)
- **Unit overview (pp. 6–7):** "Learn about" table, key inquiry questions, learning outcomes (read/write/compare numbers to 4 digits; subtract with/without borrowing; divisibility test for 2/5/10; compare simple equivalent fractions). **The introduction's unit table (p. 1) calls Unit 1 "Numbers: multiplication, division and fractions" — but neither the pupil's book nor this guide teaches any multiplication or division in Unit 1** (see slips).
- **1.1 Reading numbers (pp. 7–10).** Activities 1–3 (picture numbers; counting forwards/backwards on the number chart); Activity 3 mental-maths key (10/100 less/more for 430, 128, 904, 327, 999, 254, 929 — all correct, e.g. 999 → 989/1009/899/1099).
- **1.2 Writing numbers (pp. 11–13).** Keys for Activities 4–7 (203, 639, 704, 444, 898, 1300, 1202; "Three" … "Three thousand six hundred and ten"; 1166, 2449, 795, 368, 9502, 6315, 4150, 656, 7874, 5600; number names for 892 … 2018). All correct.
- **1.3 Place value (pp. 13–15).** Activity 8 (digit-card game); Activity 9 key (234 = 2 hundreds, 3 tens, 4 ones; 1236; 3468; 705); Activity 10 key — this RESOLVES the pupil's garbled items d/e: d) "2 hundreds 2 tens 2 ones = 222", e) "1 thousands 4 hundreds 3 tens 1 ones = 1431", f) "2 thousands 2 hundreds 2 tens 0 ones = 2220", g) 62, h) 932, i) 5432; Activity 11 expanded-form key (319 = 300 + 10 + 9 … 4973 = 4000 + 900 + 70 + 3). Activity 10 (a) 463 and Activity 11 all correct.
- **1.4 Comparing numbers (pp. 15–19).** Activities 12–14 (counters, ordering); Activity 15 ascending key — CONTAINS ERRORS (see slips): (d) lists "4" instead of "14"; (e) duplicates "31" and lists 8 numbers for 7 given; (g) "432" instead of "532"; (h) "7306" instead of "7303". Activity 16 descending key — CONTAINS ERRORS: (a) omits 7 (20, 15, 10, 9, 6, 4 — should include 7); (e) omits 123; (h) "521" instead of "512". Activity 17 place-value-of-7 key (a) Hundreds; (b) Ones; (c) Thousands; (d) Hundreds; (e) Tens; (f) Thousands; (g) "One" [typo for Ones]; (h) Thousands; (i) Hundreds; (j) Hundreds) — all correct values.
- **1.5 Addition & subtraction (pp. 20–21).** (Contents title misspelt "Addition & Substation", as in the pupil's book.) Activity 18 key: 26 + 42 = 68; 98 + 41 = 139; 152 + 61 = 213; 36 − 18 = 18; 108 − 72 = 36; 120 + 32 = 152; 244 − 128 = 116; 3411 + 892 = 4303; 2443 + 722 = 3165; 6981 − 2221 = 4760; 4794 − 794 = 4000. All correct. Activities 19–21 (word problems; write your own problem) have no numeric key — guidance only.
- **1.6 Divisibility test for 2, 5 and 10 (pp. 21–22).** Teaching notes only; Activities 22–24 have no key.
- **1.7 Comparing simple equivalent fractions (pp. 22–25).** Activity 25–27 (folding/cutting; "the said fractions represent the same part of the total… half of the whole" — repeats the pupil's error of including 1/4 among the equivalents of 1/2, see slips); Activity 28 key (picture fractions): 2/7 = 6/21; 3/4 = 6/8; 1/2 = 3/6; 2/4 = 7/14; 2/3 = 4/6; 6/7 = 18/21; 3/4 = 6/8. Activity 29 key: "1) 2/5 = 6/16" — WRONG (2/5 = 6/15); "2) 2/6 = 4/12" correct.

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (guide printed pp. 26–44)
- **Unit overview (pp. 26–27):** length/capacity/weight, time conversion, money. Note the overview says learners will "apply their knowledge of addition, subtraction, multiplication and division to length, capacity and weight" — multiplication/division are not used in this unit.
- **2.1–2.5 Length (pp. 28–34).** Activities 1–16: estimation, 15 cm rulers, strides, converting cm↔m, adding/subtracting m + cm. No numeric keys are printed for Activities 9–16 (the guide says "Check for correct answers" throughout without giving them). The pupil's worked errors (Example 3's 34/24 cm addend change; Example 2's 1 m 92 cm) are NOT corrected or flagged in the guide.
- **2.6 Capacity (pp. 35–37).** Activity 19 repeats the pupil's "paste a strip of pare" typo; Activity 20 key: 5, 9, 23, 30, 33 litres; missing addends 13, 20, 50, 14 litres; Irene's paraffin 6 − 2 − 3 = 1 litre left. Activity 21 key: 2, 5, 3, 16, 10, 13, 17, 2 litres. All correct.
- **2.7 Weight (pp. 37–39).** Repeats the pupil's text verbatim INCLUDING the error "There are 100 grams in 1 kilogram" on the same page as "1 kg = 1000 g", and "For smaller or litres objects". Activity 29 key: 105 − 45 = 60 litres; 75 + 35 − 60 = 50 litres; Kim's petrol 7 + 8 = 15 litres; Mary's milk 5 half-litres minus one = 2 litres. All correct.
- **2.8 Units of time (pp. 39–43).** Activity 30 key (2 weeks 3 days + 3 weeks = 5 weeks 3 days; holiday 12 + 10 + 3 = 25 days = 3 weeks 4 days; 10:00–11:30 = 1 h 30 min) — numbered 1, 3, 4 (item 2 skipped, see slips). Activity 31 key: minutes/hours/seconds; 600 s in 10 min; 1800 s in half an hour; 180 min in 3 h; 48 h in 2 days; 35 days in 5 weeks; 104 weeks in 2 years; 18 months in 1½ years. All correct. Activity 34 key: item 1 "13624 seconds" — WRONG (for "1 hours 24 seconds" the value should be 3624; 13624 has an extra leading 1); items 2–6 correct (1245; 1108; 7212; 2177; 54019 s). Activity 35 key: 7:35 a.m.; 6:45 p.m.; 2:20 a.m. (resolving the pupil's "2:20 midnight"); 4:35 p.m.; 8:48 a.m.; 12:50 p.m.; 5:05 p.m.; 11:27 p.m.; 8:36 a.m.; 9:17 p.m. Activity 36 key: 12 answers for the pupil's 6 items (items 1–6 correct: 9:20 p.m.; 11:45 a.m.; 3:15 a.m.; 6 p.m.; 6 a.m.; 8:15 a.m.; items 7–12 are phantom). Activity 37 key: 12 answers for 6 items; items 2 and 3 WRONG on a.m./p.m. (8:30 p.m. + 4 h = 12:30 a.m., guide says 12:30 p.m.; 1:25 a.m. + 4 h = 5:25 a.m., guide says 5:25 p.m.); items 1, 4, 5, 6 correct; items 7–12 phantom.
- **2.9 Money (pp. 43–44).** Activity 40 items reprinted WITHOUT answers (Willie, Bonnie, Carlos, Thomas, Donna, Susan, Arthur, Linda) — no key given.

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (guide printed pp. 45–56)
- **Unit overview (p. 45):** drawing shapes accurately, properties, shapes in daily life.
- **3.1 Properties of geometric shapes (pp. 45–56).** Repeats the pupil's text verbatim, including: "Label then line ab" (typo), "Join the points c and d to make the square complete" (in the rectangle activity), "A rectangle has 2 of its sides equal", "two limes of symmetry" / "3 limes of symmetry", and the CIRCLES properties headed "Properties of a rectangle". Activity 6 (count shapes) has no counts given; Activity 7 (Exercise 7) ADDS an item the pupil's book does not have — "a. A circle of radius 4cm" — and re-letters the rest (rectangle 4 × 7, square 5 cm, triangle 4-5-3). Activity 8 gives square/rectangle/circle properties (square: 4 equal sides, 4 corners, 4 right angles, 4 lines of symmetry; rectangle: opposite sides equal, 2 lines of symmetry; circle: circumference, diameter, radius, all diameters lines of symmetry).

#### UNIT 4: ALGEBRA (guide printed pp. 57–59)
- **Unit overview (p. 57):** inequalities, symbols >, <, ≠, comparing values.
- **4.1 Inequalities (pp. 58–59).** Activity 2 key: 15 items (a–o) for the pupil's 10 items (a–j) — 5 phantom items (k–o: 18 < 20; 30 < 100; 21 < 22; 14 < 19; 13 > 9); the 10 shared items are all correct (3 > 2; 4 < 7; 30 < 40; 60 < 110; 72 > 40; 30 = 30; 45 > 20; 61 < 72; 31 > 20; 24 > 17). Activity 3 true/false key: True, False, False, True, False — correct. Activity 4 key: True, True, False, True — correct.

#### UNIT 5: STATISTICS (guide printed pp. 60–71)
- **Unit overview (p. 60):** pictographs and block graphs.
- **5.1 Pictographs (pp. 61–65).** Activities 1–6 reprinted with "check for correct answers" but NO numeric keys (loaves per day, mangoes per day, shirts per day, boys/girls counts, Juba houses pictograph, sports survey).
- **5.2 Block graphs (pp. 66–71).** Activities 7–13 reprinted with guidance only; no numeric keys (loaves block graph; pupils per class; countries votes; favourite subjects; classroom objects; favourite colour; pets). The guide reproduces the loaves block graph (0–4.5, Mon–Sat) immediately above the pupils graph (0–60, P1–P3) in Activity 8 — a duplicated/pasted graph image. The final pets activity is mislabelled "Activity 7" (it is the pupil's Activity 13), and the favourite-colour activity is labelled "Activity 13" (it is the pupil's Activity 12); the guide also repeats the pupil's "block paragraph" typo. The guide ends at printed p. 71 with no revision or index.

### Companion verdict — does it companion Pupil's Book 3?
**Yes in structure; weakly in keys.** Same 5 units in the same order, same sub-unit titles and numbering (1.1–1.7, 2.1–2.9, 3.1, 4.1, 5.1–5.2), same activity numbering. It gives worked keys for Unit 1 arithmetic (Activities 3–18, 28–29) and some Unit 2 keys (Activities 20–21, 29–37), but:
- It gives NO keys for most of Unit 2 (Activities 9–16 length work; Activity 40 money), NO keys for any Unit 5 pictograph/block-graph reading, and NO counts for the Unit 3 shape-counting activity.
- Its keys contain more errors than the pupil's book's own worked examples in places (Activity 15/16 ordering keys; Activity 29's 6/16; Activity 34's 13624; Activity 37's a.m./p.m. errors).
- It repeats the pupil's errors verbatim wherever it reprints pupil's text (1/4 among the 1/2 equivalents; "100 grams in 1 kilogram"; "2 of its sides equal"; "limes of symmetry"; "Properties of a rectangle" for circles; "block paragraph").
- The guide's contents page numbers are the GUIDE's own pages (e.g. guide 1.5 at guide p. 20 vs pupil's p. 12; guide 5.1 at guide p. 61 vs pupil's p. 64).
- No unit in the guide is missing from the pupil's book and vice versa.

### What it does NOT teach / does not provide (honest negatives)
- No keys for the majority of the book's exercises (length, money, all of statistics, shape counting).
- No tests, revision material, schemes of work or timings.
- No multiplication/division content despite the introduction naming them for Unit 1; no additional content beyond the pupil's book.

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 77 PDF pages present, in order, legible. PDF p. 4 is blank in the text layer (a full-page illustration/title verso).
- The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles and lists "1.5 Addition & Substation".
- Activity numbering confusion in the final pages: the pets survey is labelled "Activity 7" and the favourite-colour survey "Activity 13" (pupil's Activities 13 and 12 respectively).

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 17 (PDF p. 23), Activity 15 key (ascending):** (d) "= 7, 4, 21, 24, 39, 98" — "4" should be "14" (given set: 98, 14, 21, 39, 7, 24). (e) "= 2, 14, 25, 31, 31, 40, 61, 79" — "31" is duplicated and there are 8 numbers for 7 given (correct: 2, 14, 25, 31, 40, 61, 79). (g) "= 432, 903, 6114, 9001" — "432" should be "532". (h) "= 774, 894, 2001, 7306" — "7306" should be "7303".
2. **Printed p. 18 (PDF p. 24), Activity 16 key (descending):** (a) "= 20, 15, 10, 9, 6, 4" — 7 is omitted (given: 7, 4, 9, 10, 6, 15, 20). (e) "= 503, 315, 305, 132" — 123 is omitted (given: 305, 315, 503, 123, 132). (h) "= 6032, 5121, 3001, 751, 521" — "521" should be "512".
3. **Printed p. 25 (PDF p. 31), Activity 29 key:** "1) 2/5 = 6/16" — wrong; 2/5 = 6/15.
4. **Printed p. 41 (PDF p. 47), Activity 34 key:** "1. 13624 seconds" for "1 hours 24 seconds" — wrong; 3600 + 24 = 3624 seconds (the extra leading "1" is a typo).
5. **Printed p. 43 (PDF p. 49), Activity 37 key:** item 2 "12:30 p.m." — wrong; 8:30 p.m. + 4 hours = 12:30 a.m. Item 3 "5:25 p.m." — wrong; 1:25 a.m. + 4 hours = 5:25 a.m.
6. **Printed p. 23 (PDF p. 29), Activity 26–27:** repeats the pupil's claim that 1/2, 1/4, 3/6, 4/8, 5/10 "all indicate the same part" — 1/4 is not equivalent to 1/2.
7. **Printed p. 37 (PDF p. 43):** repeats the pupil's "There are 100 grams in 1 kilogram" alongside the correct "1 kg = 1000 g".
8. **Printed p. 1 (PDF p. 7), introduction unit table:** Unit 1 is called "Numbers: multiplication, division and fractions" — multiplication and division are not taught anywhere in Pupil's Book 3.
9. **Printed p. 59 (PDF p. 65), Activity 2 key:** 15 items (a–o) for a 10-item pupil's activity — items k–o have no pupil's-book counterpart.

## Grade 4, student — Pupil's Book 4 (© 2018; 88 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 3)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 21 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Pupil's Book 4 (cover: "Mathematics Pupil's Book 4", "PRIMARY 4")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Copyright line (p. 1):** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named anywhere. No publisher name is printed inside this file (the foreword thanks "Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya").
- **Foreword:** by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Hon. Minister of General Education and Instruction; approves the book for use in all schools except international schools "with effect from 4th February, 2019". Thanks UNICEF South Sudan (Global Partnership for Education funding), the Curriculum Foundation (UK), the British Council and DfID.
- **File:** `g04-student.pdf` — 88 PDF pages, 5,034,712 bytes. Printed pagination: contents on printed p. i, then printed pp. 1–85. **Printed page = PDF page − 3** throughout (PDF p. 4 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 88 = printed p. 85). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF". **Re-confirmed from its own cover and contents: this file really is Primary 4** (the English G4/G5 files were swapped in the library; the maths files are not — G4 cover, contents and Unit 1 all say Primary 4 / numbers to 5 digits, following Book 3's 4-digit work).
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete for all text pages). Unit titles are set as graphics and are absent from the text layer; I confirmed them by rendering the five unit-opener pages and reading them visually: Unit 1 NUMBERS, Unit 2 MEASUREMENT, Unit 3 GEOMETRY, Unit 4 ALGEBRA, Unit 5 STATISTICS. Picture-only exercises (area grids pp. 38–39, volume cubes pp. 45–46, shop price list p. 55, map p. 68, angle/protractor diagrams pp. 62–67, bar/line graphs pp. 81–82) were read from captions and the teacher's guide's keys where they exist; pictured values that exist only as images are flagged picture-dependent. No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 1–31)
- **1.1 Write, read, compare and order numbers up to 5 digits (pp. 1–8).** Place value to ten-thousands (3647/3467 in words — see slips; 23,456; 47,892); writing numbers in words and numerals; place value of each digit and of a named digit (incl. digit 9, digit 7, digit 0); comparing/ordering to 5 digits (4263 > 4193; 7826 < 9014; 1407 < 1423); forming numbers from digit cards; ordering South Sudan state populations (358,692; 782,504; 645,465) and trees planted 2014–2017; Juba vehicle census (9,798 cars; 9,643 pick-ups; 9,742 lorries; 9,160 buses).
- **1.2 Rounding off numbers to the nearest thousands (pp. 9–12).** Rule: keep the thousands digit, round up if the next digit is 5+ (7421 → 7000; 8697 → 9000; 36,178 → 36,000; 57,812 → 58,000; 96,574 → 97,000); rounding lists and add-then-round sums (25,812 + 404 etc.).
- **1.3 Multiples and factors of whole numbers (pp. 13–17).** Multiples by skip counting (first five multiples of 7: 7, 14, 21, 28, 35); factors as pairs (factors of 12: 1/12, 2/6, 3/4); multiplication by zero (any number × 0 = 0) and by one; multiplication by 10 (add a zero); ×2/×5/×10 puzzles; first 10 multiples of 2, 5, 10; full 1–9 multiplication tables; LCM-style word problems (buses every 12 and 8 minutes; tyre rotation 8,000 km vs oil 5,000 km); factors of 36/27/50/30; first ten multiples of 6/10/25/35.
- **1.4 Addition and subtraction of fractions (pp. 18–22).** Fraction = numerator over denominator; shaded parts (1/4); equivalent fractions (1/3 = 2/6 = 4/12; 1/2 = 2/4 = 4/8; dividing top and bottom by the same number, 6/12 = 3/6 = 1/2); adding same-denominator fractions (2/9 + 1/9 = 3/9); subtracting same-denominator fractions (3/7 − 2/7 = 1/7; 4/8 − 2/8 = 2/8 = 1/4; 5/9 − 2/9 = 3/9 = 1/3); completing equivalent fractions (8/12 = 2/? etc.).
- **1.5 Ratio (pp. 22–24).** Ratio as comparison (flour:water 3:1 for asida; boys:girls 2:3; shaded:unshaded 2:6); ratio written with ":", the word "to", or as a fraction; scaling ratios (4:5 = 8:10); ratio problems (gumboots 14 of 36 people; milk:water 2:3; textbooks 80:70; pens 4 blue of 12; leaves/stems from 32 pupils; brown:white squares).
- **1.6 Percentages (pp. 25–31).** Percent = part per hundred (1% = 1/100; 70% = 70 out of 100); 100-square diagrams (100%, 50%); percent ↔ decimal ↔ fraction table (25% = 0.25 = 1/4; 50% = 0.5 = 1/2; 75% = 3/4); percent → decimal (÷100) and decimal → percent (×100); 25% of 80 = 20; bad apples 15% of 500; shirt price drop 30% of SSP 1500; percentages of quantities (70% of 700 oranges; 15 of 45 books; 2 of 10 oranges bad; 50% of 200 mangoes); fractions as percentages (2/3, 1/8, 4/5, 3/4, 7/10); 12% of 350; 48/50 as a percent (96%); 24/30 attained, percent missed (20%); population growth 12% on 500,000 → 560,000; percentages as fractions (20% = 1/5; 15% = 3/20; 67% = 67/100); percentages as ratios (60% = 60:100 = 3:5) and ratios as percentages (1:4 = 25%; 2:5 = 40%; 1:2 = 50%; 1:3, 2:3, 10:13).

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 32–59)
- **2.1 Length (pp. 32–34).** Length in mm/cm/m/km; metre rule and tape measure; measuring by string then a ruler; converting cm↔m (10 cm = 0.1 m; 70 cm = 0.7 m; 250 cm = 2.5 m; 100 m = 10,000 cm; 2.7 m = 270 cm); conversion exercises (20/420/57/3709/500/6050 cm; 2/5/3.7/20/1.4/400 m); 2,000 cm in metres; 1,000 cm walked per day; wire lengths 102 m + 70 m; pipes 10.5 m + 7 m.
- **2.2 Area (pp. 35–43).** Area = space a shape takes, measured in squares; rectangle area = length × width (3 × 2 = 6; 8 × 6 = 48; 9 × 5 = 45 square units); counting squares on grids (Exercise 2, picture-dependent); units cm²/m²; square area = side × side (4 cm → 16 cm²; 9 cm → 81 cm²; land 25 m → 625 m²; stool top 30 cm); rectangle area (bench 70 × 50 cm = 3,500 cm²; room 4 m × 6 m — see slips; plots 20 × 15 m etc.); measuring the classroom door/floor.
- **2.3 Volume and Capacity (pp. 44–50).** Volume = space inside a solid (cube, ball, cylinder, pyramid); volume = L × W × H; counting cubes in layers (6 layers of 40 — see slips); volume exercises (picture-dependent cubes); capacity in litres; half/quarter-litre bottles filling 1/3/5/10/20/60-litre containers; litres in 4/12/16/20/24 quarter-litres and 2/4/8/12/16/18/20/40 half-litres; weight: 1 kg = 1000 g; a paperclip ≈ 1 g; 1000 paperclips ≈ 1 kg; estimating weights of classroom objects.
- **2.4 Money (pp. 51–56).** South Sudanese Pound; denominations listed as 1, 5, 10, 20, 25, 50, 100 pounds ("six notes" for seven denominations — see slips); buying/selling, buyer/seller; making change (SSP 100 in 10s; SSP 50 in 20s; 2 × 100 in 25s; 5 × 100 in 20s; 150 in 5s; 500 in 20s; 200 in 10s; 5 × 100 in 50s; 1000 in 50s; 50 in 5s); profit = selling price − buying price (book SSP 20 → 30, profit 10); loss = buying − selling (radio SSP 1200 → 1000, loss 200); profit/loss cases (car 800,000 → 950,000; cooker 6,000 → 5,000; book 750 → 790; table 2,400 → 1,800); class shop price list (picture-dependent prices) with 7 change problems.
- **2.5 Time (pp. 57–59).** Clock hands (hour, minute, seconds); 12-hour vs 24-hour clock; converting 12-hour → 24-hour (12 midnight = 0:00; 12:35 AM = 0:35; 4:45 PM = 16:45; 11:50 PM = 23:50) and 24-hour → 12-hour (0:10 = 12:10 AM; 14:55 = 2:55 PM); conversion exercises both ways (1:24 pm … 12:42 am; 13:41 … 15:39).

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 60–69)
- **3.1 Intersection lines (p. 60).** "Intersection lines is a single point where two lines meet or cross each other and will not form right angles" (muddled definition — see slips); point K = intersection of line PQ and line AB.
- **3.2 Perpendicular lines (pp. 60–61).** Perpendicular = "at right angles" (90°); AB ⊥ DF; walls perpendicular to the ground; are pictured lines perpendicular? (3 items, picture-dependent).
- **3.3 Parallel lines (p. 61).** Parallel lines stay the same distance apart and never meet; which pair of lines are parallel? (picture-dependent).
- **3.4 Angles (pp. 62–63).** Angle = space between two meeting lines; arms of the angle; acute < 90°, right = 90°, obtuse > 90° and < 180°, straight = 180°; drawing right angles from flat-object corners; clock-face angles; roof pitch "at least 39 degrees and at maximum 48 degrees" (an unsupported specific claim — see slips); carpenter visit.
- **3.5 Using a protractor to measure angles (pp. 64–67).** Lining up the protractor (vertex, zero line); worked readings 45° and 138°; drawing angles (mark, connect, label); recap (360° in a circle; protractor measures and draws angles); Exercise 3: draw intersecting lines, measure angles, identify types (picture-dependent); map activity (Thompiny Road, Gudele Avenue, Teresa Road, John Street, Mary 1st/2nd Streets, Buluk Avenue, Ministries Road, Janet Street) — parallel/perpendicular street pairs; Exercise 4: identify 8 pictured line pairs as parallel/perpendicular.

#### UNIT 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 70–78)
- **4.1 Like and unlike terms (pp. 70–71).** Like terms = same literal coefficients to the same powers (3m and −7m; z and 2/3 z); unlike terms (9p and 9q; x/3 and y/3); "we can only add or subtract like terms" (pencils vs books analogy); x + x = 2x; x + y stays x + y.
- **4.2 Algebraic problems involving addition and subtraction (pp. 72–74).** Akong's x bananas + y oranges = x + y fruits; collecting like terms (x + 2x + 3x = 6x; 7x + 2 + 3x = 10x + 2; 12x − 3 − 9x = 3x − 3; 2x + 7y + 3x − 2y = 5x + 5y; 10w + 3z + 11w − z + y = 21w + 2z + y); simplification exercises.
- **4.3 Formation and simplification of algebraic expressions (pp. 75–78).** Writing expressions from words ("five times a number minus three times another number" = 5n − 3m; "(n + 5) × 3" = 3(n + 5)); age problems (Kamal twice his sister: sum 3x; man 2 years older than wife: sum 2x + 2; teacher thrice Debora: difference 2y; Kariem 7 cm taller than Rachael — names change mid-example, see slips; Hillary twice Abraham, Amon 3 years older: sum 4x + 3); Exercise 2 (simplify 8 expressions; farmer's y cows and 20 more goats; girls twice boys; 15 marks less in geography; Anne 5 years older than Vivian, sum 4 years ago; "think of a number, square it and add 5, result 21" → 4). Note: the unit introduction promises "we'll look at [linear equations] in more detail soon" (p. 70) but no equation-solving is ever taught — only x + 4 = 10 → x = 6 and y − 7 = 5 → y = 12 as illustrations.

#### UNIT 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 79–85)
- **5.1 Data collection (pp. 79–80).** Statistics = collection, recording, representation, analysis, interpretation of data; primary data (counting sellers at a market) vs secondary data (books, magazines, newspapers, trade journals); methods: observation, interview, questionnaire (essentials of a good questionnaire), experimentation.
- **5.2 Methods of recording and representing data (pp. 80–85).** Recording by tallying, charts, tables, grouping, listing responses; bar graphs (same-width rectangles, gaps between bars); line graphs (trend over time; x = time/variable); pie charts (segments proportional to cases); car-colours survey (50 cars: 15 red, 27 white, blue = 8 implied); vehicles per day (Sun 300, Mon 600, Tue 400, Wed 450, Thu 700, Fri 800, Sat 1000) as a bar graph; "When I Grow Up" jobs survey + bar graph + handedness pie chart; household size survey (most common size, smallest, largest). The book ends at printed p. 85 with no revision, glossary or index.

### What it does NOT teach (honest negatives)
- No multiplication or division of multi-digit numbers (only ×10/×100 facts and tables to 9 × 10); no long division; no decimals as a topic (decimals appear only as conversion results, e.g. 0.1 m, and in percent conversion); no negative numbers.
- Fractions: only same-denominator addition/subtraction and equivalence; no unlike-denominator work, no fractions of quantities, no mixed numbers, no fraction↔decimal conversion beyond the percent table.
- Ratio/percentage: no unitary method, no "percentage increase/decrease" as a general method (only two worked cases), no compound interest or discount beyond one shirt example.
- Area: rectangles and squares only — no triangles, circles, or compound shapes; no perimeter taught anywhere in the book (despite the G2/G3 guides' repeated perimeter example).
- Volume: counting cubes only — no cm³/m³ units, no formula applied to numbers (the guide claims cm³/m³ are taught; they are not).
- Geometry: no triangles' angle sum, no symmetry (that was Primary 3), no coordinates, no transformations, no 3-D shapes beyond naming cube/ball/cylinder/pyramid.
- Algebra: no solving equations (despite the p. 70 promise), no substitution/evaluation, no brackets expansion, no multiplication of terms.
- Statistics: no tally charts actually drawn, no mode/median/mean/range, no probability (despite the guide's Unit 5 preamble about "events that will happen, may happen or can never happen" — probability is never taught).
- Time: no elapsed-time/duration word problems, no timetables, no calendar work.

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 88 PDF pages present, in order, legible; contents page matches the body (all sections present at the listed pages).
- The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles (NUMBERS / MEASUREMENT / GEOMETRY / ALGEBRA / STATISTICS appear only as graphics on unit openers) and repeats every heading twice (a layout duplication in the contents list itself).
- Several exercises exist only as pictures with no text (area grids pp. 38–39, volume cubes pp. 45–46, shop prices p. 55, street map p. 68, angle diagrams pp. 62–67); a text-only reader cannot recover their values. The teacher's guide supplies some of these answers (area: 24, 40, 64, 27, 55 …).
- Exercise numbering repeats: there are two different "EXERCISE 2"s in Unit 1 (pp. 7 and 12) and two "Exercise 1"s in Unit 4 (pp. 74 and 78).

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 2 (PDF p. 5), 1.1 Example 1:** "Write 3647 in words" but the answer line reads "3467 = Three thousand, four hundred and sixty seven." The number changes from 3647 to 3467 mid-example (3467 is correctly named; 3647 is not).
2. **Printed p. 6 (PDF p. 9), 1.1 Example 3 Q2:** "Use 8, 6, 5, 3 to form two different numbers" — the solution gives "86531, 13568", five-digit numbers using digits (1, 8, 6, 5, 3) that were not in the given set.
3. **Printed p. 7 (PDF p. 10), 1.1 Exercise 2 Q2:** "form three different numbers of 4 digits" but each digit set has FIVE digits (7, 1, 3, 2, 0 etc.) — instruction and sets disagree.
4. **Printed p. 11 (PDF p. 14), 1.2 rounding explanation:** "In this case the number is one (1) so add a zero into the digit in the thousands position. If the number is between (0) and (4) add a zero (0) to the digit in the thousands position." — "add a zero into the digit" is wrong phrasing for "leave the thousands digit unchanged". (Worked answers 36,178 → 36,000; 57,812 → 58,000; 96,574 → 97,000 are correct.)
5. **Printed p. 22 (PDF p. 25), 1.4 Exercise 6:** items j and k are identical (1/4 + 1/4 + 1/4 + 1/4 twice); items i–m end with "−𝑜𝑟 −" (a garbled "or" prompt).
6. **Printed p. 22 (PDF p. 25), 1.4 subtraction key point:** "In subtraction, when the denominator is the same, we just subtract the numerator then divide by the denominator" — wrong wording (the denominator stays the same; nothing is divided). The worked examples (3/7 − 2/7 = 1/7 etc.) are correct.
7. **Printed p. 23 (PDF p. 26), 1.5:** "The ratio of shaded to unshaded is 2:6. What is the ratio of unshaded to shaded? This ratio expressed as a fraction is 2/6." The fraction given (2/6) is the shaded:unshaded ratio, not the unshaded:shaded ratio asked for (6:2 = 6/2). The shaded-to-whole fraction 2/8 that follows is correct.
8. **Printed p. 24 (PDF p. 27), 1.5 Exercise 8 Q4:** "A class seven science teacher asked her students…" — "class seven" in a Primary 4 book (copied from elsewhere).
9. **Printed p. 27 (PDF p. 30), 1.6 Example 17:** "Convert 0.54 to percent … thus it becomes 50%." Wrong: 0.54 = 54%, not 50%. (The reverse conversion 54% → 0.54 just above it is correct.)
10. **Printed p. 29 (PDF p. 32), 1.6 Exercise 9 Q9:** "class four scoped 24 points" — "scoped" for "scored".
11. **Printed p. 43 (PDF p. 46), 2.2 Example 3 Q2:** "Work out the area of a rectangular room which measures 4 m by 6 m. Solution: Area = 2 m × 3 m = 6 m²." The numbers change: 4 × 6 = 24 m², not 2 × 3 = 6 m².
12. **Printed p. 45 (PDF p. 48), 2.3 Example 4 (volume):** "There are 6 layers of 40 cubes each. 40 + 40 + 40 + 40 + 40 + 40 = 240 cubic units. Volume = 40 × 11 = 440 cubic units." The two results contradict each other; 40 × 6 = 240 is right, "40 × 11 = 440" is wrong.
13. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 54), 2.4:** "Look at these six notes. They are in (1, 5, 10, 20, 25, 50 and 100 pounds)" — six notes claimed, seven denominations listed.
14. **Printed p. 60 (PDF p. 63), 3.1:** "Intersection lines is a single point where two lines meet or cross each other and will not form right angles." Muddled: the intersection is the point, not the lines; and intersecting lines CAN form right angles (that is the perpendicular case taught on the next page).
15. **Printed p. 63 (PDF p. 66), 3.4:** "the roof of a house has to be at least 39 degrees and at maximum 48 degrees" — a precise claim with no source or justification in the book.
16. **Printed p. 68 (PDF p. 71), 3.5 Activity 3 Q5:** "Is Janet Street parallel to Julie Street?" — there is no "Julie Street" on the map (the streets are Thompiny, Gudele, Teresa, John, Mary 1st, Mary 2nd, Buluk, Ministries, Janet). The teacher's guide answers "No, they intersect" instead of flagging the phantom street.
17. **Printed p. 68 (PDF p. 71), 3.5 Activity 3 Q4:** "perpendicular to Terasa Road" — the map spells it "Teresa Road".
18. **Printed p. 77 (PDF p. 80), 4.3 Example 4 Q4:** "Kariem is 7 cm taller than Rachael… Let Mary be h cm tall. Peter = (h + 7) cm" — the names change mid-example (Kariem/Rachael → Mary/Peter).
19. **Printed p. 78 (PDF p. 81), 4.3 Exercise 2 Q3–Q5 are underdetermined as set:** Q3 "The number of girls in a class is twice that of boys. What is the difference in the number of students?" (no total or count given); Q4 "A student scored 15 marks less in geography than mathematics. What was the total marks?" (no marks given); Q5 "Anne is 5 years older than Vivian. Find the sum of their ages four years ago?" (no ages given). (The guide supplies algebraic answers — y, 2x − 15, 2x − 3 — treating the unknowns as the answer.)
20. **Printed p. 5 (PDF p. 8), 1.1 Exercise 1 Q13:** "place value of the forth digit in 100000" — "forth" for "fourth".

### Citation anchors (checked 2026-11-05 — see the check line at the top of this section)
1. Place value to ten-thousands: 23,456 = twenty-three thousand, four hundred and fifty-six — printed p. 2.
2. Comparing: 4263 > 4193 (same thousands, bigger hundreds) — printed p. 6.
3. Rounding to nearest thousand: 8697 → 9000 — printed p. 11.
4. First five multiples of 7: 7, 14, 21, 28, 35 — printed p. 13.
5. Factors of 12: 1 and 12, 2 and 6, 3 and 4 — printed p. 14.
6. Equivalent fractions: 1/3 = 2/6 = 4/12 — printed p. 19.
7. Adding same-denominator fractions: 2/9 + 1/9 = 3/9 — printed p. 21.
8. Ratio: 3 cups flour to 1 cup water = ratio 3:1 — printed p. 23.
9. Ratio scaling: 4:5 is the same as 8:10 — printed p. 24.
10. Percent: 25% of 80 = 20 — printed p. 27.
11. Percent to decimal: 54% = 0.54 — printed p. 27.
12. Percentages as fractions: 20% = 20/100 = 1/5 — printed p. 29. [corrected 2026-11-05: Example 18 is on p. 29, not p. 30]
13. Length conversion: 250 cm = 2.5 m — printed p. 33.
14. Rectangle area = length × width: 8 × 6 = 48 square units — printed p. 37.
15. Square area: side 9 cm → 81 cm² — printed p. 41.
16. Volume = L × W × H — printed pp. 44–45.
17. Profit = selling price − buying price (book SSP 20 → 30, profit SSP 10) — printed p. 54.
18. 12-hour to 24-hour: 4:45 PM = 16:45 — printed p. 57.
19. Perpendicular lines meet at a right angle, 90° — printed p. 60.
20. Angle types: acute < 90°, right = 90°, obtuse > 90° and < 180°, straight = 180° — printed p. 62.
21. Like terms: 3m and −7m are like terms; 9p and 9q are unlike — printed p. 70.
22. Collecting like terms: x + 2x + 3x = 6x — printed p. 72.


## Grade 4, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 4 (© 2018; 66 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 3)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 18 anchors — 16 passed, 2 corrected, 0 not found.

### Identification (as printed)
- **Title:** Primary Mathematics — Teacher's Guide 4 (cover: "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 4", "PRIMARY 4")
- **Series:** Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction primary mathematics series
- **Copyright line (p. 1):** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual authors are named anywhere. No publisher name is printed inside this file (the foreword thanks "Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya").
- **Foreword:** by Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Hon. Minister of General Education and Instruction; approves the guide for use in all schools except international schools "with effect from 4th February, 2019". Thanks UNICEF South Sudan (Global Partnership for Education funding), the Curriculum Foundation (UK), the British Council and DfID.
- **File:** `g04-teacher.pdf` — 66 PDF pages, 5,448,362 bytes. Printed pagination: contents on printed p. i, then printed pp. 1–63. **Printed page = PDF page − 3** throughout (PDF p. 4 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 66 = printed p. 63). All page numbers below are PRINTED pages unless marked "PDF".
- **Language:** English throughout.

### Method
Read in full, page by page, from the PDF's embedded text layer (clean and complete). The companion Pupil's Book 4 was read separately (see g04-student.md) — companion verdicts below rest on both books. I checked the guide's numeric keys against the pupil's book's items wherever both exist. No section was sampled: every page's text was read.

### Contents — what it teaches, unit by unit
The guide has a 5-page introduction (printed pp. 1–5, identical in substance to the P1/P2/P3 guides: problem-based learning, open-ended questions with the same 160 cm rectangle example, group work, peer teaching, assessment by observation/conversation/product) then five units. The introduction's unit table (p. 1) calls Unit 5 "Statistics: line graphs and bar chars" ("chars" for "charts").

#### UNIT 1: NUMBERS (guide printed pp. 6–26)
- **Unit overview (pp. 6–7):** "Learn about" table, key inquiry questions, learning outcomes (numbers to 5 digits; rounding to nearest thousands; multiples/factors; same-denominator fractions; percentages and ratios).
- **1.1 Write, read, compare and order numbers up to 5 digits (pp. 8–15).** Activity 1 key (645; 89,321; 6,450; 21,534; 64,500; 48,502 in words). Abacus-making instructions (p. 9). Exercise 1 key: writing in words (25,810; 32,481; 48,362); numerals from words (67,820; 36,514; 82,356); place-value tables; place value of named digits — Q7 all correct (57691 tens; 79841 ten-thousands; 1869 ones; 50927 hundreds; 94641 ten-thousands; 59243 thousands; 6293 tens; 69342 thousands); Q8 underlined-digit answers (64516 thousands; 97201 ones; 16037 hundreds; 97165 ten-thousands; 10000 ten-thousands; 99999 ten-thousands; 87923 ones; 67245 tens — the last is labelled "e" a second time); Q9 ten-thousands; Q10 ones; Q11 thousands; Q12 ten-thousands; Q13 "Ten Thousands" for the 4th digit in 100000 — inconsistent (see slips); Q14 "4010" for the sum of the 4th- and 2nd-digit place values in 34261 — wrong, should be 4060 (see slips); Q15 "80000" correct. Exercise 2 (ordering) is NOT keyed here — the guide moves straight to rounding (p. 12: "After explaining the example Learners should be able to work out exercise 2" with no answers).
- **1.2 Rounding off to the nearest thousands (pp. 12–15).** Activity 2 key (87,163 → 87,000; 21,875 → 22,000; 74,169 → 74,000). Exercise 2 key: 58,712 → 59,000; 72,162 → 72,000; 15,062 → 15,000; 90,762 → 91,000; add-then-round (25,812 + 404 = 26,216 → 26,000; 82,167 + 523 = 82,690 → 83,000; 39,418 + 600 = 40,018 → 40,000; 91,276 + 723 = 91,999 → 92,000). All correct.
- **1.3 Multiples and factors (p. 15).** Exercise 4: Q1 key "9 × 6 = 54; 8 × 7 = 56; 4 × 9 = 36" does not match any identifiable pupil's-book item (the pupil's Q1 is a picture-based fill-in); Q2 10 people × 5 sweets = 50; Q3 9 learners × 10 SSP = 90 SSP; Q4a 44 × 100 = 4400; Q4b "59 × 10 =" left BLANK (should be 590); Q4c 7 × 8 = 56. The factors/multiples lists (Q5–Q6) have NO key.
- **1.4 Addition and subtraction of fractions (pp. 16–18).** Teaching notes (proper/improper/mixed/equivalent fractions — types named but not taught in the pupil's book). Activity 4: "A has denominator 12 and is equivalent to 2/3 i.e. 8/12 and 12/18 are also equivalent to 2/3" — muddled (12/18 has denominator 18; see slips). Exercise 6 key — only items a–e of 13: a) 5/7; b) 3/8; c) 2/4 or 1/2; d) 7/9; e) "3/6 or 1/3" — WRONG (pupil's item e is 1/6 + 3/6 = 4/6 = 2/3). Exercise 7 key all correct: 8/12 = 2/3; 16/24 = 2/3; 8/20 = 2/5; 9/21 = 3/7; 8/24 = 1/3; subtractions 5/9, 2/19, 1/11, 3/5, 9/17, 2/4 = 1/2.
- **1.5 Ratio (pp. 19–20).** Activity 5 keys: green:red = 5:6 (picture-dependent); without gumboots 36 − 14 = 22, ratio 22:14 = 11:7 (the working is printed "34 − 14", see slips). Exercise 8 keys: milk:water 3:2, water:milk 2:3, milk fraction 3/5; textbooks 80:70 = 8:7, English fraction 70/150 = 7/15; black:blue 8:4 = 2:1; stems 32 − (11 + 9) = 12, stems:leaves 12:11 (printed "Leaves 71", see slips); brown:white squares 5:11, as a fraction 5/11. All values correct.
- **1.6 Percentages (pp. 21–26).** Activity 6 keys: bad apples 15% of 500 = 75, good 425; shirt 30% of 1500 = 450, new price SSP 1050 (both methods). Exercise 9 key: 20% = 20/100 etc.; 70% of 700 = 490; 15/45 = 33⅓%; 2/10 = 20%; 50% of 200 = 100; fractions as percentages — 2/3 = 66⅔%, 1/8 = 12.5% (printed "1/8 × 10%", see slips), 4/5 = 80%, 3/4 = 75%, 7/10 = 70%; "12% of 350 = 42%" — should be 42, not 42% (see slips); 48/50 = 96%; missed 6/30 = 20%; population 112% of 500,000 = 560,000. Exercise 10 key: 30% = 3/10; 75% = 3/4; 90% = 9/10; "62% = 62/100 = 31/30" — WRONG, should be 31/50; 22% = 11/50 (only 5 of 19 items keyed). Exercise 11 key: ratios 3:10, 9:10, 4:5, 18:25, 29:50; percent↔decimal "a) 0.28, b) 20%, c) 0.3%, d) 25%, e) 0.09" — c) should be 0.3 (not 0.3%) and e) should be 0.96 (not 0.09); ratios as percentages (1:3 = 33⅓%; 2:3 = 66⅔%; 10:13 = 76¹²⁄₁₃%; 1:4 = 25%; 1:5 = 20%); ratios as fractions (2/5, 7/9, 10/17, 1/4, 1/3). The guide also repeats the pupil's "divide the percentage by 10 0" typo.

#### UNIT 2: MEASUREMENT (guide printed pp. 27–43)
- **Unit overview (pp. 27–28):** length/area/volume/capacity/weight/time/money. Note the overview claims volume will be investigated "in cm3 and m3" — the pupil's book teaches volume only by counting cubes (see slips).
- **2.1 Length (pp. 29–30).** Exercise 1 key: cm→m (0.2; 4.2; 0.57; 37.09; "05m" for 500 cm — should be 5 m; 60.5 m); m→cm (200; 500; 370; 2000; 140; 40000 cm); 2000 cm = 20 m; 1000 cm = 10 m; wire 102 + 70 = 172 m; pipes 1050 + 700 = 1750 cm. All values correct except the "05m" typo.
- **2.2 Area (pp. 31–35).** Exercise 2 key (counting squares, picture-dependent): a. 24; b. 40; c. 64; d. 27; e. 55; f. 36; g. 36; h. 15; i. 117; j. 28; k. 180; l. 80; m. 81. Exercise 3 key: squares 4, 16, 225, 400 cm²; 49, 36, 25, 100 m²; land 625 m²; the stool-top question (30 cm side → 900 cm²) is NOT answered. Exercise 4 key: rectangles 18, 70, 40 cm²; plots 300, 540, 300 m². The guide cites "example 2 (page 34 Learner's book)" for square area — the pupil's Example 2 is on printed p. 41, not p. 34 (see slips).
- **2.3 Volume and Capacity (pp. 35–37).** Exercise 5 (volume cubes): no key given. Exercise 6 key: 20 half-litre bottles for 10 L; "Eighty ½ litre bottles" for 20 L — wrong unit (the question asks for ¼-litre bottles; should be "eighty ¼-litre bottles"); 60 bottles for 60 L; quarter-litres → 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 litres; half-litres → 6, 9, 20, 2, 8, 10, 4, 1 litres. All values correct apart from the unit slip.
- **2.4 Money (pp. 39–40).** Exercise 7 (making change): no key ("Check the working out"). Exercise 8 key: profit SSP 150,000; loss SSP 1,000; profit SSP 40; loss SSP 600. All correct. Exercise 9 (shop role-play): no key.
- **2.5 Time (pp. 41–43).** Worked note: 20:27 = 8:27 p.m. (20 − 12 = 8). Exercise 10 key: 1:24 pm = 1324h; 2:56 am = 0256h; 7:45 pm = 1945h; 5:16 am = 0516h; 3:56 pm = 1556h; 12:25 pm = 1225h; 11:27 pm = 2327h; 8:13 pm = 2013h; 12:42 am = 0042h; reverse: 13:41 = 1:41 pm; 17:50 = 5:50 pm; 04:32 = 4:32 am; 12:36 = 12:36 pm; 23:25 = 11:25 pm; 08:53 = 8:53 am; 00:51 = 12:51 am; 19:08 = 7:08 pm; 15:39 = 3:39 am. All correct.

#### UNIT 3: GEOMETRY (guide printed pp. 44–51)
- **Unit overview (pp. 44–45):** intersecting/parallel/perpendicular lines, angle types, protractor.
- **3.1–3.2 Intersection / perpendicular lines (pp. 46–47).** Teaching notes only. Exercise 1 (are these lines perpendicular?): only Q1 answered — "No, they are parallel". Q2–Q3 unanswered.
- **3.3 Parallel lines (p. 47).** Exercise 2 key: "A parallel. B perpendicular. C intersect." (picture-dependent).
- **3.4 Angles (pp. 47–48).** Teaching notes; roof-pitch application repeated without comment.
- **3.5 Using a protractor (pp. 49–51).** "A protractor is an angle of measure" (garbled — should be "an instrument for measuring angles"). Exercise 3 key: "iii) 1800, straight line" (should be 180°) and "vi) 900; right angle" (should be 90°); other items "check accuracy". Activity 3 (map) key: parallel pairs Mary 1st & Mary 2nd Streets, Ministries Road & Buluk Avenue; Gudele Avenue perpendicular to Mary 1st Street; perpendicular to John Street: Thompiny Road, Gudele Avenue, "Terasa Road" (spelling as in the pupil's book); perpendicular to Teresa Road: Ministries Road, "Bulik Avenue" (typo for Buluk) and John Street; "Is Janet Street parallel to Julie Street? No, they intersect" — the guide answers the phantom-street question instead of flagging it. Exercise 4 key: Parallel, Perpendicular, Perpendicular, Parallel, Perpendicular, Parallel, Perpendicular, Parallel — but numbered 1–7 then "6) Parallel" again (duplicate 6, no 8).

#### UNIT 4: ALGEBRA (guide printed pp. 52–57)
- **Unit overview (p. 52):** letters for numbers, like/unlike terms, simple algebraic expressions.
- **4.1 Like and unlike terms (p. 53).** Teaching notes only.
- **4.2 Algebraic problems (pp. 54–55).** Exercise 1 key: a) 4x + 2y; b) 2p + 5q; c) 6a + 4b; d) 3x + 2y + w; e) 3x + w; Q2: 4x + 7y + 5. All correct.
- **4.3 Formation and simplification (pp. 55–57).** Exercise 1 key: a) "5x =27y" — wrong operator (should be 5x + 27y; the pupil's item is 13x + 7y − 8x + 20y); b) 27x + 1; c) 4x + 17y; d) 21a + 5b; e) −40q + 22s; f) 8x + 4; g) 11x + 23; h) −10x + 11y — all others correct. Exercise 2 key: cows y, goats 20 + y, total 2y + 20; girls 2y, boys y, difference y; maths x, geography x − 15, total 2x − 15; Vivian x, Anne x + 5, sum four years ago 2x − 3; number x, x² + 5 = 21, x = 4. All correct as algebra (the questions are underdetermined as arithmetic — see the pupil's-book filing).

#### UNIT 5: STATISTICS (guide printed pp. 58–63)
- **Unit overview (p. 58):** data, bar and line graphs. The preamble (p. 59) says learners will use information "to predict or make guesses about events that will happen, may happen or can never happen" — probability language for a unit that never teaches probability.
- **5.1 Data collection (p. 59).** Teaching notes only.
- **5.2 Methods of recording and representing data (pp. 60–63).** Activity 2 (car colours): extended notes on survey validity (same vehicles counted twice; parked vs moving; least-popular colours with frequency zero). Exercise 1 (jobs survey): guidance on grouping jobs; Exercise 2 (household size): guidance only. No numeric keys anywhere in Unit 5. The guide ends at printed p. 63 with no revision or index.

### Companion verdict — does it companion Pupil's Book 4?
**Yes in structure; partially in keys.** Same 5 units in the same order, same sub-unit titles and numbering (1.1–1.6, 2.1–2.5, 3.1–3.5, 4.1–4.3, 5.1–5.2), same exercise numbering (with the same duplicated "EXERCISE 2" in Unit 1). It gives worked keys for most of Units 1, 2 and 4, but:
- It gives NO keys for: Exercise 2 ordering (Unit 1), Exercise 4 Q5–Q6 (factors/multiples), Exercise 6 items f–m (fractions), Exercise 5 volume, Exercise 7 money change, Exercise 9 shop role-play, Exercise 1 Q2–Q3 perpendicular lines, and ALL of Unit 5.
- Its keys contain fresh errors of their own (Exercise 1 Q13/Q14; Exercise 6e; Exercise 10 Q1d; Exercise 11 Q2c/e; Exercise 6 Q2's unit; Exercise 1a's "=" for "+").
- It repeats the pupil's errors wherever it reprints pupil's text (the "1 m 92 cm"-type errors are not flagged; the phantom "Julie Street" question is answered, not flagged; "divide the percentage by 10 0" is repeated).
- It cites wrong pupil's-book pages twice: "example 2 (page 34)" for square area (actually pupil's p. 41) and ratio notes "(Page 21)" (pupil's ratio section is pp. 22–24; p. 21 is fractions subtraction).
- The guide's contents page numbers are the GUIDE's own pages (e.g. guide 1.4 at guide p. 16 vs pupil's p. 18; guide 2.3 at guide p. 35 vs pupil's p. 44).
- No unit in the guide is missing from the pupil's book and vice versa.

### What it does NOT teach / does not provide (honest negatives)
- No keys for roughly a third of the book's exercises (see above), including all of statistics.
- No tests, revision material, schemes of work or timings.
- No additional content beyond the pupil's book; no equation-solving despite the pupil's book's promise.

### Held-copy defects
- None structural: all 66 PDF pages present, in order, legible.
- The Table of Contents prints blank unit titles (NUMBERS / MEASUREMENT / GEOMETRY / ALGEBRA / STATISTICS appear only as graphics in the pupil's book) and repeats every heading twice (a layout duplication).

### Slips / wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 11 (PDF p. 14), Exercise 1 Q14 key:** "4010" for the sum of the place values of the 4th and 2nd digits in 34261. Correct: 4th digit from the right is 4 (thousands = 4000), 2nd is 6 (tens = 60); sum = 4060. [page corrected 2026-11-05: was cited as printed p. 14]
2. **Printed p. 11 (PDF p. 14), Exercise 1 Q13 key:** "Ten Thousands" for the 4th digit in 100000. Counting from the right (the guide's own convention in Q11–Q12), the 4th digit is 0 in the thousands place; "ten thousands" is the 5th digit. [page corrected 2026-11-05: was cited as printed p. 14]
3. **Printed p. 18 (PDF p. 21), Exercise 6 key (e):** "3/6 or 1/3" for 1/6 + 3/6. Correct: 4/6 = 2/3.
4. **Printed p. 25 (PDF p. 28), Exercise 10 key (d):** "62% = 62/100 = 31/30". Correct: 62/100 = 31/50.
5. **Printed p. 25 (PDF p. 28), Exercise 11 key Q2:** "c) 0.3%" — 30% as a decimal is 0.3, not 0.3%; "e) 0.09" — 96% as a decimal is 0.96, not 0.09.
6. **Printed p. 23 (PDF p. 26), Exercise 9 key Q7:** "12% of 350 = 42%" — the answer is 42, not 42%.
7. **Printed p. 23 (PDF p. 26), Exercise 9 key Q6b:** "1/8 × 10% = 12.5%" — "10%" is a typo for "100%".
8. **Printed p. 36 (PDF p. 39), Exercise 6 key Q2:** "Eighty ½ litre bottles" for "How many ¼ litre bottles can fill a 20 litre container?" — the number 80 is right but the unit is wrong (¼-litre bottles).
9. **Printed p. 30 (PDF p. 33), Exercise 1 key Q1e:** "05m" for 500 cm — should be 5 m.
10. **Printed p. 57 (PDF p. 60), Exercise 1 key (a):** "5x =27y" — should be "5x + 27y" (13x + 7y − 8x + 20y).
11. **Printed p. 19 (PDF p. 22), Exercise 4 Q4b:** "59 × 10 =" left blank (no answer given; 590).
12. **Printed p. 15 (PDF p. 18), Exercise 4 Q1 key:** "9 × 6 = 54; 8 × 7 = 56; 4 × 9 = 36" — these do not correspond to any identifiable pupil's-book item (the pupil's Q1 is a picture-based fill-in).
13. **Printed p. 17 (PDF p. 20), Activity 4 note:** "8/12 and 12/18 are also equivalent to 2/3" in a sentence about fractions "with denominator 12" — 12/18 has denominator 18.
14. **Printed p. 20 (PDF p. 23), Activity 5 Q2 working:** "Without gumboots = 34 − 14 = 22" — "34" is a typo for "36" (36 − 14 = 22 is right).
15. **Printed p. 20 (PDF p. 23), Exercise 8 Q4:** "Leaves 71" — typo for "Leaves 11".
16. **Printed p. 50 (PDF p. 53), Exercise 3 key:** "iii) 1800, straight line" and "vi) 900; right angle" — should be 180° and 90°.
17. **Printed p. 51 (PDF p. 54), Exercise 4 key:** items numbered 1–7 then "6) Parallel" again — duplicate 6, no 8.
18. **Printed p. 50 (PDF p. 53), Activity 3 key Q5:** answers "No, they intersect" for "Is Janet Street parallel to Julie Street?" — there is no Julie Street on the map; the guide should have flagged the phantom street. Q4 also prints "Bulik Avenue" for "Buluk Avenue".
19. **Printed p. 49 (PDF p. 52), 3.5:** "A protractor is an angle of measure" — garbled definition.
20. **Printed p. 33 (PDF p. 36):** cites "example 2 (page 34 Learner's book)" for square area — the pupil's Example 2 is on printed p. 41. Printed p. 19 cites ratio notes "(Page 21)" — the pupil's ratio section is printed pp. 22–24.
21. **Printed p. 27 (PDF p. 30), Unit 2 overview:** claims volume will be investigated "in cm3 and m3" — the pupil's book teaches volume only by counting cubes, never cm³/m³.
22. **Printed p. 1 (PDF p. 4), introduction unit table:** Unit 5 is "Statistics: line graphs and bar chars" — "chars" for "charts".
