## Band A cross-notes (for course building)

- **Ladder.** P1 numbers to 99, add/subtract two-digit, non-standard measure, o'clock time, shapes → P2 three-digit numbers, multiplication/division tables, fractions as parts of a whole, time to quarters → P3 four-digit numbers, divisibility, equivalent fractions, cm/m, kg/g, pictographs/block graphs, first inequalities → P4 five-digit numbers, rounding to thousands, multiples/factors, same-denominator fractions, ratio, percentages, area, volume by counting cubes, profit/loss, 12/24-hour clock, angles, like terms, bar/line/pie graphs.
- **Guide quality falls as the grades rise.** P1 guide: no answer key at all. P2 guide: the strongest companion in the band, keys most arithmetic. P3 guide: weak, error-prone keys, repeats the pupil's errors verbatim. P4 guide: keys most of Units 1, 2 and 4, with fresh errors of its own. Where a guide key and the pupil's book disagree, re-compute — the guide is wrong as often as the pupil's book in this band.
- **What to carry, corrected.** P1 TOC page numbers are all 2–4 low; P3 Activity 27's "1/4 = 1/2" claim is false (the 1/2 = 2/4 = 4/8 chain is UNVERIFIED BY CHECK); P4 Example 17 (0.54 = 50%, should be 54%), the 4 × 6 room worked as 2 × 3 = 6 m² (should be 24 m²), and the volume example's "40 × 11 = 440" (should be 40 × 6 = 240). The P2 guide's "8 ÷ 4 = 4" and its "50 litres + 27 litres = 77 litres" (for a subtraction item, correct 23 L) are guide errors, not pupil errors.
- **Picture-led work.** Several exercises exist only as pictures (P2 clocks/money/shape counts, P4 area grids, volume cubes, shop prices, street map, angle diagrams) — the guides supply some of these answers; where neither book gives a number, the lesson must not invent one.


# BAND B — Primary 5–8 (8 files, 708 PDF pages)

All 8 books are in English; all 8 filings are in English. Same 2018 Ministry series (the two Grade 7 books print no © line, ISBN or author). The degree symbol renders as "0" in text layers throughout this band — an extraction artefact, not a print error.


## Grade 5, student — Pupil's Book 5 (© 2018; 94 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 5)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 5, Pupil's Book (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g05-student.pdf` — 94 PDF pages, 7,832,127 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 94 PDF pages, in order, from the extracted text layer plus a visual read of the one image-only page (PDF p. 42, printed p. 37). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 5 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–5 are covers, book-care page, title/imprint, foreword, contents; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 6). The last printed page is 89 (PDF p. 94). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Cover check:** the cover and title page both say "Primary Mathematics 5" / "Pupil's Book 5" and the contents describe the Primary 5 syllabus. NOT swapped with Primary 4 — confirms the brief's cover-proved note.
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** © 2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION (title/imprint page, PDF p. 3). "Published by: MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS" (cover). Foreword (PDF p. 4, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) says the book was approved for use in all schools except international schools with effect from 4 February 2019, developed with Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers (Kenya), funded by the Global Partnership for Education, project managed by UNICEF South Sudan, with professional guidance from the Curriculum Foundation (UK). **No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual author is named anywhere** — authorship is given only as "Ministry of General Education and Instruction … in conjunction with Subject experts". This matches the pattern reported for the English books in this series.
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer (clean, complete text on every page except PDF p. 42, which is a figure-only page read visually). Worked-example arithmetic I checked by hand/calculator where a slip was suspected; those checks are noted under Slips. Exercise items were read in full (they are mostly bare numbers / short prompts). Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the book teaches, unit by unit

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS — printed pp. 1–27 (PDF pp. 6–32)
- **1.1 Reading, writing, ordering and comparing numbers up to six digits (pp. 1–4):** place value to hundred-thousands (worked example 583,216 broken into place values and written in words); flash-card group activity making and ordering 3–6 digit numbers; ordering exercises; a South Sudan state population/area table (10 states, total population 8,923,553 — the printed total checks out) with comparison questions; a disease-case table (malaria 170,000; HIV/AIDS 75,000; typhoid 150,000; TB 1,575; cholera 49,100) with sum/difference questions; a market-price activity (sugar, rice, wheat flour, beans, maize per 50 kg).
- **1.2 Divisibility tests for 3, 4, 6 and 9 (pp. 5–11):** digit-sum rule for 3 and 9 (with repeated summing), last-two-digits rule for 4 (plus a halve-twice shortcut), combined 2-and-3 rule for 6; tables of yes/no examples; pair activities and Exercise 2 (test whether 8,712 is divisible by 3, 4, 6, 9; multiple-choice divisibility items).
- **1.3 Prime numbers (pp. 12–13):** prime = divisible evenly only by 1 or itself; composite defined; examples 5 (prime), 6 and 9 (composite), 7 (prime); Exercise 3 multiple-choice (incl. "how many different prime factors does 252 have?").
- **1.4 Roman numbers and Hindu–Arabic numbers up to 50 (pp. 14–17):** symbols I, V, X, L; full 1–50 table; add/subtract rules (VI = 6, IX = 9); converting numbers to Roman numerals by breaking into tens/ones (worked example 34 = XXXIV); then a 0–50 table of English number words headed "Hindu–Arabic notation" (see Slips — the heading does not match the content, which is just number words).
- **1.5 Factors and multiples (pp. 17–18):** factors as numbers multiplied to get another number; factors of 12 listed including negatives; common factors (12 and 30 → 1, 2, 3, 6).
- **1.6 HCF and LCM (pp. 18–21):** HCF also called GCD; LCM defined; factorisation method stated; real-life examples — HCF of 36 and 24 = 12 (cloth strips); LCM of 12 and 8 = 24 (exercise days); Exercise 5 word problems (identical sets/tables, photo groups, shelving books).
- **1.7 Adding and subtracting fractions using LCM (pp. 21–23):** worked addition 1/3 + 1/6 = 1/2; worked subtraction 1/2 − 1/3 (answer 1/6, layout garbled in the text layer but the method is clear); Exercise 6 (addition, incl. word problems) and Exercise 7 (subtraction, incl. two- and three-term items).
- **1.8 Fractions and decimals (pp. 23–26):** decimals to fractions (0.5 → 1/2; 0.75 → 3/4, with ×10/×100 method); fractions to decimals via making denominator 10/100/1,000 (3/4 → 0.75); Activities 9–10 and Exercises 8–9. Note: Activity 10 asks pupils to convert 1/3 to a decimal — a recurring decimal, though the book's own method (denominator to 10/100/1,000) cannot do it; the book never says so.
- **Unit Revision Exercise (pp. 26–27):** ordering, column addition/subtraction, division, numbers in words/figures, place value of underlined digits, true/false divisibility, "only one number divisible by 3" (326, 811, 179, 928, 657).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT — printed pp. 28–45 (PDF pp. 33–50)
- **2.1 Metres ↔ kilometres (pp. 28–30):** 1 km = 1,000 m, 1 m = 100 cm; worked conversions 750 m = 0.75 km and 7.5 km = 7,500 m; unit table mm/cm/m/km; speed mentioned in passing (bus at 50 km/h); Exercise 1 (convert 6 km, 0.575 km, 7.50 km to metres).
- **2.2 Area of rectangle and square (pp. 30–32):** area of square = side²; area of rectangle = width × height (worked area 15 square units); Exercise 2 (side from area 16 cm²; width from area 45 cm² and length 9 cm; cutting a 10 m × 4 m rectangle into squares; tiling a 24 cm × 8 cm rectangle with 2 cm squares).
- **2.3 Volume of cube and cuboid (pp. 33–37):** cube facts (6 faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices); volume = base area × height; worked examples 3³ = 27, 8³ = 512 cm³, cuboid 9×3×5 = 135, 12×10×8 = 960 cm³; Exercise 3 (15 items, incl. tank volumes and unit conversion m³↔cm³); printed p. 37 is a full-page figure exercise "Calculate the length of the unknown edges" — 9 cuboids with given volumes (I checked all nine: heights/lengths/widths 8, 9, 10, 7, 10, 6, 9, 5, 7 — all consistent; corrected by citation check 2026-11-05: the 7th value was listed as 7.5, the figure gives 360 ÷ (5 × 8) = 9).
- **2.4 Time (pp. 38–41):** clock hands (hour/minute/second); reading 10:10; conversions hours↔minutes↔seconds (2 h = 120 min = 7,200 s; 360 min = 6 h); Exercise 4 (convert hours to minutes incl. halves/quarters, minutes to hours, to seconds, seconds to minutes, mixed minutes-and-seconds; word problems: 4¾ h journey; 7 × 45-min periods).
- **2.5 Temperature in Celsius or Fahrenheit (pp. 42–43):** ice = 0 °C = 32 °F; conversion formulas °C = (°F−32)×5/9 and °F = °C×9/5+32; Activity 4 (measure water temperature); Exercise 5 converts 24°, 19°, 46°, 37° (units not stated) and 168°, 72°, 143°, 200° — the numbers are implausibly large for °C water/air temperatures and no context is given.
- **2.6 Money (pp. 44–45):** profit = selling price minus costs; loss = sold below cost; worked potato-sack example (80 sacks: cost SSP 6,000 each, sold SSP 9,500 each → profit SSP 280,000 — correct); loss version sold at SSP 5,500 (see Slips — printed answer wrong); Exercise 5 (TV bought SSP 18,950, loss SSP 4,780; pencils at SSP 450 per hundred sold at SSP 5 each; second-hand bicycle; apples; chocolates); short note on regional currencies and an exchange-bureau visit activity. No percentage profit/loss, no discount, no simple interest.

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY — printed pp. 46–57 (PDF pp. 51–62)
- **3.1 Constructing parallel lines (pp. 46–49):** ruler-and-compass construction via copying an angle (7 steps, through point R, line RS ∥ PQ); uses of parallel lines (houses, carpentry, planting).
- **3.2 Constructing angles (pp. 49–52):** 60° via equilateral triangle; 30° by bisecting 60°; 90° via arcs (7 steps); 45° by bisecting 90° ("Bisect the angle of 90º" is the whole instruction); measuring angles mentioned only as an activity heading. Note: the degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer ("600 angle") — that is an extraction artefact, not a print error; the printed figures show 60°.
- **3.3 Line of symmetry (pp. 52–57):** reflection/mirror symmetry; regular vs irregular polygons; lines of symmetry of triangles (equilateral 3, isosceles 1, scalene 0); symmetry in nature/art (dog's face, lake reflection, beehives, rugs); Exercise 1 (construct parallel lines of 5/7/10/3 cm; construct 60°, bisect 60°, 90°, bisect 90°; lines of symmetry in A, H, T, X).

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA — printed pp. 58–69 (PDF pp. 63–74)
- **4.1 Purpose of algebraic equations; solving one-step equations (pp. 58–64):** recalls Primary 4 collecting like terms (see Slips — the recall example changes −2 into −z); why algebra exists (shorthand for relationships, e.g. force = mass × acceleration); solving x−4=0, x+5=0, x+7=−5 (x=−12), x−5=4 (x=9), x/5=−3 (x=−15), x/−7=−2 (x=14), x/8=5 (x=40), 4x=20 (x=5), −5x=30 (x=−6); Activities 1–5 and Exercise 1 (20 equations, incl. 340=−17x, −9=n/12, 13a=−143, p/20=−12).
- **4.2 Forming and solving equations (pp. 65–69):** keyword table for +/−/×/÷/= ("four less than a number" → x−4; "nine less a number" → 9−x; "three fourths of a number" → ¾x); worked word problems — goats thrice cows, total 40 → 10 cows; rectangle perimeter 50 cm, length 3 cm more than width → 11 cm × 14 cm; Mary SSP 200 less than Tom, total SSP 1,000 → Tom SSP 600; Activity 7 and Exercise 2 (8 word problems). Summary: algebra lets you substitute letters for names of items.

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS — printed pp. 70–89 (PDF pp. 75–94)
- **5.1 Why data is collected (pp. 70–72):** data = plural of datum; quantitative vs qualitative (mentioned, not developed); uses of data (assess learners, school progress, improvement areas, predict the nation's future); collection methods — observation, interview (structured/unstructured), questionnaire, experimentation; six steps of data collection.
- **5.2 Representing data (pp. 72–89):** bar graphs (construction steps; family-spending percentages; vehicle traffic 6 am–2 pm; test-marks data — see Slips, the frequency table is wrong; hospital patients by weekday; rainfall by month; shoe sizes); line graphs (parts of a line graph; Sam's weight Jan–May 49→73 kg; temperature in South Sudan; people in a store; exercises reading the graphs); pie charts (sector angle = quantity/total × 360°; pet-ownership chart; farm-animals example 1,200 chickens / 200 cows / 300 goats / 100 ducks out of 1,800 — angles correct, percentages wrong, see Slips); Exercise 4 (pie chart of a 24 ha farm; bar graph of trees planted 1998–2002; line graphs of vehicle imports 1994–2002 and of temperature by time of day). The book ends abruptly after Exercise 4 — no unit revision, no glossary, no closing summary for Unit 5.

### What the book does NOT teach (honest negatives, at Primary 5 level)
- No multiplication/division of fractions, no mixed numbers, no equivalent fractions as a topic; no percentages as a topic (percentages appear only inside a bar-graph example and a pie chart); no ratio/proportion; no decimals beyond two places, no recurring decimals explained (1/3 is set as an exercise without comment).
- Measurement: no mass/weight, no capacity/volume-of-liquid (litres), no perimeter as a taught topic (perimeter appears only inside an algebra word problem), no area of triangles or circles, no conversion between cm²/m².
- Geometry: no types of angles (acute/obtuse/reflex) as a topic, no triangle construction, no coordinates, no nets of solids, no tessellation; angle measurement is mentioned but never taught (no protractor use).
- Algebra: only one-step equations (x on one side, single operation); no two-step equations, no equations with x on both sides, no inequalities.
- Statistics: no mode/median/mean, no range, no tally charts as a taught method, no pictographs; pie-chart angles are taught but the worked percentages are wrong (see Slips).
- No revision exercise for Units 2–5 (only Unit 1 has one); no answers section anywhere in the book.

### Held-copy defects
- None observed in the content: no missing pages (printed pp. 1–89 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–94 complete), no blurred/duplicated pages. The only text-layer gap is PDF p. 42 (printed p. 37), which is a figure-only page — read visually, content verified (see above). Minor typographic noise throughout: "angel" for angle, "suck" for sack, "factions" for fractions, "Multiple" for Multiply, "perimetre" (British spelling, consistent), "LCF" for LCM.

### Slips and wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 44, money/loss example:** "480,000 − 440,000 = SSP 4,000" — the correct difference is **SSP 40,000** (a missing zero). The profit example on the same spread is correct (SSP 280,000).
2. **Printed p. 76, statistics frequency table (Exercise 2):** the table does not match its own raw data. The listed 42 marks contain 50 ten times and 35 four times; the table claims 50 eight times and 35 nine times, and its frequencies sum to 45, not 42. Any question answered from the table (e.g. "how many pupils scored 40?") is therefore unreliable.
3. **Printed p. 88, pie-chart percentages:** the printed pie chart labels chicken 58%, goats 23%, cows 10%, ducks 9%; the true shares of 1,200/200/300/100 out of 1,800 are **66.7%, 11.1%, 16.7%, 5.6%**. (The sector angles on pp. 87–88 — 240°, 40°, 60°, 20° — are correct; only the percentage labels are wrong.) The same page's other pie chart (Accounting 48%, Rent 19%, Food 9%, etc., p. 86) sums to 100% and is not checkable against any data.
4. **Printed p. 21, fractions addition example (Example 14):** "Find the L.C.M of denominators 3 and 6. L.C.M of 2 and 3 is 6." The second sentence names the wrong pair (2 and 3); the LCM of 3 and 6 is 6, and the final answer (1/2) is right.
5. **Printed p. 19, HCF/LCM method:** "The product of highest powers of all prime factors gives LCF." "LCF" is a misprint for LCM, and the sentence as written is a garbled statement of the LCM rule.
6. **Printed p. 46, parallel lines defined wrong:** "Lines that divide items into equal parts are called parallel lines." That is not what parallel lines are; the following sentence (lines keep the same distance throughout) is the correct idea.
7. **Printed p. 58, algebra recall example:** the problem is "Simplify x + 2y + x + y − 2" but the worked solution collects terms as "x + x + 2y + y − z = 2x + 3y − z" — the constant −2 has silently become −z.
8. **Printed p. 68, Activity 7 Q1:** asks "how many English text books are in the school?" for a problem about mathematics vs science textbooks — the subject named in the question does not match the problem.
9. **Printed p. 69, Exercise 2 Q5c:** asks for "the number of tulips that were planted" in a problem about mangoes and oranges — tulips appear nowhere else.
10. **Printed p. 68, Exercise 2 Q1:** "the length of a classroom is 8 m more than the width. Given that the area of the classroom is 80m…" — area given in metres (not m²) and the numbers give no whole-number solution (w(w+8)=80 has no integer root); the question as printed is not solvable as a Primary 5 exercise.
11. **Printed p. 85, pie-chart method:** "we take a quantity out of the total then multiply by 3600" — should be 360° (3600 is presumably a misprint for 360°).
12. **Printed pp. 14–17, "Hindu–Arabic notation":** the section so headed is only a table of English number words 0–50; no Hindu–Arabic numerals or place-value notation are shown under that heading. (Roman numerals, the other half of the topic, are taught correctly.)
13. **Printed p. 43, temperature exercise:** converts 24°, 19°, 46°, 37° and 168°, 72°, 143°, 200° with no units or context stated; as °C values several are implausible (46 °C air temperature; 200 °C). The conversion formulas themselves (p. 42–43) are correct.
14. Minor: p. 15 "Break the number according to its specific order of adjectives" (garbled instruction for converting to Roman numerals); p. 7 "take in in turns" (typo); p. 28 "Converting decimals into factions".

## Grade 5, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 5 (no © line or ISBN printed; 76 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 6)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 21 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 5, Teacher's Guide (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g05-teacher.pdf` — 76 PDF pages, 10,636,940 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 76 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full; the two text-empty pages were read visually (PDF p. 4 = the foreword as a scanned image; PDF p. 6 = a genuinely blank page). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 6 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–6 are covers, book-care page, title page, foreword, contents, blank page; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 7). The last printed page is 70 (PDF p. 76). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** title page (PDF p. 3) reads "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 5", "Funded by:" (funder name not printed on the page — same layout as the student book), "This book is the property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE". No © line, no ISBN, no individual author is printed anywhere in the guide. The foreword (PDF p. 4, scanned image, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) is the student book's foreword adapted to the Teacher's Guide, same approval date 4 February 2019, same credits (Longhorn and Mountain Top, Kenya; Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation UK). **No ISBN; no named author** — same as the student book.
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer plus visual reads of the two image/blank pages. Every answer in the guide's keys was re-checked by hand/calculator against the student book's questions; the results are under Companion check and Slips. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the guide contains, unit by unit

#### Front matter — Introduction (printed pp. 1–5, PDF pp. 7–11)
- Introduction (pp. 1–2): the guide is used alongside the learner's book; learner-centred, practical, competency-based; unit overview table (Unit 1 Numbers: Operations; Unit 2 Measurement: profit and loss; Unit 3 Geometry: simple geometric constructions; Unit 4 Algebra: simple equations; Unit 5 Statistics: representation and interpretation of data).
- Teaching strategies (pp. 2–4): problem-based learning, open-ended questions (worked example: rectangle perimeter 160 cm, "what could the side lengths be?"), group work, peer teaching; advice to set additional exercises.
- Classroom assessment (p. 5): observation / conversation / product ("triangulation"), tied to the syllabus "Learn About" sections.

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 6–25, PDF pp. 12–31)
- Unit opener (pp. 6–7): "Learn about" list, key inquiry questions, learning outcomes (knowledge/skills/attitudes), competencies, links to other subjects. Note the outcomes list "Simplification of fractions by cancelling" — the student book never teaches cancelling.
- 1.1 Place value (pp. 8–10): teaching notes, Activity 1 guidance, Exercise 1 expected answers (state populations/disease table — all correct: Jonglei largest, Western Bahr el Ghazal smallest; Warrap ~half of Western Bahr el Ghazal; malaria most common, TB least; sums 219,100 / 445,675 / 168,425 / 200,675 all check out).
- 1.2 Divisibility (pp. 11–14): restated rules for 3, 4, 6, 9 with fresh examples (105 for 3; 1,824 for 4; 68,154 for 6); Exercise 2 key (see Slips — partly wrong/misaligned).
- 1.3 Primes (pp. 15–16): teaching notes; Exercise 3 key (252 has 2 different prime factors; 105 not prime; 19 prime; 69 not prime — all correct).
- 1.4 Roman / Hindu–Arabic to 50 (pp. 16–18): guidance; Exercise 4 key (21 = XXI; 26/24/25/27; 24/6/47/41 — all correct). The "Hindu–Arabic" test items are plain additions (23+15=38 etc.) read aloud — the guide repeats the student book's mislabelling without correcting it.
- 1.5 Factors (pp. 18–19): notes; factors of 18 listed incompletely (2, 3, 6, 9 — omits 1 and 18).
- 1.6 HCF/LCM (pp. 19–21): definitions, "starter activity" questions, Exercise 4 key (2, 3, 10, 30 — correct for the student book's Exercise 5 word problems).
- 1.7 Fractions with LCM (pp. 21–23): method restated; Exercise 6 key (all nine additions correct: 13/42, 13/18, 16/21, 11/15, 5/6, 15/56, 17/33, 7/36, 3/4; word problems 11/15, 17/20, 17/60 — correct); Exercise 7 key (all subtractions correct: 3/20, 17/40, 1/9, 1/6, 1/28, 13/60, 5/14, 1/6, 4/35, 5/18; word problems 5/36, 5/12, 9/14 — correct).
- 1.8 Fractions/decimals (pp. 23–24): method restated; no answer key given for Exercises 8–9 or Activities 9–10.
- Unit Revision Exercise key (p. 25): ordering, addition, subtraction, division, numbers in words/figures — all correct; true/false items mis-numbered and one wrong (see Slips).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 26–37, PDF pp. 32–43)
- Unit opener (pp. 26–27): "Learn about" and outcomes — **promises litres↔millilitres conversion** ("Convert millilitres to litres and vice-versa") which the student book never teaches; the guide gives no litre material either.
- 2.1 m↔km (pp. 28–29): notes and real-life measuring suggestions.
- 2.2 Area (p. 30): brief guidance, refers to student book pp. 30–32. No answer key for Exercise 2 (area problems).
- 2.3 Volume (pp. 31–32): model-making advice; Exercise 3 key (PDF p. 38): 600, 729, 120, 420, 90, 512, 1,080, 6,250 cm³; 480,000 cm³; 22.5 m³; 1,512 cm³; 1,800 m³; 531.441 m³; 160,000 cm³; 166.375 m³. All check out against the student book's dimension triples except item 5 (90 cm³), whose figure dimensions are not recoverable from the text layer — see note in filing.
- 2.4 Time (pp. 33–35): notes; Exercise 4 key (PDF p. 41) fully correct in every part (hours→minutes, minutes→hours, to seconds, seconds→minutes, minutes-and-seconds; 4¾ h = 285 min; 7 × 45 min = 5 h 15 min).
- 2.5 Temperature (p. 36): one short paragraph; no answer key for Exercise 5 (the student book's context-free temperature conversions).
- 2.6 Money (pp. 36–37): profit/loss guidance; answers labelled "Exercise 3" (the student book's money exercise is Exercise 5) — two of the five answers are wrong (see Slips).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 38–45, PDF pp. 44–51)
- Unit opener (pp. 38–39): "Learn about" promises angle types (acute, obtuse, reflex), scale drawing, grid coordinates and vertical/horizontal/diagonal mirror lines — **none of these are taught in the student book** (which covers only parallel lines, 60°/30°/90°/45° construction and line symmetry). Outcomes likewise promise linear scales and angle types the student book lacks.
- 3.1 Parallel lines (pp. 40–41): definitions (parallel = never meet; perpendicular = meet at a right angle — correct here, unlike the student book's p. 46 definition); construction guidance.
- 3.2 Construct angles (p. 42): "construct a 600, 300 and 900 angles" (degree symbol lost in the text layer); no 45° mention, no key for Exercise 1.
- 3.3 Line of symmetry (pp. 43–45): keyword list, learning outcomes (incl. "orders of rotational symmetry" — rotational symmetry is never taught in the student book), a long generic introduction on folding-paper symmetry and group organisation; no answer key for the symmetry exercise (letters A, H, T, X).

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 46–55, PDF pp. 52–61)
- Unit opener (p. 46): learn-about, inquiry questions, outcomes.
- 4.1 Solving equations (pp. 47–50): recall example worked as "x + 2 = 6 … x = 6 − 4 therefore x is 4" (see Slips — the subtraction shown is wrong though the answer is right); addition/subtraction/multiplication/division problem types; Activity 1 key (p. 50) matches the student book's Activity 5 (y=1, y=4, x=0, x=5, x=−3 — correct) but is labelled "Activity 1"; the "Activity 2" key that follows matches no activity in the student book (see Slips).
- Keyword table (pp. 51–52): the student book's pp. 65–66 table reproduced in full.
- 4.2 Forming equations (pp. 53–55): fresh example (Musa's 6 children, girls twice boys → 2 boys); Exercise 1 key (p. 50): 20 items, 18 correct, 2 sign errors (see Slips); Exercise 2 key (p. 55): Q3, Q4, Q5, Q6, Q7 correct; Q1, Q2 and Q8 wrong or meaningless (see Slips).

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 56–70, PDF pp. 62–76)
- Unit opener (p. 56): learn-about, inquiry questions, outcomes.
- 5.1 Data collection (pp. 57–58): definitions, methods (observation/interview/questionnaire/experiment), questionnaire-writing advice.
- 5.2 Representation (pp. 59–70): bar-graph "dos", tally tables, lesson focus; Exercise 2 key (pp. 62–63) — inherits the student book's wrong frequency table (45 learners instead of 42) and is otherwise correct; Exercise 3 line-graph key (p. 66) — values are read off graph images and cannot be verified from the text layer (see note); pie-chart section (pp. 66–68) with angle formulas; Exercise 4 key (pp. 68–69): pie angles coffee 60°, grass 45°, maize 105°, homestead 7.5°, vegetables 67.5° (all correct) but the banana angle (75°) is omitted; percentages shown (17/12/29/21/2/19%) are correct roundings; bar/line graph images follow with no further text. The guide ends abruptly after the Exercise 4 graphs — no closing section.

### Companion check (guide vs its student book)
- **Same book, same order:** yes — the guide follows the student book's five units and sub-topics in the same order, with the same numbering (1.1–1.8, 2.1–2.6, 3.1–3.3, 4.1–4.2, 5.1–5.2). Cross-references are usually to the right student-book pages, with the exceptions listed under Slips.
- **Guide covers things the student book lacks:** Unit 2 outcomes promise litres↔millilitres (never taught in either book); Unit 3 "Learn about"/outcomes promise acute/obtuse/reflex angles, scale drawing, grid coordinates, vertical/horizontal/diagonal mirror lines and rotational symmetry (none taught in the student book); Unit 1 outcomes promise simplifying fractions by cancelling (never taught). The guide never flags these as extras — a teacher following the guide would look for material that is not in the pupil's book.
- **Student book content the guide does not key:** no answers for Unit 1 Exercises 8–9 (decimals/fractions), Unit 2 Exercise 2 (area), Unit 2 Exercise 5 (temperature), Unit 3 Exercise 1 (constructions/symmetry), Unit 5 Exercises 1 (data collection) and 4 Q2–Q4 graphs (only the pie angles are keyed). The guide also gives no correction of any of the student book's slips — it repeats the wrong frequency table (p. 62), repeats the wrong loss figure implicitly by not reworking Example 10, and repeats the "tulips" question without comment.
- **Guide-only errors:** several (see Slips). The guide's keys are mostly accurate where they exist, but the Exercise 2 (divisibility) key, the money key, two algebra sign errors and the Exercise 2 Q1/Q8 answers are wrong.

### What the guide does NOT teach / lacks
- No scheme of work, no lesson plans with timings, no weekly pacing — only per-topic teaching notes and keys.
- No answers for the exercises listed above; no diagnostic or remedial guidance; no guidance for the student book's image-only page (printed p. 37 volume figures) beyond "use the notes … page 33–37".
- No treatment of the student book's errors (see companion check).

### Held-copy defects
- PDF p. 6 is a genuinely blank page (between contents and Introduction) — a printing artefact, not missing content. PDF p. 4 (foreword) has no text layer (scanned image) — read visually; the text matches the student book's foreword adapted for the guide. No missing pages otherwise (printed pp. 1–70 run continuously).

### Slips and wrong answers in the guide itself (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 14 (PDF p. 20–21), Exercise 2 key:** "3. B. 6915 C. 6921" — the student book's Q3 asks about 237, 833, 6,488 and 3,528; 6,915/6,921 appear nowhere in the question. And "5. D. 107" answers nothing in the student book's Q5 (divisibility by 4 of 4,137,156 etc.). The key is misaligned from Q3 onward; only the Q4 sub-answers (divisible by 3: 237, 3,528; by 4: 6,488, 3,528; by 6: 3,528; by 9: 3,528) are correct.
2. **Printed p. 25, Unit Revision key, true/false:** the key labels these "7. a) true b) false c) true d) true" but they answer the student book's Q8, and **d) is wrong: 429 is NOT divisible by 6** (429 is odd; 4+2+9=15 is divisible by 3 only). Correct: a) true (981÷9), b) false (28÷9), c) true (3,716÷4), d) false.
3. **Printed p. 37 (PDF p. 43), money key ("Exercise 3"):** Q1 "SSP 23,730" is wrong — the TV cost SSP 18,950 and was sold at a loss of SSP 4,780, so the selling price is **SSP 14,170**; the guide added instead of subtracting. Q4 "SSP 10.20" is wrong — total cost SSP 5,200 + SSP 1,000 profit = SSP 6,200 over 600 apples = **SSP 10.33… per apple** (10.20 × 600 = 6,120, a profit of only SSP 920). Q2 (SSP 50 profit), Q3 (SSP 145 gain), Q5 (SSP 725) are correct.
4. **Printed p. 50, algebra Exercise 1 key:** two sign errors — Q7 "n = 108" should be **n = −108** (−9 = n/12); Q19 "p = 240" should be **p = −240** (p/20 = −12). The other 18 answers are correct.
5. **Printed p. 55, algebra Exercise 2 key:** Q1 "24 m" is wrong/meaningless — the classroom problem (length 8 m more than width, area 80 m²) has no whole-number solution; 24 m corresponds to nothing in it. Q8 "a) 4a + l = 10" is wrong — with perimeter 4a and width 20 cm, the length is **2a − 20** (from 2L + 40 = 4a), and part (b), the area expression, is not answered at all.
6. **Printed p. 50, "Activity 2" key:** the answers given ("1. i) x=2 ii) x=6 iii) y=4 iv) x=2 v) x=7; 2. i) x=−2 ii) x=2 iii) y=5 iv) x=1 v) y=5") match no activity in the student book. The student book's Activity 2 (p. 61) is m−4=−13 (m=−9), −14=x−18 (x=4), −13−p=−19 (p=6); its Activity 1 (p. 60) is b=11, k=6, n=2. The guide's key appears to belong to a different book/edition.
7. **Printed p. 22 (PDF p. 27), fractions worked example:** "Step 1: find the LCM. The LCM of 3 and 5 is 15" — the example is 1/3 + 1/6, so the LCM needed is of **3 and 6 (= 6)**; 5 appears from nowhere.
8. **Printed p. 47 (PDF p. 53), algebra recall:** "x + 2 = 6 … x = 6 − 4 therefore the value of x is 4" — the subtraction shown (6−4) is wrong; it should be 6−2=4. Answer right, working wrong.
9. **Printed p. 29 (PDF p. 35), km/m:** "A kilometer equals to one thousand meters which is 0.001 km" — garbled; 1 m = 0.001 km, 1 km = 1,000 m.
10. **Printed p. 5 (TOC) and p. 11:** "1.2 divisibility tests of numbers 3, 4, 6 and 8" — should be **9**, not 8.
11. **Wrong cross-references:** p. 19 "Use activity 10 of page 16" for factors (Activity 10 is on student p. 25, fractions→decimals; the factors activity is Activity 8, p. 18); p. 24 "give learners exercise 7" after fractions→decimals (should be Exercise 9; Exercise 7 is fraction subtraction); p. 40 "See examples at learner's book page 45" for parallel lines (student p. 45 is the money exercise; parallel lines are pp. 46–49); p. 50 "refer learner's book example 8 and 9" for division problems (student Examples 8–9 are Roman numerals; the division examples are 7–8); p. 55 "Assign activity 3" in section 4.2 (Activity 3 is a 4.1 division activity; the 4.2 activity is Activity 7); p. 68 "Assign exercise 3" for pie charts (should be Exercise 4); p. 61 "examples 1, 2, 3 and 4 (67–84)" — the student book's statistics examples are on printed pp. 74–88.
12. **Printed p. 62, Exercise 2 key:** inherits the student book's wrong frequency table — "e) 45 learners" should be **42 learners** (the table's frequencies sum to 45; the raw list has 42 marks, with 50 occurring 10 times and 35 four times, not 8 and 9).
13. **Printed p. 68, Exercise 4 key:** the banana sector angle (5/24 × 360 = **75°**) is omitted from the list of pie-chart angles.
14. **Printed p. 66, Exercise 3 key, Q1c "16kgs":** unverifiable from the text layer (Sam's weights run 49–73 kg, a range of 24 kg); the value appears to be read off the graph image and I could not confirm what "range of the vertical scale" means here. Flagged, not asserted as wrong.
15. Minor: p. 20 "lowest common multiple" for least common multiple; p. 18 factors of 18 listed without 1 and 18; p. 26 "divisibly" for divisibly; p. 11 "divisibility tests of numbers 3, 4, 6 and 8" (see 10).

## Grade 6, student — Pupil's Book 6 (© 2018; 91 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 4)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 20 passed, 2 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 6, Pupil's Book (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g06-student.pdf` — 91 PDF pages, 2,834,956 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 91 PDF pages, in order, from the extracted text layer plus visual reads of the two low-text pages (PDF p. 44, printed p. 40 — photo/diagram page; PDF p. 74, printed p. 70 — blank). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 4 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–4 are title/imprint, foreword, contents pp. i–ii; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 5). The last printed page is 87 (PDF p. 91). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Cover check:** the title page (PDF p. 1) says "Mathematics Pupil's Book 6" / "PRIMARY 6" and the contents describe the Primary 6 syllabus. Primary 6 confirmed.
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" (title page, PDF p. 1). The foreword (PDF p. 2, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) is the same series foreword as Primary 5: approved for use in all schools except international schools with effect from 4 February 2019; Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers (Kenya); Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation (UK). **No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book. No individual author is named anywhere** — authorship is given only as the Ministry. This matches the pattern reported for the English books and for Primary 5 maths.

Worked-example arithmetic I checked by hand/calculator where a slip was suspected; those checks are noted under Slips. Exercise items were read in full. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the book teaches, unit by unit

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS — printed pp. 1–17 (PDF pp. 5–21)
- **1.1 Read, write, compare and order numbers to a million (pp. 1–4):** place value in groups (millions/thousands/ones); reading large numbers by group name (worked: 520,246,978; 355,645; 16,006,006); Activities 1–2 and Exercises 1–2 (write numbers in words/figures; order lists; compare Unity 645,465 vs Eastern Equatoria 962,716 populations; compare Upper Nile vs Jonglei areas — see Slips, the units are mixed up).
- **1.2 Divisibility tests of 8 and 11 (pp. 5–7):** test of 8 = last three digits divisible by 8 (examples 723,810; 456,791,824; 923,780); test of 11 = alternating digit sum divisible by 11 (examples 280,819; 53; 1,364; 913; 3,729; 987); Activities 3–4 and Exercise 3.
- **1.3 Squares and square roots (pp. 7–9):** square = number × itself (3² = 9; 4² = 16); square root as the reverse (√9 = 3); Activities 5–6 and Exercise 4 (squares to 32²; roots incl. √169, √289, √400, √196, √144).
- **1.4 Decimals, fractions and percentage conversions (pp. 9–15):** decimal→percent (×100; 0.125 = 12.5%); percent→decimal (÷100; 55% = 0.55); fraction→percent (÷ then ×100; 3/8 = 37.5%); percent→fraction (÷100 then simplify; 60% = 3/5); fraction→decimal (divide numerator by denominator; 1/3 given as 0.3 — see Slips); decimal→fraction (0.55 = 11/20); Activities 7–12 and Exercises 5–10. Exercise 7 Q2: 17/20 of homeless children are boys → 85%; Q3: "5/2 of the number of beds … are occupied" (see Slips — impossible).
- **1.5 Ratios and proportion (pp. 15–17):** ratio as comparison, simplified (3 girls : 6 boys = 1:2); proportion as equal ratios (unitary method: 2 learners → 10 books, so 5 learners → 25 books; ½-litre bottles into 2 litres = 4 — question garbled, see Slips); Activities 13–15 and Exercises 11–12 (simplify ratios incl. 72:16, 64:12; learners:teachers 410:10; cost of 5 kg wheat SSP 180 → 12 kg; car 180 km on 5 L → 252 km; boat 365 km in 5 h → 454 km).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT — printed pp. 18–34 (PDF pp. 22–38)
- **2.1 Millimetres as units of length (pp. 18–20):** mm/cm/m, 10 mm = 1 cm, 1 m = 1,000 mm; worked 5,810 mm = 5.810 m; Activities 1–2 and Exercises 1–2 (convert cm↔mm, m↔cm, km↔m; car park front 2,400 cm in metres; string 1.4 m in cm).
- **2.2 Parts of a circle (pp. 21–22):** circle, centre, radius (OA), diameter (d = 2r); Exercise 3 (identify radius/diameter in six diagrams — diagrams are images, values not in text).
- **2.3 Calculate the value of π (pp. 23–25):** π = circumference ÷ diameter = 3.141592654…; C = πd = 2πr; area = πr²; worked: circle diameter 100 m → C = 314 m; area with r = 14 → 616 cm² (see Slips — the r = 14 appears from nowhere); area r = 7 cm → 154 cm²; circumference 88 cm → r = 14 cm (working shows "288 × 7" — see Slips); Activity 5 (measure plates, compute π). π is used as 22/7 or 3.14.
- **2.4 Units of area in acres and hectares (pp. 26–27):** acres × 0.405 = hectares (20 acres = 8.1 ha); hectares × 2.471 = acres (40 ha = 98.84 acres); Activities 6–7 (same nine numbers both ways: 164, 634, 46, 363, 349, 67, 797, 946, 82).
- **2.5 Area of triangles (pp. 27–28):** area = ½bh; worked right triangle 6 × 9 → 27 cm²; Exercise 4 (areas from diagrams — dimensions partly in text: 9 m, 25 m, 25 m, 7 m).
- **2.6 Capacity (pp. 29–30):** litre as standard unit; 1 ml = 0.001 L, 1 cl = 0.01 L, 1 dl = 0.1 L, 1 kl = 1,000 L; worked: 20 × 250 ml cans = 5,000 ml = 5 L; Activity 8 (estimate/measure container capacities); Exercise 5 (10 L in ml; 4,000 ml in L; 7.4 L in ml).
- **2.7 Tonnes ↔ kilograms ↔ grams (pp. 30–32):** 1 t = 1,000 kg; 1 kg = 1,000 g; 1 g = 0.001 kg; worked conversions of 5 t, 5 kg, 5 g (see Slips — one conversion line is garbled); Exercise 6 (paperclips 8,000 × 1 g; 5 kg in g; 9,000 g in kg; 20 g in mg; mango 0.8 kg in g; 4 bags totalling 6 kg).
- **2.8 Profit and loss (pp. 33–34):** profit = SP − CP; loss = CP − SP; worked bicycle SSP 3,390 → SSP 3,820, profit SSP 430; Exercise 7 (write your own profit and loss word problems and swap with another group — no set problems, no key possible).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY — printed pp. 35–61 (PDF pp. 39–65)
- **3.1 Constructing and bisecting lines (pp. 35–38):** "bisect = divide into two equal parts"; the steps given are actually for bisecting an ANGLE (vertex, arc through P and Q, intersecting arcs, line through vertex and X) under a line-bisection heading (see Slips); Activity 1 (bisect paper/sticks); Activity 2 (construct 60°); Exercise 1 (draw any angle and bisect it).
- **3.2 Vertically opposite and supplementary angles (pp. 38–42):** vertically opposite angles equal (worked: b = 40°, a = c = 140°); paper-cutting activity to verify; real-life photos (scissors, crossed planks — see Slips for the caption); supplementary angles sum to 180° (140° + 40°; 60° + 120°); Exercise 1 (find angles 1–4 in four diagrams — values are images: 45°, 83°, 157°, 82°).
- **3.3 Constructing a circle of a given radius (pp. 43–45):** compass use; worked circle radius 4 cm; Activity 4 (circles radius 5 cm, diameter 12 cm, radius 4.5 cm with diameter drawn/measured; radii 2.3/6.0/5.4 cm; diameters 10/13/19 cm — list lettered a, b, d with no c); Exercise 2 (circles diameter 3 cm / 8 cm, radius 5 cm / 25 mm).
- **3.4 Making patterns with circles (pp. 45–47):** spiral from semicircles in 4 steps; Activity 5 (draw the spiral); Exercise 3 (circle pattern with different radii).
- **3.5 Properties of 3D shapes (pp. 47–48):** cube — 6 square faces, 8 vertices, 12 edges; faces/edges/vertices defined; cuboid introduced (faces to count).
- **3.6 Making cubes and cuboids (pp. 48–55):** cube net, cut/fold/glue in 5 steps with safety note on blades; Activity 6 (make the net object — a cube); cuboid net, same 5 steps; Activity 7 (make the cuboid). The nets themselves are images.
- **3.7 Conversion of length (pp. 56–59):** mm/cm/m/km with everyday references (credit card, fingernail, guitar, football field); worked 298 cm = 2.98 m and back; Exercise 3 (cm→m: 9,200; 4,620; 6,426; 2,130; 7,718; 976; 3,580; 5,800; and "25.3 m" — see Slips; m→cm: 83.6; 17.45; 79.21; 28.64; 87.9; 3.49; 3).
- **3.8 Writing scale in ratio form (pp. 59–60):** scale as fraction or ratio (1 cm : 1 m = 1:100).
- **3.9 Making scale drawings (pp. 60–61):** scale = drawing length ÷ object length (D/O); worked example header with no worked numbers in text; Exercise 4 (find the scale of two drawings — images; man 2 cm in a photo, actual height 1.8 m → scale 1:90).

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA — printed pp. 62–70 (PDF pp. 66–74)
- **4.1 Solving equations (pp. 62–63):** two-step equations by inverse operations (4w + 2 = 18 → w = 4; 4/5 q − 2 = 6 → q = 10, with fraction cleared by ×5); "if any sign goes to the other side it becomes opposite" rule; Activities 1–2 (3z − 4 = 5; 7p + 3 = 17; 9y − 8 = 19; 5y − 17 = 18; 6e + 7 = 31; 22f + 2 = 46; fractional-coefficient equations).
- **4.2 Simplify algebraic equations (pp. 64–70):** collecting like terms (5y + 2y + 14 = 7y + 14); expanding brackets (3x(y + 1 + 2y) = 9xy + 3x; 4(y+4) + 2(y−6) − 4z = 6y + 4 − 4z); Activities 3–4; Exercise 1 (12 items mixing solving and simplifying: 2z + 4 = 10; 12 + 2q = 8q; 25q + 10p − 15q + 7p; 6p + 2 − 3p = 20; 17p + 5 − 2p − 3 = 32); Example 5 (phrases → expressions: "nine increased by a number x" → 9 + x; "seven less than a number t" → t − 7); Activity 5 (15 fractional/rational items — badly garbled in the text layer, e.g. item 7 is unreadable; the equations are recoverable only in part); Example 6 (three consecutive numbers summing to 216 → 71, 72, 73, fully worked with check); Example 7 (rectangle area 72 cm², width twice length → 6 cm × 12 cm, with a perimeter check); Exercise 2 (six equations incl. 4(3y−5) − 7y = −60; 19 = 9 + 2(x−7); plus seven word problems — ages, buses (472 learners, 4 in cars, 9 buses → 52 per bus), erasers (SSP 180 total, magazine SSP 100 → SSP 20 each)). Printed p. 70 is blank; Unit 4 ends at printed p. 69.

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS — printed pp. 71–87 (PDF pp. 75–91)
- **5.1 Reading and interpreting data from tables (pp. 71–73):** data as information; reading tables (bicycle table — Ranger/Outdoor/Tourer/Starburst/Mountain with colours, gears and prices in £); tally marks and frequency tables (bikes sold: Ranger 3, Outdoor 5, Tourer 0, Starburst 2, Mountain 10; total 20).
- **5.2 x and y axes, scale and co-ordinates (pp. 73–87):** axes, origin (0,0), ordered pairs (2,5); bar charts (from the bike frequency table); pictograms (key: one wheel picture = 2 bikes); Example 2 (insect picture graph — beetle; ants/crickets); Example 3 (monkeys/seals picture graphs — fewer on Wednesday, more on Tuesday, same Thursday and Friday); pie charts (circle = 100%; Nick's spending — savings about 50%); Exercise 1 (cake sales pie chart: apple 40, cupcake 20, chocolate 10, lemon 15, vanilla 15; match results pie chart: tie 10, B win 35, no result 20, A win 25); line graphs (hot drink cooling — 40 °C after 20 minutes); Activity 1 (market data in four graph types); Example 2 (bar graph "score on a basic math test" — 6 students scored 80, 4 scored 60); Exercise 2 (class grades pie graph; worm lengths line graph); Activity 2 (car colours data); Exercise 3 (pictograph of pictures drawn — key = 2 pictures; class election pie chart — Nunu 29%, Deng 21%, Kiden 12%, Peter 18%, Akuol 11%, Taban's share not in the text layer). The book ends after Exercise 3 Q2 — no revision, no summary, no answers section.

### What the book does NOT teach (honest negatives, at Primary 6 level)
- No HCF/LCM, no prime numbers, no Roman numerals (all left in Primary 5); no multiplication/division of fractions; no negative-number arithmetic as a topic (negatives appear only inside algebra answers); no percentages of quantities (only conversions between forms); no probability.
- Measurement: no area of circles applied beyond two worked examples (no circumference problems set); no volume in this book at all (cubes/cuboids appear only as nets to build, not volumes); no time, no temperature, no money beyond one profit/loss example and one exercise that asks pupils to invent their own problems.
- Geometry: no triangle construction, no angle measurement with a protractor (angles are constructed or computed, never measured), no coordinates beyond plotting (2,5) — no plotting exercises set; no nets given as exercises with dimensions.
- Algebra: no equations with the unknown on both sides taught as a method (Exercise 1 Q4, 12 + 2q = 8q, assumes it); no simultaneous equations; no substitution/evaluation of expressions.
- Statistics: no mean/mode/median/range; no tallying taught as a pupil exercise (only explained); no data-collection exercise with real numbers set as a marked task; pie-chart angles (quantity/total × 360°) are not taught in this book (only "the circle represents 100%").
- No answers section anywhere; no unit revision exercises in any unit.

### Held-copy defects
- Printed p. 70 is a genuinely blank page (PDF p. 74) between Units 4 and 5 — a printing artefact, not missing content. Otherwise no missing pages (printed pp. 1–87 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–91 complete), no blurred pages. The degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer in places ("600" for 60°) — an extraction artefact, not a print error. Several exercise diagrams are images only (circle radius/diameter items, angle diagrams, scale drawings, all statistics graphs) so their values are not in the text layer; I read what the text layer gives and noted where values live only in images.

### Slips and wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 5, divisibility of 8, Example 2a:** the worked check for 723,810 suddenly says "Take a look at the last two digits: 723 810. Does 4 divide evenly into 10? No." — it tests divisibility by 4 on the wrong digits (10) inside a divisibility-by-8 example. (The conclusion — not divisible by 8 — is right, since 810 ÷ 8 = 101 r 2, but the working shown is nonsense.)
2. **Printed p. 4, Exercise 2:** "The size of Upper Nile is 77 283 square metres while Jonglei is 122 580 square miles. Which state has a smaller area?" — the two areas are in different units and neither unit is right: the Primary 5 book's own table gives these as 77,283.42 and 122,580.83 (square kilometres). The question as printed cannot be answered.
3. **Printed p. 12, Exercise 7 Q3:** "5/2 of the number of beds in a hospital are occupied by patients. What is the percentage of beds occupied?" — 5/2 of the beds (250%) is impossible; the fraction should be less than 1.
4. **Printed p. 13, fraction→decimal, Example 10:** "Convert 1/3 to a decimal. 1 ÷ 3 = 0.3. Therefore 1/3 as a decimal is 0.3" — 1/3 = 0.333…, not 0.3; the book never marks it as recurring or approximate.
5. **Printed p. 23, Activity 4:** after computing a 100 m-diameter circle's circumference (314 m), the text continues "Now, substituting 14 in r in the formula for area of circle, πr²" and computes 616 cm² — the radius 14 comes from nowhere (a 100 m circle has r = 50 m) and the units switch to cm² without explanation.
6. **Printed p. 25, Example 2 Q2:** "r = (288 × 7)/44 = 14 cm" — the 288 is a typo for 88 (2πr = 88 → r = 88×7/44 = 14). The answer is right; the working shows the wrong number.
7. **Printed p. 31, kg→tonnes:** "1 gram (kg) is equal to 1000000 tons (t). 1 kg = (1/1000) t = 0.001 t" — the first sentence is garbled/wrong (1 kg is not 1,000,000 tons); the formula beside it is correct.
8. **Printed p. 35–37, section 3.1:** headed "Constructing and bisecting lines" but the steps given bisect an ANGLE (angle ABC, vertex, arcs through P and Q); no line-bisection (perpendicular bisector) construction is actually given.
9. **Printed p. 40, real-life vertical angles (visual page):** the caption under both pictures reads "a, b, c, and d are vertical angles" — a, b, c, d are the four angles around the crossing; only the opposite pairs (a with c, b with d) are vertically opposite each other.
10. **Printed p. 17, Activity 15 Q3:** "One pen weighs 2kg" — a pen weighs grams, not 2 kg; the numbers work (8 pens = 16 kg) but the premise is absurd.
11. **Printed p. 59, Exercise 3 Q1i:** "25.3 m" listed under "Centimetres to metres" — the item should be in centimetres.
12. **Printed p. 64, Example 3 Q1:** "Solve 5y + 2y + 14" — this is an expression, not an equation; "solve" is the wrong verb (the book simplifies it to 7y + 14).
13. **Printed p. 3, Example 1:** "16 006 006 is read sixteen millions, six thousand, six" — "sixteen millions" should be "sixteen million".
14. **Printed p. 69, Exercise 2 word problem (d):** "The product of 5 and x decreased by 7 is as much as 42" gives x = 9.8 — a non-integer answer in a Primary 6 exercise with no comment.
15. Minor: p. 20 Exercise 2 Q3 is cut off mid-sentence in the text layer ("How long is this in centimetres/" — the question continues onto the next page heading; content is not lost, it is a layout break). The bicycle table (p. 72) prices bikes in £ (pounds sterling) — a borrowed example, out of place in a South Sudan book. Activity 5 (p. 66) items 7–15 are badly garbled in the text layer and only partly recoverable; the printed page itself may be a typeset mess (fractions stacked without clear operators).

## Grade 6, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 6 (© 2018; 80 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 4)

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 6, Teacher's Guide (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g06-teacher.pdf` — 80 PDF pages, 4,705,745 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 80 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full; the one text-empty page (PDF p. 68, printed p. 64) was read visually — it is a montage of four reduced student-book pages (see below). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 4 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–4 are title/imprint, foreword, contents; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 5). The last printed page is 76 (PDF p. 80). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" (title page, PDF p. 1). The foreword (PDF p. 2, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) is the series foreword adapted to the Teacher's Guide, same approval date 4 February 2019, same credits (Longhorn and Mountain Top, Kenya; Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation UK). **An ISBN is NOT printed. No individual author is named anywhere** — authorship is given only as the Ministry. This matches the student book and the Primary 5 books.
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer plus a visual read of PDF p. 68. Every answer in the guide's keys was re-checked by hand/calculator against the student book's questions; the results are under Companion check and Slips. Where a key reads values off a graph/diagram image, I say so and do not claim verification. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the guide contains, unit by unit

#### Front matter — Introduction (printed pp. 1–5, PDF pp. 5–9)
- Introduction (pp. 1–2): five units, performance objectives, "teach and practice" approach; unit overview table (Unit 1 Numbers: Operations (2); Unit 2 Measurement: weight and mass; Unit 3 Geometry: geometric constructions; Unit 4 Algebra: algebraic expressions (1); Unit 5 Statistics: reading statistical graphs). Claims "the answers [to the learner's book exercises] are provided in this teachers guide" — this is not fully true (see Companion check).
- Teaching strategies (pp. 2–4): problem-based learning, open-ended questions (same rectangle-perimeter-160 cm example as the Primary 5 guide), group work, peer teaching.
- Classroom assessment (p. 5): observation / conversation / product ("triangulation").
- TOC errors: "2.7 Conversion of tones to kilograms" (should be tonnes); sections 3.5 and 3.6 are both listed as "Making cubes and cuboids" (the student book's 3.5 is "Properties of 3D shapes").

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 6–25, PDF pp. 10–29)
- Unit opener (pp. 6–8): "Learn about", key inquiry questions, learning outcomes (incl. "Squares and square roots of perfect squares up to 3 digits" and "recurring decimals" — the student book teaches 1/3 = 0.3 with no mention of recurring decimals), competencies, links.
- 1.1 Numbers to a million (pp. 8–13): keyword definitions (digit, interval, place value…; "Tens: twin values" is a typo for "two-digit values"); frequently asked questions; common errors (writing 5,060 as 560; 4,624 as 400,060,024); evaluation guide; place-value table worked for 1,940,613; extension place-value items (21,816,835 etc., no key given); Exercise 1 key (p. 13): 5,821,456; 1,235,847; 6,540,674; 7,821,365 — all correct. Exercise 2 key: "1. Eastern Equitoria 2. Upper Nile" — numerically right, but repeats the student book's mixed-units problem without flagging it (see Slips).
- 1.2 Divisibility of 8 and 11 (pp. 14–15): rules restated with fresh examples (8,120 for 8; 2,728 for 11 — "since −11 is divisible by 1 so is 2,728", see Slips); Exercise 3 key (p. 15): Q1 "31415" (correct — 31,415 is the one not divisible by 11); Q2 "760672" (incomplete — 65,432 is also divisible by 8, see Slips); Q3 correct (3,624, 2,728, 7,120 divisible by 8; 2,728 and 28,182 divisible by 11).
- 1.3 Squares and square roots (pp. 16–19): objectives ("squares of whole numbers more than 50", "square roots of perfect squares greater than 400" — beyond what the student book sets); keyword definitions; starter activities; Exercise 4 key (p. 19): 81, 121, 625, 36; 2, 14, 12, 10 — all correct.
- 1.4 Decimals, fractions, percentages (pp. 19–22): keyword definitions; conversion methods restated (0.05 × 100 = 5%; 11% = 0.11); only Exercise 10 is keyed (p. 22): 0.56 = 14/25, 0.26 = 13/50, 0.25 = ¼, 0.45 = 9/20 — all correct. Exercises 5–9 (decimals→%, %→decimals, fractions→%, %→fractions, fractions→decimals) have no key; the student book's impossible "5/2 of the beds" item and its "1/3 = 0.3" are not corrected.
- 1.5 Ratios and proportion (pp. 23–25): keyword definitions ("Truncated", "Scaled down/up" listed though never used); ratio/proportion lesson focuses (2 buses : 4 cars = 1:2); Exercise 12 key (p. 25): 144 bags of rice; 6 learners passed; 7:8, 1:5, 3:7, 1:3, 7:12, 1:5 — all correct. Exercise 11 (simplify ratios) and Activity 15 have no key.

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 26–42, PDF pp. 30–46)
- Unit opener (pp. 26–28): "Learn about" (incl. "contrast volume and capacity" and "convert litres and millilitres") and outcomes (incl. "Find areas in units of acres and hectors" [sic], "Solve problems involving length, mass, volume and capacity"); introduction list.
- 2.1 Millimetres (pp. 29–30): starter/lesson focus; Exercise 1 key (p. 31): 30, 120, 2,850 mm; 600, 240, 70 cm; 4, 25, 40 cm; 5, 7.5, 0.2 m — all correct. Exercise 2 key: 80 mm, 600 cm, 7,000 m, 24 m, 140 cm — all correct.
- 2.2 Parts of a circle (pp. 31–32): outdoor demonstration (draw a circle, divide into two, then four); refers to "exercise 2" (the student book's radius/diameter exercise is Exercise 3 — wrong label); Exercise 3 key (p. 33): letter-pair answers (RG diameter / OF radius, etc.) — these read labels off the student book's diagrams and cannot be verified from the text layer.
- 2.3 Value of π (pp. 33–34): measuring plates with string; "Refer to activity 4 (page 22)" then "Guide them using activity 4 (page 21)" — inconsistent references (student Activity 4 is on printed p. 23); formulas C = 2πr, A = πr²; the student book's unexplained "substituting 14 in r" jump is not addressed.
- 2.4 Acres/hectares (p. 35): formulas restated (ha × 2.471 = acres; acres × 0.405 = hectares); "remind themselves on what they learnt at primary 5 about hectares" — Primary 5 teaches no hectares. No key for Activities 6–7.
- 2.5 Area of triangles (pp. 36–37): right triangle as half a rectangle; Exercise 4 key: a) 112.5 m² (checks out: ½ × 25 × 9), b) 84 m² (cannot be verified from the text layer — the diagram's dimensions are images).
- 2.6 Capacity (pp. 37–38): container-matching activity; Exercise 5 key (p. 38): 10,000 ml; 4 L; 7,400 ml — all correct. The matching activity itself has a slip: a "3 250 ml" container is paired with a "3.2ℓ" flash card (3,250 ml = 3.25ℓ, see Slips).
- 2.7 Tonnes/kg/grams (pp. 39–41): headed "Conversion of tones to kilograms" throughout ("tone" for tonne); Exercise 6 key (p. 41): 8 kg; 5,000 g; 9 kg; 20,000 mg; 800 g; 1,500 g — all correct. The student book's garbled "1 gram (kg) is equal to 1000000 tons" line is not corrected.
- 2.8 Profit and loss (pp. 41–42): brief guidance; Exercise 7 "key" is just "Learners should come up with the questions and try to solve them" (the student book's exercise asks pupils to invent problems, so no key is possible).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 43–59, PDF pp. 47–63)
- Unit opener (pp. 43–44): "Learn about" promises protractor use ("use a protractor to measure angles") and translation on grids — **neither is taught in the student book** (angles are constructed/computed, never measured; no translation). Outcomes list matches the student book otherwise.
- 3.1 Bisecting lines and angles (pp. 45–48): line bisection via cutting an orange; angle bisector defined (ray BD bisects angle ABC, ABD = DBC); "You can give them specific angles for example 600 and let them bisect it" (60°). Unlike the student book, the guide does separate line bisection from angle bisection — but it gives no actual line-bisection construction steps either (it says "Use the notes in the learner's book", whose steps bisect an angle).
- 3.2 Vertically opposite and supplementary angles (pp. 48–50): starter diagram (angles A0/B0 — labels garbled in the text layer); "Vertical in this case means they share the same vertex"; full-circle 360° method; Exercise 1 key (p. 50): Q1 135°/45°/135°/45°; Q2 83°/97°/83°/97°; Q3 157°/23°/157°/23°; Q4 98°/98°/82°/82° — all correct against the student book's given angles (45°, 83°, 157°, 82°).
- 3.3 Circle of a given radius (pp. 51–52): outdoor then paper demonstration; Exercise 2 key is "Mark this according to the learners drawing" (no numeric key possible — it is a drawing exercise).
- 3.4 Patterns with circles (p. 53): practical lesson guidance; Exercise 3 to be assessed by the teacher.
- 3.5/3.6 Cubes and cuboids (pp. 54–55): cube facts (6 faces, 8 vertices, 12 edges); similarities/differences of cubes and cuboids (incl. "The formulas of finding both of their volumes are the same" — volume is not taught in either book at this grade); a 9-step cardstock cube construction; Activities 6–7 for practice.
- 3.7 Conversion of length (pp. 55–57): 1 cm = 10 mm, 1 m = 100 cm, 1 km = 1,000 m; Exercise 3 key (pp. 57–58): cm→m all correct (92, 46.2, 64.26, 21.3, 77.18, 9.76, 35.8, 58, 25.3 m); m→cm: 8,360, 1,745, 7,921, 2,864, 8,790, 349 correct — but **g) "3000cm" for 3 m is wrong (3 m = 300 cm)**, see Slips. The key also repeats the student book's "25.3 m" item under "Centimetres to metres" without noticing it.
- 3.8 Scale in ratio form (p. 58): 1 cm = 10 mm → 1/10 → 1:10.
- 3.9 Scale drawing (pp. 58–59): scale = drawing ÷ object; Exercise 4 key: "a) ½ b) 1/8" — the two drawings are images, so these cannot be verified from the text layer; the photo-scale question (2 cm photo, 1.8 m man → 1:90) is not answered.

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 60–65, PDF pp. 64–69)
- Unit opener (pp. 60–61): "Learn about" (incl. BODMAS and "3x + 6 = 2x" as an example equation); outcomes; introduction.
- 4.1 Solving equations (pp. 62–63): guidance to use the student book's examples; Activity 1 key (p. 63): 3, 2, 3, 7, 4, 2 — all correct. Activity 2 key: 6, 15, 14, 9 — all correct.
- Printed p. 64 (PDF p. 68) is a montage of four reduced student-book pages (printed pp. 66–69: Activity 5, Example 6, Example 7, Exercise 2) — read visually; the small print is not reliably legible at this size, so I do not claim to have verified the guide's Activity 5 key against it (see Slips, Exercise 1).
- 4.2 Simplifying (p. 65): Activity 3 key: 16y + 6; 4x + 18xy; x = −6 — all correct. Activity 4 key: 14y + 12x; 3y + 2xy + 4; 8x − 8 + xy; 10y − 10 — all correct. Exercise 1 key: badly wrong from Q5 onward (see Slips). Exercise 2 key: equations −8, 12, 4, 4, 2, 4 and word problems 4, −14, 10, 49/5, 78, 52, 20 — all correct.

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 66–76, PDF pp. 70–80)
- Unit opener (p. 66): "Learn about", inquiry questions, outcomes, objectives (incl. "Prepare a tally of data").
- 5.1 Tables (pp. 67–69): starter activities; tally/frequency tables; "Work through the example on page 61 of the learner's book" — the tally example is on student printed p. 73, not 61 (wrong reference).
- 5.2 Axes, scale, co-ordinates (pp. 70–76): axes/origin restated; pictograms (uses student pp. 76–77 examples); bar charts (worked class-ages example: ages 12–17 with counts 0, 10, 12, 5, 6 — note the table lists five counts for six ages, and the drawn chart's x-axis runs 12–16); pie charts; line graphs (construction steps a–d); "EXPECTED ANSWERS" (p. 76) covers only Exercise 2: Q1 a) "C's", b) "D's", c) "Around 90% (measure with a protractor)", d) "yes he is"; Q2 a) 6.75 cm, b) 9 cm, c) 4 worms, d) 3 worms. The Q1c "Around 90%" and Q1d "yes he is" look wrong/garbled (see Slips); the worm answers are read off the line-graph image and cannot be verified from the text layer. No keys are given for Exercise 1 (cake sales, match results) or Exercise 3 (pictograph, class election). The guide ends at printed p. 76 with these answers — no closing section.

### Companion check (guide vs its student book)
- **Same book, same order:** yes — five units in the same order with the same sub-topic numbering, except the guide's TOC duplicates "3.5/3.6 Making cubes and cuboids" where the student book has 3.5 Properties of 3D shapes / 3.6 Making cubes and cuboids.
- **Guide covers things the student book lacks:** Unit 3 promises protractor measurement and translation on grids; Unit 1 outcomes promise recurring decimals and squares/roots "more than 50 / greater than 400" beyond the student book's items; Unit 2 outcomes promise contrasting volume and capacity. None of this material is in the pupil's book.
- **Student book content the guide does not key:** Exercises 5–9 (Unit 1 conversions), Exercise 11 and Activity 15 (ratios/proportion), Activities 6–7 (acres/hectares), Exercise 1 (Unit 5 cake sales / match results), Exercise 3 (Unit 5 pictograph / election), the scale-drawing photo question, and the Unit 3 drawing exercises (assessed by eye). The guide's Introduction claim that exercise answers "are provided in this teachers guide" overstates what is actually keyed.
- **Guide-only errors:** several (see Slips), the worst being the algebra Exercise 1 key (Q2/Q10 swapped, Q5–Q9 nonsense answers to simplification items) and the 3 m = 3,000 cm conversion.
- **Guide does not correct the student book:** it repeats the mixed-units area question's answers without flagging the units, repeats the "25.3 m" item, and does not address the student book's 1/3 = 0.3, the "5/2 of the beds" item, the angle-bisection/line-bisection mix-up, or the garbled "1 gram (kg) = 1000000 tons" line.

### What the guide does NOT teach / lacks
- No scheme of work, no lesson plans with timings, no pacing.
- No keys for the exercises listed above; no diagnostic/remedial guidance.
- No answer key for the student book's image-only items beyond the letter-pair radius/diameter answers and the angle answers.

### Held-copy defects
- Printed p. 64 (PDF p. 68) is a reduced-size montage of four student-book pages — legible as a layout reference but its small print is not reliably readable; I read it visually and did not rely on it for any key. Otherwise no missing pages (printed pp. 1–76 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–80 complete), no blurred pages. The degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer in places ("600" for 60°, "3600" for 360°) — an extraction artefact, not a print error.

### Slips and wrong answers in the guide itself (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 65, Exercise 1 key (algebra):** Q2 "6" should be **3** (6y − 2 = 16 → 6y = 18 → y = 3); Q10 "3" should be **6** (6p + 2 − 3p = 20 → 3p = 18 → p = 6) — the two answers appear swapped. Q5–Q9 are not equations at all in the student book (they are simplifications: 5z + 6 − 3z; 10y − 4 + 5y; 15z − 6 + 3z + 4; 25q + 10p − 15q + 7p; 19r + 14 − 12r) and the guide's answers ("-3", "4/15", "1/9", "q = −17/10 q", "-2") match nothing; the correct simplifications are 2z + 6, 15y − 4, 18z − 2, 10q + 17p, 7r + 14. Q1 (3), Q3 (6), Q4 (2), Q11 (3), Q12 (2) are correct.
2. **Printed p. 58, Exercise 3 key:** "g) 3000cm" for 3 m is wrong — **3 m = 300 cm**.
3. **Printed p. 15, Exercise 3 key Q2:** only "760672" is listed as divisible by 8, but **65,432 is also divisible by 8** (432 ÷ 8 = 54). The key is incomplete.
4. **Printed p. 76, Exercise 2 key Q1:** c) "Around 90% (measure with a protractor)" for the share of the class receiving a B — implausible as a pie-slice estimate (the same key says grade C is the largest share and D the smallest); and d) "yes he is" answers "do you think Mrs. Kiden's class is hard working? Why or why not?" with the wrong pronoun and no reason. Both read as garbled; the underlying pie graph is an image, so I could not verify the true values from the text layer.
5. **Printed p. 15, divisibility of 11:** "2 728 has alternating sum of digits (2-7) + (2-8) = -11 since -11 is divisible by 1 so is 2 728" — should be "divisible by **11**", not "by 1".
6. **Printed p. 38, capacity matching activity:** a "3 250 ml" container is paired with a "3.2ℓ" flash card — 3,250 ml = **3.25ℓ**, not 3.2ℓ. (The other four pairings — 1ℓ/1,000 ml, 1,500 ml/1.5ℓ, 4.5ℓ/4,500 ml, 2,500 ml/2.5ℓ — are correct.)
7. **Printed p. 13, Exercise 2 key:** answers the area question ("2. Upper Nile") without flagging that the student book states the two areas in different, wrong units (square metres vs square miles; both should be square kilometres per the Primary 5 table).
8. **Printed p. 57–58, Exercise 3 key:** repeats the student book's item "i) 25.3m" under "Centimetres to metres" without noticing the unit is wrong (the item should be 25.3 cm → 0.253 m).
9. **Wrong cross-references:** p. 32 "attempt the exercise 2" for radius/diameter (student Exercise 3); p. 33 "activity 4 (page 22)" vs p. 34 "activity 4 (page 21)" (student Activity 4 is on printed p. 23); p. 69 "Work through the example on page 61" for tally tables (student tally example is on printed p. 73); p. 62 "the exercise 3" for scale drawing (student Exercise 4).
10. **TOC errors:** "2.7 Conversion of tones to kilograms" (p. 3) and the duplicated "3.5/3.6 Making cubes and cuboids" (p. 3); "tones" for tonnes recurs on pp. 39–41 ("1 tone = 1000kg").
11. **Printed p. 12:** "Tens: twin values" — should be "two-digit values" (in the keyword definitions).
12. Minor: p. 20 "U n it 11 Squares and square roots" (stray heading); p. 27 "Unit 5oKey word definitions" (stray characters); p. 36 "take the learners out of calls" (should be "class"); p. 71 bar-chart example lists five learner counts for six ages (12–17).
