## Grade 7, student — Pupil's Book 7 (no © line, ISBN or author printed; 98 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 6)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 7, Pupil's Book (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g07-student.pdf` — 98 PDF pages, 8,893,864 B
- **Pages actually read:** all 98 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full, plus visual reads of the two image-only pages (PDF p. 4, foreword; PDF p. 30, printed p. 24, weight exercise). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 6 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–6 are covers, book-care page, title page, foreword, contents pp. i–ii; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 7). The last printed page is 92 (PDF p. 98). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Cover check:** cover and title page both say "Primary Mathematics 7" / "Pupil's Book 7" and the contents describe the Primary 7 syllabus. Primary 7 confirmed.
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** **no © line, no ISBN and no individual author appears anywhere in the extracted text** — unlike Primary 5 and 6, whose title pages carry "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION". The foreword (PDF p. 4, scanned image, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) is the same series foreword: approved for use in all schools except international schools with effect from 4 February 2019; Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers (Kenya); Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation (UK). The book is evidently the same 2018 series, but the year is not printed on the pages I could read. "Published by: MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS" (cover). Authorship is given only as the Ministry "in conjunction with Subject experts".
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer. Two font-encoding quirks affect this book: (a) several passages extract as Caesar-shifted gibberish (e.g. "EHORZ" for "BELOW", "DQJOH" for "ANGLE") — the printed page is legible, and I decoded those passages by the consistent +3 letter shift, or read them visually where decoding was uncertain; (b) mathematical symbols (√, π, ∈, ∪, ∩, ², ³) often extract as odd glyphs — the surrounding words make the meaning clear. Worked-example arithmetic I checked by hand/calculator where a slip was suspected; those checks are noted under Slips. Exercise items were read in full. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the book teaches, unit by unit

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS — printed pp. 1–8 (PDF pp. 7–14)
- **1.1 Squares and square roots of perfect squares (pp. 1–2):** squaring as a × a; perfect square defined; square root as the inverse (√25 = 5); Activities 1–2 (squares of 8, 10, 14, 19, 30², 40², 52²; roots of 100, 125, 169, 256, 625 — see Slips, 125 is not a perfect square).
- **1.2 Squares and square roots of fractions and decimals (pp. 2–4):** squaring fractions (4/7)² = 16/49; mixed numbers via improper fractions (1 4/7)² = 121/49 = 2 23/49; square roots of fractions (√(81/169) = 9/13); square roots of decimals (√0.0169 = 0.13 — see Slips for the typo); Exercise 1 (square of 2 3/8 as a mixed number; roots of 2,304, 4,489, 3,481, 529, 3,249, 1,369; roots of 2.56, 7.29, 51.84, 42.25).
- **1.3 Cubes of numbers (pp. 4–5):** cube = number × itself three times; volume of a cube s³; perfect cubes 8, 27, 64, 125, 216, 343; Exercise 2 (cubes of 4, 9, 16, 14, 30, 20).
- **1.4 Ratios and proportions using the unitary method (pp. 5–6):** unit value then scale up (12 tins = 30 kg → 5 tins = 12.5 kg); Activity 2 and Exercise 3 (8 unitary word problems: bricks 16 → 192 kg; girls planting trees; workers digging holes; coins; buses seating people; pipes; toy blocks; flour for cakes; two fuel-consumption problems).
- **1.5 Percentage increase and decrease (pp. 7–8):** % increase = increase ÷ original × 100; % decrease likewise; worked 200 → 240 customers = 20% increase; 240 → 200 = 16.6…% decrease; Exercise 4 (SSP 50,000 → 70,000; SSP 90,000 → 75,000; sale SSP 16,000 → 13,000; town population 2,087 → 1,560; school trees 90 → 120).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT — printed pp. 9–30 (PDF pp. 15–36)
- **2.1 Circumference of circles (pp. 9–12):** C = πd = 2πr, π ≈ 22/7 or 3.14; worked C for r = 7 m both ways (44 m vs 43.96 m — the book shows both and notes "about 44 m"); semicircle perimeter (½πd + d; 21 cm diameter → 54 cm); Exercise 1 (circumferences with π = 22/7 for 14 m, 70 cm, 28 cm, 0.35 cm, 42 cm; with π = 3.14 for 10 m, 12 cm, 15 m, 20 cm, 100 cm; flower garden diameter 63 m; semicircular plot diameter 42 m fenced with posts 3 m apart; vegetable garden fenced with 5 strands of wire).
- **2.2 Relationships between quadrilaterals (pp. 13–16):** parallelogram, rhombus, trapezium, kite — properties and area formulas (parallelogram A = L × H; rhombus A = (a × b)/2 for diagonals; trapezium A = ½h(L₁ + L₂) — printed as "(L + L₂)"); summary table; Activities 2–3 (cut-out shapes, "guess my shape"); Exercise 2 (rectangular lawn 24 m × 30 m; a trapezium area in hectares from a diagram; rhombus diagonals 8 m and 5 m; rectangular plot perimeter 280 m, width 60 m; trapezium area 208 cm², find CD; Ruth's house — square area 196 cm², ¼ of the rectangle's area, length 49 cm, find width).
- **2.3 Surface area of common solids (pp. 17–20):** surface area as the material to cover a solid; volume = base area × height; prism (open like a carton box); cylinder worked fully (r = 2, h = 6: two circles 12.56 each, rectangle 75.36, total 100.48 square units); Exercise 3 (total surface area of a pictured solid; cube side 1.5 m; rectangular prism 6 × 4 × 2.1 m; cylinder r = 30 cm, h = 45 cm, open at one end; square pyramid base 20 cm, slant edge 30 cm).
- **2.4 Speed (pp. 20–23):** speed = distance ÷ time; km/h → m/s (×5/18; 54 km/h = 15 m/s); m/s → km/h (×18/5; 20 m/s = 72 km/h); Activities 1–2 (12 conversions each way, incl. 252 km/h, 840 m/s); Exercise 4 (car 400 m in 20 s; 50 m in 10 s; 90 m in 45 s; plane 395,000 m in 9,000 s); Activity 3 (200 m at 1.8 m/s in the hallway; flying disc 139 m at 13.0 m/s).
- **2.5 Weight (pp. 24–25):** metric weight conversion chart (1 g = 1,000 mg; 1 dag = 10 g; 1 kg = 1,000 g; 1 tonne = 1,000 kg (1 Mg); 1 gigagram = 1,000 megagrams); Exercise 5 (read visually from PDF p. 30: baked-beans tins 485 g vs 1.55 kg; 6 TVs combined 138 kg; 4 TVs + 4 DVD players at 3 kg each; Achol's 24,000 g and Garang's 23,500 g suitcases vs a 20 kg per-person limit; butter for cakes — 70 g per 250 g cake; balancing scales 0.975 kg vs 255 g + 300 g, and making 1,560 g).
- **2.6 Temperature (pp. 25–30):** thermometer; freezing/boiling points (0 °C/100 °C; 32 °F/212 °F; body temperature 37 °C/98.6 °F); conversion formulas °F = °C × 9/5 + 32 and °C = 5/9(°F − 32) with steps; worked 50 °C = 122 °F and 212 °F = 100 °C; matching game; Exercise 6 (read five thermometers in °C and five in °F — the thermometers are images; convert 35/20/50/65/90 °C to °F and 149/95/50/122/41/194 °F to °C).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY — printed pp. 31–51 (PDF pp. 37–57)
- **3.1 Transversal and angles they form (pp. 31–34):** transversal defined; corresponding, alternate and interior/exterior angles (see Slips — several statements are wrong/muddled); worked: angle 6 = 65° → angles 8, 2, 4 also 65°; Exercise 1 (parallelogram ABCD with transversal CDE, angles 58° and 133°, find angle EBC; parallel lines PQ/RS with transversals TR/TU, angles a–h with 110° and 25° given; exterior angle of quadrilateral WXYZ with a 66° angle and y; parallelogram BCDF with triangles DFE and ABF, angles 63° and 85°, find angle DFE — several questions are garbled in the text layer and decoded by the +3 shift; the figures are images).
- **3.2 Types of triangles based on sides (pp. 34–43):** equilateral (all sides/angles equal; compass construction); isosceles (two equal sides, base angles equal; protractor/compass construction, base 8 cm, sides 6 cm); right-angled triangle (construction: hypotenuse 5 cm, side 3 cm, right angle at C — the steps work out to a 3-4-5 triangle, though written confusingly); Activities 1–3; inscribing a circle (angle bisectors — see Slips, step 3 is wrong/confused) and circumscribing a circle (perpendicular bisectors); Activities 4–5.
- **3.3 Pythagoras' Theorem (pp. 44–46):** a² + b² = c²; 3-4-5 check (9 + 16 = 25); worked hypotenuse from 5 and 12 (c = 13); Activity 6 (find missing sides — the items are images, values not in the text layer).
- **3.4 Construct a parallelogram (pp. 46–47):** ABCD, AB = 4 cm, AD = 5 cm, angle A = 60° (decoded); three steps with compass arcs.
- **3.5 Construct a trapezium (pp. 47–49):** AB = 4.5 cm, height AD = 3.3 cm, BC = 3.6 cm; steps via perpendiculars and arcs; "Angle B is obtuse".
- **3.6 Drawing and interpreting linear scale (pp. 50–51):** scale in statement or ratio form; worked: 1 cm represents 50 m (ratio form; drawing length for 1.25 km; actual length for 10.5 cm — the worked answers are not in the text layer, only the questions); Activity 6 (2.5 cm represents 12.5 m; distance on plan for 35 m apart); conversions to recall (1 km = 100,000 cm; 1 ha = 10,000 m²).

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA — printed pp. 52–64 (PDF pp. 58–70)
- **4.1 Simplify algebraic expressions (pp. 52–53):** collecting like terms (21y + 5 − 13y + 8 = 8y + 13); expanding brackets (2(xy + 2) − 4(y + 3) = 2xy + 16 − 4y — note the book writes "−4y + 12" then collects to "+16 − 4y", correct); Activities 1–2 (incl. "q(2 + 4) − 4(5 + 2p)" — see Slips, variables mixed).
- **4.2 Evaluating expressions by substitution (pp. 53–55):** replace letters with numbers (4p + 5q with p = 3, q = 2 → 22; (2p + 3y) − (2y + 3p) with p = 4, y = 6 → 2); Example 4 (see Slips — y computed as 12 instead of 7, so the whole worked answer 362 is wrong); Activity 3 (p = 3, q = 5, r = 2, s = 7, c = 6, g = 4); Exercise 1 (solve for p/w/z/r/y from substituted equations; evaluate six expressions with c = 3, a = 4, b = 5; four with h = 8, g = 7, f = 5; six with d = 4, e = 6, q = 2).
- **4.3 Forming and solving algebraic equations (pp. 56–58):** translating statements ("d added to thrice d" → d + 3d; 6 pens at (p − 3) each → 6p − 18); Activities 4–5 (word statements; Samuel/Mercy books; boys/girls in a class of 24; Mary's pen/book/bag prices; James's rice and sugar); Exercise 2 (12 substitution items with chained values, e.g. "value of (5e + f)/g + e if e = 3, f = 3g + 2 and g = e + 1"; "value of J if JL = (12 × 0.7)/6 and L = 25").
- **4.4–4.7 Sets (pp. 59–64):** set notations table (∈ belongs to, ∉ does not belong to, : or | such that, ∅ null set, n(A), ∪ union, ∩ intersection, ℕ naturals, 𝕎 wholes, ℤ integers, ℚ rationals, ℚ⁺, ℝ reals, ℝ⁺, ℂ complex — symbols garbled in extraction but definitions readable); notes on set writing (curly braces, capitals, no repeats); equivalent sets (same cardinal number, symbol printed as 'ļ' — see Slips); equal sets (same elements); Exercise 3 (equivalent vs equal for five pairs, incl. rainbow colours vs days of the week, {4, 8, 12, 16} vs {8, 12, 4, 16}); Venn diagrams (Example 9: 100 learners, 35 science, 45 maths, 10 both → 70 either, 30 neither); Activity 5 (30 learners, 8 both, 18 English, all take at least one → maths total; 115 people, 65 passport, 30 both → voter-ID-only — see Slips, unsolvable as stated; three-colour percentages — 8% liked all three).

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS — printed pp. 65–78 (PDF pp. 71–84)
- **5.1 Frequency distribution (pp. 65–68):** frequency, frequency tables, grouped class intervals (size 40 groups from 0; "each group starts at a data value that is a multiple of that group"); Example 1 (roadside-service calls, December 2016 — the raw data list and the finished table are images, not in the text layer; the steps name values 28, 122, 217); Exercise 1 (heights of 20 Primary 7 pupils, intervals 130–135 upwards; weights of 30 oranges, intervals of 5 starting 40–45); Activity 1 (measure the class's heights).
- **5.2 The mean (pp. 68–69):** sum ÷ count; worked (15 + 18 + 22 + 20)/4 = 18.75; Exercise 2 (five lists of 9–10 numbers).
- **5.3 The median (pp. 69–70):** middle value; worked 3, 9, 15, 17, 44 (see Slips — printed median "150" instead of 15); even case (8 + 12)/2 = 10; Exercise 3 (mean and median of 13, 18, 13, 14, 13, 16, 14, 21, 13).
- **5.4 The mode (p. 70):** most frequent value; worked list with mode 15, and the "one fewer 15 → four modes (3, 15, 17, 44)" point (correct); Exercise 4 (headed "Calculate the Median" though it follows the mode section — five lists).
- **5.5 Scale drawings (pp. 71–73):** van 240 inches vs paper under 12 inches → scale 1:20; worked 12-inch drawing at 1:20 → 240 inches real; Exercise 5 (map 1 cm : 3 miles, 7 cm → 21 miles; school/shop map 1 cm = 100 m — distance in the image; 1 cm : 4 km, 52 km → 13 cm; 1:4000, 9 cm → 360 m; boat 150 m at 1:20 → 750 cm drawing).
- **5.6 Graph (pp. 74–76):** bar graph (movie votes: Ice Age, Up, Cars, Brave, Spy Kids — values in the image); pie charts (sector angle ∝ frequency; Example 6: Year 1 750, Year 2 420, Year 3 630, total 1,800 — the sector angles are not computed in the text); Activity 3 (traffic pie chart: cars 140, motorbikes 70, vans 55, buses 5, total 270).
- **5.7 Probability (pp. 76–78):** probability between 0 and 1; certain = 1, impossible = 0; examples (coin, die, rain, cards, lottery); "certain" vs "uncertain" lists (see Slips — "tomorrow is Friday if today is Monday" is listed as uncertain though its probability is 0); Activity 4 (marble sampling: sack with 100 blue and 25 green marbles; draw 5 at a time, with replacement; estimate the ratio, then count).

#### Unit 6: BUSINESS ACCOUNTING — printed pp. 79–92 (PDF pp. 85–98)
- **6.1 Profit and loss (pp. 79–81):** profit = SP − CP; loss = CP − SP; Example 1 (see Slips — rice profit computed as 100,000 − 70,000 = 30,000 although the buying price was 7,000); maize loss 10,000 − 8,000 = 2,000; Activity 1 (potatoes 1,500 → 800; shirt 600 → 900; potatoes 20,000 + 5,000 profit; maize 3,000, loss 600); Exercise 1 (profit/loss table, 7 rows; bike 500 → 300; vegetables 50 → 70; dress 120, loss 50).
- **6.2 Discount (pp. 81–83):** discount = marked price − selling price; worked trousers 1,000 → 800, discount 200; Activity 2 (shoes 250 after 20 discount; bicycle 500 → 350; 14 sweets at 60, marked 5 each — see Slips, ambiguous; 20 bags of maize at 5,800, marked 300 each); Exercise 2 (discount table, 6 rows; shoes 5,800 → 4,500; dress 3,500 → 3,300; watch marked 800, "discounted price of 150" — see Slips, ambiguous).
- **6.3 Simple interest (pp. 83–86):** SI = P × R/100 × T; worked 2,000 at 12% for 1 year = 240; 10,000 at 13% for 6 months = 650; 2,000 at 18% for 4 months = 120, total repaid 2,120; Activity 3–4; Exercise 3 (SI/amount table, 7 rows; Samantha 120,000 at 12% for 2 years; Sam 500,000 at 14% for 4 years; Mary 150,000 at 12% for 3 years).
- **6.4 Commission (pp. 87–88):** % of sales; worked Joy 5% of 10,000 = 500; David salary 10,000 + 2% of 30,000 = 10,600; Activity 5 (salespersons A 20,000 / B 15,000 / C 35,000 / D 55,000 at 5%; salary 30,000 + 5% of 40,000); Exercise 4 (10% of 50,000; 5% of 10,000 + 30,000 salary; 3% of 50,000; 25 packets × 300 at 5%; 15 bags × 250 at 8%).
- **6.5 Hire purchase (pp. 88–90):** deposit + instalments; worked chair: deposit 2,000 + 5 × 1,000 = 7,000 hire-purchase price (see Slips — interest printed as 7,000 − 2,000 = 5,000 instead of 7,000 − 5,000 = 2,000); Activity 6 (bed 2,000 cash or 800 + 4 × 400; dining set 5,000 cash or 1,500 + 4 × 1,000); Exercise 5 (phone 20,000 + 5 × 5,000 vs cash 30,000; radio 30,000 + 4 × 5,000 vs cash 80,000 — hire purchase cheaper than cash, an oddity worth noting; TV 120,000, 7 × 20,000, no deposit; microwave 15,000, 10% deposit + 6 × 4,000).
- **6.5 Bills (pp. 90–92; second section numbered 6.5 — see Slips):** a bill as a statement of money owed; Example 7 (see Slips — the bill's lines do not match its own item list); Activity 7 (radio/TV/fridge/phone/sofa bill; market items totalling 420, paid 500, change 80); Exercise 6 (pencil/textbook/exercise book/rubber/ruler bill, paid 500; shoes/dress/sunglasses/bracelet bill; plates/cups/spoons/table mats bill, paid 1,000; farm inputs — 3 kg pesticides @ 750, 5 kg fertiliser @ 550, 5 kg beans @ 650, 8 bags maize @ 1,050, paid 150,000); Activity 8 (visit a shop/hotel — cut off mid-sentence at the end of the book; the book ends without a closing section).

### What the book does NOT teach (honest negatives, at Primary 7 level)
- No HCF/LCM, no prime numbers, no Roman numerals, no decimals/fractions conversions (left in earlier grades); no multiplication/division of fractions; no negative-number arithmetic as a topic; no area of circles (circumference only); no volume of solids (surface area only, and no volume exercises set); no time; no coordinates/graphs of functions (the "Graph" section is bar/pie charts only); no simultaneous equations; no inequalities; no compound interest.
- Statistics: no grouped mean, no range, no quartiles, no cumulative frequency; the frequency-distribution example's data and finished table are images only.
- Sets: union/intersection symbols are listed but no union/intersection problems are set (only equivalent/equal and Venn "either/neither" problems); no complement, no subsets.
- Probability: no probability calculations are set (no P(event) = favourable/total anywhere); only the 0–1 scale and examples.
- No answers section anywhere; no unit revision exercises in any unit; the book ends mid-activity (Activity 8, printed p. 92).

### Held-copy defects
- No missing pages (printed pp. 1–92 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–98 complete). Two systematic text-layer problems, both read around rather than lost: (1) several passages extract as Caesar-shifted gibberish — the printed pages are legible (I decoded them by the consistent +3 shift and verified one page visually); (2) mathematical symbols often extract as odd glyphs. The frequency Example 1 data list and finished table (pp. 66–68) and several exercise figures are images only, so their values are not in the text layer. The degree symbol renders as "0" or "Ý" in places — an extraction artefact, not a print error.

### Slips and wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 54, Example 4 (substitution):** "y = ½x + 5" with x = 4 is worked as "y = ½ × 4 × 6 = 12" — wrong; ½ × 4 + 5 = **7**. The whole worked answer (362) follows from the wrong y = 12; with y = 7 the expression ⅓(3x + 5y) + 2y² + 7p − 6 (x = 4, p = 8) is ⅓(12 + 35) + 98 + 56 − 6 = 163⅔, not 362.
2. **Printed p. 79, Example 1 Q1 (profit):** buying price SSP 7,000, selling price SSP 100,000, but the working subtracts "100000 − 70000 = 30000". The correct profit is **SSP 93,000** (100,000 − 7,000); the book subtracted 70,000, a number that appears nowhere in the question.
3. **Printed p. 89, Example 6 (hire purchase):** "Interest = total amount paid − original price = 7000 − 2000 = 5000" — the subtraction uses the deposit (2,000) instead of the original price (5,000); the correct interest is **SSP 2,000** (7,000 − 5,000).
4. **Printed p. 91, Example 7 (bill):** the bill does not match its own item list. "2 packets of rice @ 200" is billed SSP 2,000 (correct: 400); "2 packets of flour @ 500" is billed SSP 5,000 (correct: 1,000); "a loaf of bread @ 50" is billed SSP 500 (correct: 50); "a litre of oil @ 300" is billed SSP 3,000 (correct: 300). The printed total SSP 10,500 should be **SSP 1,750**.
5. **Printed p. 69, Example 3 (median):** "Find the median. 3, 9, 15, 17, 44. The middle or median number is 150" — the median is **15**, not 150.
6. **Printed p. 32, transversal angles:** "All angles that are either exterior angles, interior angles, alternate angles or corresponding angles are all congruent" — false; only corresponding and alternate pairs (with parallel lines) are congruent; adjacent angles on a straight line are supplementary. Also "Angles that are in the area between the parallel lines like angle 2 and 8 … are called interior angles" mislabels angle 8 (exterior), and "alternate angles e.g. 1 + 8" pairs angles that are not alternate.
7. **Printed p. 42, inscribing a circle:** step 3 says "Draw the circle with radius at the intersection point that passes through the point you obtained in the last step" — this does not construct the inscribed circle (the radius must be the perpendicular distance from the incentre to a side); as written it is unworkable.
8. **Printed p. 64, Activity 5 Q2 (Venn):** "115 people… 65 had passport and 30 had both, how many had voter id only and not passport?" — unsolvable as stated: the total number with voter ID is never given.
9. **Printed p. 2, Activity 2b:** "find the square root of … 125" in a section on square roots of perfect squares — 125 is not a perfect square (√125 ≈ 11.18); it has no whole-number square root.
10. **Printed p. 4, Example 5:** "√0.0168 = 0.13" — a typo for √0.0169 (the example is about 0.0169 throughout).
11. **Printed p. 14, trapezium properties:** "No sides, angles and diagonals are equal" stated as a property of trapezia — false in general (an isosceles trapezium has equal legs, equal base angles and equal diagonals).
12. **Printed p. 61, equivalent sets:** the symbol for equivalent sets is printed as 'ļ' — not a standard symbol (equivalence is usually written with ~ or ↔); a pupil copying it would be marked wrong elsewhere.
13. **Printed p. 90, Exercise 5 Q2:** radio hire purchase (30,000 + 4 × 5,000 = 50,000) is CHEAPER than the cash price (80,000) — the opposite of how hire purchase works; the numbers contradict the concept being taught.
14. **Printed p. 53, Activity 2b:** "q(2 + 4) − 4(5 + 2p)" mixes variables q and p in one item with no instruction on which to use — inconsistent with the other items.
15. **Printed p. 82, Activity 2c:** "Samuel bought 14 sweets at 60 South Sudanese Pounds, if the marked price of each sweet is 5" — ambiguous whether 60 is the total or per sweet (14 × 5 = 70 marked; if 60 is per sweet the numbers are absurd).
16. **Printed p. 83, Exercise 2 Q4:** "James buys the watch at a discounted price of 150" for a watch marked 800 — ambiguous whether 150 is the price paid or the discount (pays 150 vs pays 650).
17. **Printed p. 25, Activity 4:** "one containing normal water and another containing worm water" — "worm" for "warm".
18. **Printed p. 46, Example 3:** the answer is printed with a garbled symbol ("c = ¥…") — the value 13 is recoverable from the working (5² + 12² = 169) but the printed answer line is unreadable.
19. **TOC/structure:** two different sections are both numbered 6.5 (Hire purchase, p. 88; Bills, p. 90) — the Bills section should be 6.6.
20. Minor: p. 70 Exercise 4 is headed "Calculate the Median" but sits in the mode section (it is a median exercise — the heading is arguably right and the placement is the oddity); p. 53 Example 3 Q2's displayed working shows "+ (3 × p)" inside a subtracted bracket though the numbers are computed correctly.

## Grade 7, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 7 (no © line, ISBN or author printed; 74 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 6)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 7, Teacher's Guide (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g07-teacher.pdf` — 74 PDF pages, 9,395,247 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 74 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full, plus visual reads of the two text-empty pages (PDF p. 4 = foreword as a scanned image; PDF p. 62, printed p. 56 = a montage of two reduced student-book pages). No page skipped.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 6 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–6 are covers, book-care page, title page, foreword, contents pp. i–ii; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 7). The last printed page is 68 (PDF p. 74). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** title page (PDF p. 3) reads "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 7", "Funded by:" (funder name not printed), "This book is the property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE". **No © line, no ISBN and no individual author is printed anywhere in the guide** — same as the Primary 7 student book. The foreword (PDF p. 4, scanned image, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, Minister) is the series foreword adapted to the Teacher's Guide, same approval date 4 February 2019, same credits (Longhorn and Mountain Top, Kenya; Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation UK).
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer plus visual reads of the two image pages. Every answer in the guide's keys was re-checked by hand/calculator against the student book's questions; the results are under Companion check and Slips. Where a key reads values off a graph/diagram image, I say so and do not claim verification. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the guide contains, unit by unit

#### Front matter — Introduction (printed pp. 1–5, PDF pp. 7–11)
- Introduction (pp. 1–2): six units; unit overview table (Unit 1 Numbers: percentages and scale; Unit 2 Measurement: Surface areas of solids; Unit 3 Geometry: Transverse and parallel lines; Unit 4 Algebra: algebraic expressions (2); Unit 5 Statistics: Group data and probability (1); Unit 6 Business accounting).
- Teaching strategies (pp. 2–4): problem-based learning, open-ended questions (same rectangle-perimeter-160 cm example as the Primary 5 and 6 guides), group work, peer teaching.
- Classroom assessment (p. 5): observation / conversation / product ("triangulation").
- TOC gaps: Unit 3 lists only 3.1 Transversals (p. 31) and 3.2 Types of triangles (p. 32) — Pythagoras (3.3), parallelogram (3.4), trapezium (3.5) and linear scale (3.6) have no TOC entry and no section in the body either (see Companion check). Section 4.5 Equivalent Sets is also missing from the TOC (it jumps 4.4 → 4.6), though the body does cover equal/equivalent sets. The guide's TOC correctly numbers Bills as 6.6, fixing the student book's duplicated 6.5.

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 6–13, PDF pp. 12–19)
- Unit opener (pp. 6–7): "Learn about" (incl. "cubes numbers and roots" and "conversion of fractions to decimals" — the student book teaches no cube roots and no fraction→decimal conversion in this unit), key inquiry questions, learning outcomes, competencies.
- 1.1 Squares and square roots (p. 8): brief notes; Activity 1 key (p. 9): 64, 100, 196, 361, 900, 1,600, 2,704 — all correct. Activity 2 key: "10, 13, 16, 25" for roots of 100, 125, 169, 256, 625 — misaligned (see Slips; √125 is skipped and the rest shifted).
- 1.2 Squares/roots of fractions and decimals (p. 9): method restated; Exercise 1 key (pp. 9–10) is a muddle (see Slips): its first six answers (48, 67, 59, 23, 57, 37) are the student book's Q2 roots, followed by six extra items with no question, then a three-item "2." matching nothing, then Q3 decimal roots with "27" for √7.29 (should be 2.7). The student book's Q1 (square of 2 3/8) is never answered.
- 1.3 Cubes (pp. 10–11): notes; Exercise 2 key (p. 11): 64, 729, 4,096, 2,744, 27,000 ("2 7000"), 8,000 — all correct.
- 1.4 Unitary method (pp. 11–12): Activity 2 key (228 kg; 182 trees — correct); Exercise 3 key (p. 12): 378 holes, 255 g, 840 people, 798 m, 869 cm, 1,980 g, 308 km, 948 km — seven correct, one wrong (Q3 should be 980 people, not 840; see Slips).
- 1.5 Percentage increase/decrease (pp. 12–13): three-step method restated; Exercise 4 key (p. 13): 40%, 16.67%, 18.75%, 25.25% — all correct (Q5, the trees question, has no numeric answer).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 14–27, PDF pp. 20–33)
- Unit opener (pp. 14–16): "Learn about" promises "calculate the area of a circle" and surface areas of "cubes, squares, cones" — the student book teaches circumference only (no circle area) and never mentions cones. Outcomes (p. 15) list "Calculations involving discount, percentages, simple interest, commission and hire purchase" — those belong to Unit 6, not Unit 2.
- 2.1 Circumference (pp. 17–18): C = πd, π ≈ 22/7 or 3.142, with the rule "when the diameter is 7 or its multiple, then π is 22/7" (a rough classroom rule, not a mathematical fact); Exercise 1 key (p. 18): Q1 radii answers (88 m, 440 cm, 176 cm, 2.2 cm, 264 cm) and Q2 diameters answers (31.4 m, 37.68 cm, 47.1 m, 62.8 cm, 314 cm) all correct; Q3 "99m", Q4 "65 posts" and Q5 "81m" are wrong or unverifiable (see Slips).
- 2.2 Quadrilaterals (pp. 18–21): properties restated (parallelogram, rhombus, trapezium); a comparison table (parallelogram/rectangle/rhombus/square — note "Diagonals are perpendicular: square ✓" is correct, "Diagonals are congruent: rhombus ✕" correct); Activity 3 ("guess my shape" — the student book says instructions are in the teacher's guide, but the guide gives only this one-line description); Exercise 2 key (p. 21): 432 m², 0.48 m², 20 m², 4,800 m², 17.33 cm, 16 cm — Q1 and Q2 wrong, Q5 unverifiable (see Slips); Q3 (20 m²) and Q4 (4,800 m²) and Q6 (16 cm) correct.
- 2.3 Surface area (pp. 22–23): formulas (cuboid 2(Lw + Lh + wh); cube 6S²; closed cylinder 2πr(r + l); open cylinder πr² + 2πrl); Exercise 3 key (p. 23): six values (18,100 m²; 13.5 m²; 90 m²; 3,110.58 cm²; 125.68 cm²; 1,531.2 cm²) for a five-part exercise — misaligned (see Slips); the cube (13.5), prism (90) and pyramid (1,531.2) values check out; the others cannot be verified from the text layer.
- 2.4 Speed (pp. 23–24): speed = distance/time; conversions ×36/10 and ×10/36; Activity 2 key (p. 23) is a scrambled permutation of the correct answers (see Slips — only 2 of 12 match their items); Exercise 4 key (p. 24): 20, 5, 2, 43.88 m/s — all correct; Activity 4 key: "1. 133.33s" (wrong — 200 m at 1.8 m/s is 111.11 s) and "2. 10.69m" (right number, wrong unit — seconds, not metres).
- 2.5 Weight (p. 25): "Mass/weight — Amount of matter in a substance"; Exercise 5 key: 580 g; 23 kg; 104 kg; "27.5kg" (wrong — see Slips; the over-limit amount is 7.5 kg); 560 g; "1kg; 2kg" (Q5b/c — correct); "420g; 585g" (Q6a/b — correct). The key's numbering is garbled (it refers to questions 7, 8, 9, 10, which do not exist — the student book's exercise has Q1–Q6).
- 2.6 Temperature (pp. 25–27): definitions; units listed (Celsius, "centigrades", Fahrenheit, Kelvin); conversion formulas as printed are WRONG (see Slips — both omit the 32 offset); Activity 4 key (p. 26): thermometer; 37.5 °C (student book's table says 37 °C); mercury; °C or °F; "No. 0°F is cooler" (correct); Exercise 5/6 key (p. 27): thermometer readings in °C (30, 13, 22, 44, 27) and °F (42, 7, 32, 10, 48) are image-only and unverifiable; the °C→°F sub-key is misaligned (194 °F and 176 °F in the wrong places — see Slips); the °F→°C sub-key (65, 35, 10, 50, 5, 90 °C) is fully correct.

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 28–34, PDF pp. 34–40)
- Unit opener (pp. 28–30): "Learn about" promises symmetry with mirrors/tracing paper, transformations (translation, enlargement), nets for cubes/prisms/cuboids/pyramids and faces/edges/vertices — **none of these are in the student book's Unit 3**. Outcomes (p. 29) promise construction of parallelogram, rhombus and trapezium and Pythagoras (2D).
- 3.1 Transversals (p. 31): definitions (alternate, vertically opposite, corresponding — all equal, with parallel lines assumed); Exercise 1 key: "1. 85°; 2. Angles a, c, e and g are equal (alternate angles); angles d, b, f, h are equal; 3. y = 48°; 4. 76°" — all read off the student book's diagram images and unverifiable from the text layer (see Slips for Q4, where 32° is the expected value if angle DFE is vertically opposite angle AFB). The key's instruction line says "assess the learners understanding of temperature" — a copy-paste error in a geometry section.
- 3.2 Types of triangles (pp. 32–34): definitions; Activities 1–5 guidance (equilateral, isosceles, right-angled, inscribing, circumscribing — page references to the student book pp. 34, 40–41, 42, 43–44). **There is no 3.3 (Pythagoras), 3.4 (parallelogram), 3.5 (trapezium) or 3.6 (linear scale) section anywhere in the guide** — four student-book sub-topics, including all of Pythagoras and scale drawing, have no guide coverage at all.

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 35–45, PDF pp. 41–51)
- Unit opener (pp. 35–36): "Learn about", inquiry questions, outcomes; introduction (p. 37) and a BODMAS table.
- 4.1 Simplifying (pp. 37–38): Activity 1 key (p. 38): a) 11x + 9 ✓; b) "2 + 15p" (wrong — should be 16p + 2); c) 17q + 3 − 3y ✓; d) 9 + 8s ✓; e) "13r + 15 − 12xy" (wrong — should be 23r + 15 − 12xy); f) 29p + 5 + 12xy ✓. Activity 2 key: all six correct (11p + 8; 6q − 20 − 8p; 6r + 4; 11s + 15; 16q + 2; 26z − 27).
- 4.2 Substitution (p. 39): Activity 3 key: "a. 10 b. 12 c. 20 d. 5 e. 105 f. 42 g. 7 h. 8" — misaligned/wrong (see Slips; correct values are 8, 20, 105, 7, 8, 12, 9, 42). Exercise 1 key (p. 40): Q1 (2.5), Q2 (10), Q5 (13), Q6a (6), Q6c (11), Q6f (2), Q7a (6), Q7c (4), Q8a (32), Q8c (22), Q8e (12), Q8f (8) correct; Q3 (2, should be 6), Q4 (3, should be −5), Q6b (3, should be 11), Q6d (20, should be 3), Q6e (3, should be 28), Q7b (38, should be 4), Q7c is listed as 4 but the correct value is 38 (Q7b/c appear swapped), Q7d (4, should be 3), Q8b (6, should be 8), Q8d (12, should be 6) wrong — 10 errors in 22 sub-items.
- 4.3 Forming equations (p. 41): Activity 4 key (all six correct in substance); Activity 5 key: a) q + 6 ✓; b) (24 − y) boys ✓; c) SSP 9y ✓; d) "SSP. 180y" (wrong — should be 100p + 80y). Exercise 2 key: Q1–Q6, Q8–Q12 verified correct (162; 78; 10.25; 7; 0.056; 4.6875; 5.7; 3.5; 7; 42; 124); Q7 (10.667) does not match the straightforward reading of the printed expression (8) and the expression as printed is ambiguous; an extra 13th answer (3.2) has no question.
- 4.4–4.7 Sets (pp. 41–45): notations table reproduced (with correct symbols, unlike the student book's garbled extraction); Exercise 3 key (p. 44): equal sets (a), (d); equivalent sets (b), (c), (e) — correct. Venn diagrams (pp. 44–45): generic union/intersection explanation copied from a web-style source ("Feel free to click on the image to try this diagram as a template", "The green circle is A, and the blue circle is B") — including web-page language that makes no sense in a printed book; no key for Activity 5 (the Venn problems, incl. the unsolvable Q2).

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 46–56, PDF pp. 52–62)
- Unit opener (pp. 46–48): "Learn about" promises "mean, mode and median of grouped data" and "travel graphs" — the student book teaches ungrouped measures only and has no travel graphs. Outcomes and introduction (pp. 47–48) repeat the grouped-data promise.
- 5.1 Frequency distribution (pp. 49–51): definitions; Exercise 1 key (pp. 50–51): heights table (3, 4, 4, 5, 1, 1, 1, 1; sum 20) and weights table (2, 2, 3, 3, 5, 3, 6, 1, 4, 1; sum 30) — both fully correct against the student book's data (tally-mark glyphs in the text layer are inconsistent with the frequencies in a few rows, but the frequencies are right).
- 5.2 Mean (p. 51): Exercise 2 key: a–e correct (54.44, 36.1, 32.33, 57.44, 42) plus four extra answers (39.33, 44.67, 36.67, 57.8) with no questions.
- 5.3 Median (p. 52): Exercise 3 key: mean 15, median 14 — correct.
- 5.4 Mode (p. 52): Exercise 4 key: ten answers (18, 6, 39, 28, 30, 24, 14, 30, 30, 18) for five questions — the first five are the correct medians; the last five belong to nothing in the student book.
- 5.5 Scale drawing (p. 53): Exercise 5 key: 21 miles; "Accurate reading × 100m" (not a number — the map distance is image-only); 13 cm; 360 m; 750 cm — the four numeric answers are correct.
- 5.6 Graph (p. 54): guidance to use the bar graph on student p. 62 (the guide says p. 62; the student book's graph is on printed p. 74); key (p. 55): "1. 5; 2. More learners voted for up than ice age; 3. Fewer voted for cars; 5. 8 − 7 = 1; 6. 10 + 7 = 17" — Q4 (which movie received exactly 10 votes?) is skipped; all values are read off the bar-graph image and unverifiable from the text layer.
- 5.7 Probability (p. 55): "Probability = chance / Number of possible outcomes" — garbled/incomplete formula (should be favourable outcomes ÷ total possible outcomes); no worked probabilities, no key for Activity 4.
- Printed p. 56 (PDF p. 62) is a montage of two reduced student-book pages (printed pp. 77–78: probability examples and the marble Activity 4) — read visually; it matches the student book.

#### Unit 6: BUSINESS ACCOUNTING (printed pp. 57–68, PDF pp. 63–74)
- Unit opener (pp. 57–58): "Learn about", inquiry questions, outcomes; activities (visit a business enterprise).
- 6.1 Profit and loss (pp. 58–60): definitions and formulas; Activity 1 key (p. 59): 700; 300; 25,000; "SSP 240" (wrong — should be SSP 2,400; see Slips). Exercise 1 key (pp. 59–60): profit/loss table fully correct (30 profit; 20 loss; 200 loss; 50 profit; 70 loss; 50 profit; 80 profit); Q2 loss 200; Q3 profit 20; Q4 SP 70 — all correct. The student book's worked rice-profit error (100,000 − 70,000) is not corrected.
- 6.2 Discounts (pp. 60–61): formula; Activity 2 key: 270; 150; 10; 10 (per bag) — all correct under the "60 total" reading of the sweets question. Exercise 2 key (p. 61): table rows mostly correct, except row f (marked 250, SP 175, discount 25 — internally inconsistent: 250 − 175 = 75); Q2 discount 1,300; Q3 discount 200; Q4 "SSP. 650" (treats 150 as the discount — the student book is ambiguous here).
- 6.3 Simple interest (pp. 61–63): SI = PRT/100; Activity 3 key (p. 61): 900 ✓; 6,300 (wrong — should be 525); 360 ✓; 900 (wrong — should be 75); 300 ✓. Activity 4 key (p. 62): 27,200; 32,250; 57,000 — all correct. Exercise 3 key (pp. 62–63): SI/amount table fully correct (1,250/21,250; 14,000/64,000; 720/12,720; 8,000/168,000; 5,250/40,250; 6,760/58,760; 1,050/16,050); Q2 (28,800; 148,800), Q3 (280,000; 780,000), Q4 (54,000; 204,000) — all correct.
- 6.4 Commissions (pp. 64–65): formula; Activity 5 key: 1,000; 750; 1,750; 2,750; 32,000 — all correct. Exercise 4 key: 5,000; 30,500; 1,500; 375; 300 — all correct.
- 6.5 Hire purchase (pp. 65–66): formula; Activity 6 key: 1) HP price 2,400, interest 400 ✓; 2) "SSP. 5500" (the hire-purchase price, not the "how much more" the question asks — the extra paid is 500). Exercise 5 key (p. 66): 1) 45,000; 15,000 ✓; 2) "(change cash price to 30000) Interest = SSP 20000; SSP. 50000" — the guide rewrites the student book's cash price (80,000 → 30,000) instead of flagging the broken question; 3) 20,000 ✓; 4) 25,500; 10,500 ✓.
- 6.6 Bills (pp. 66–68): Activity 7 key (p. 67): Q1 is wrong (see Slips — the guide drops the phone, prices the fridge at 30,000 instead of 120,000, and totals 310,000 instead of 430,000); Q2 correct (total 420, change 80). Exercise 6 key (pp. 67–68): Q1 total 305, change 195 ✓; Q2 total 760 ✓; Q3 total 1,200, balance 200 ✓; Q4 (the farmer's bill) has no key — the guide ends after Activity 8's pre-visit note, with no closing section.

### Companion check (guide vs its student book)
- **Same book, same order:** broadly yes — six units in the same order — but the guide has NO sections for student sub-topics 3.3 (Pythagoras), 3.4 (parallelogram), 3.5 (trapezium) and 3.6 (linear scale), and its TOC omits them as well as 4.5 (Equivalent Sets). A teacher using only the guide would have no guidance at all for Pythagoras or scale drawing.
- **Guide covers things the student book lacks:** Unit 2 promises circle area and cones; Unit 3 promises symmetry, transformations (translation/enlargement) and nets; Unit 5 promises grouped-data mean/median/mode and travel graphs; Unit 1 promises cube roots and fraction→decimal conversion. None of this is in the pupil's book.
- **Student book content the guide does not key:** Exercise 1 Q1 (square of 2 3/8), the scale-drawing worked answers (Example 5), Activity 5 (Venn problems), Activity 4 (probability marbles), Exercise 6 Q4 (farmer's bill), the thermometer readings (image-only), the bar-graph Q4, and all of Unit 3.3–3.6. The guide also never corrects any of the student book's slips — it repeats the wrong loss/profit figures by silence, repeats the "tulips"-style errors by silence, and in one place (Exercise 5 Q2, hire purchase) rewrites the question's numbers instead of flagging them.
- **Guide-only errors:** many and serious (see Slips) — the temperature conversion formulas without the 32 offset, the scrambled speed key, the Exercise 1 algebra key (10 errors), the Activity 3 substitution key, the simple-interest month errors, and the Activity 7 bill that drops an item.

### What the guide does NOT teach / lacks
- No scheme of work, no lesson plans with timings, no pacing.
- No keys for the exercises listed above; no diagnostic/remedial guidance; no guidance for Pythagoras, parallelogram/trapezium construction or linear scale.
- No treatment of the student book's errors (see companion check).

### Held-copy defects
- PDF p. 4 is the foreword as a scanned image (no text layer) — read visually; it matches the series foreword. Printed p. 56 (PDF p. 62) is a reduced-size montage of two student-book pages (printed pp. 77–78) — legible as a layout reference; I read it visually. Otherwise no missing pages (printed pp. 1–68 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–74 complete), no blurred pages. The degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer in places — an extraction artefact, not a print error.

### Slips and wrong answers in the guide itself (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 25, temperature conversion formulas:** "Temperature in oC to oF = Temperature in oC x 9/5" and "Temperature in oF in oC = Temperature in oF x 5/9" — both omit the 32 offset. Correct: °F = °C × 9/5 + 32; °C = (°F − 32) × 5/9. The student book has them right; the guide's versions would give 50 °C → 90 °F (instead of 122 °F).
2. **Printed p. 23, speed Activity 2 key:** the twelve answers (20, 70, 65, 45, 15, 40, 60, 30, 5, 50, 35, 55 m/s) are a scrambled permutation of the correct set. Correct mapping: 72→20, 162→45, 216→60, 180→50, 252→70, 54→15, 108→30, 126→35, 234→65, 144→40, 18→5, 198→55. Only the first (20) and last (55) match their items.
3. **Printed p. 40, Exercise 1 key (algebra):** 10 errors in 22 sub-items — Q3 "2" should be **6**; Q4 "3" should be **−5**; Q6b "3" should be **11**; Q6c "11" should be **3** (b/c swapped); Q6d "20" should be **3**; Q6e "3" should be **28**; Q7b "38" should be **4**; Q7c "4" should be **38** (b/c swapped); Q7d "4" should be **3**; Q8b "6" should be **8**; Q8d "12" should be **6**. (That is 11 wrong values counting Q7c; the correctly answered sub-items are Q1, Q2, Q5, Q6a, Q6f, Q7a, Q8a, Q8c, Q8e, Q8f.)
4. **Printed p. 39, Activity 3 key (substitution):** "a. 10 b. 12 c. 20 d. 5 e. 105 f. 42 g. 7 h. 8" for p = 3, q = 5, r = 2, s = 7, c = 6, g = 4. Correct values are a) 8, b) 20, c) 105, d) 7, e) 8, f) 12, g) 9, h) 42. The guide's list contains 10 and 5 (matching nothing) and omits 9; it appears to key a different set of items.
5. **Printed p. 61, Activity 3 key (simple interest):** Q2 "SSP. 6300" should be **SSP 525** (5,000 × 18% × 7/12); Q4 "SSP. 900" should be **SSP 75** (3,000 × 5% × 6/12). Both forget to convert months to years.
6. **Printed p. 67, Activity 7 key Q1 (bill):** the guide's bill lists Radio 50,000; TV Set 80,000; Fridge 30,000; Sofa Set 150,000; total 310,000. The student book's items are radio 50,000; TV 80,000; fridge **120,000**; phone **30,000**; sofa 150,000 — total **430,000**. The guide drops the phone entirely and prices the fridge at the phone's price.
7. **Printed p. 59, Activity 1 key d):** "SSP 240" should be **SSP 2,400** (3,000 − 600).
8. **Printed p. 38, Activity 1 key:** b) "2 + 15p" should be **16p + 2** (13p + 3p = 16p); e) "13r + 15 − 12xy" should be **23r + 15 − 12xy** (18r + 5r = 23r).
9. **Printed p. 9, Activity 2 key (square roots):** "10, 13, 16, 25" for 100, 125, 169, 256, 625 — four answers for five items; √125 is skipped and the rest shifted. Correct: 10, √125 (≈11.18, not a whole number), 13, 16, 25.
10. **Printed pp. 9–10, Exercise 1 key:** misaligned throughout — its "1." i–vi (48, 67, 59, 23, 57, 37) are the student book's Q2 roots, followed by six extra items (76, 89, 24, 32, 56, 30) with no question; its "2." (8, 12, 67) matches nothing in the student book; its "3." gives "27" for √7.29, which should be **2.7** (1.6, 7.2, 6.5 are correct). The student book's Q1 (square of 2 3/8 = 5 41/64) is never answered.
11. **Printed p. 12, Exercise 3 key Q3:** "840 people" should be **980 people** (420 ÷ 15 × 35).
12. **Printed p. 18, Exercise 1 key Q3/Q4:** "3. 99m" — a full circle of diameter 63 m has circumference 198 m; 99 m is only half the circumference, and a semicircle perimeter would be 99 + 63 = 162 m, so 99 matches neither reading. "4. 65 posts" — a semicircular plot of diameter 42 m has perimeter ½πd + d = 108 m, giving 36 posts at 3 m spacing; 65 is the semicircular arc length in metres (≈66), mistaken for a post count. Q5 "81m" is unverifiable from the text layer (the garden figure is an image).
13. **Printed p. 21, Exercise 2 key:** Q1 "432m²" should be **720 m²** (24 × 30); Q2 "0.48m²" is wrong on number and unit — the trapezium (parallel sides 120 m and 200 m, height 50 m) has area 8,000 m² = **0.8 hectares**; Q5 "17.33cm" is unverifiable and the guide itself says to "change ae to 8cm", i.e. to alter the printed question.
14. **Printed p. 23, Exercise 3 key:** six values for a five-part exercise; items i (18,100 m²), iv (3,110.58 cm²) and v (125.68 cm²) cannot be matched to any part of the student book's Exercise 3 from the text layer (the cylinder, r = 30 cm, h = 45 cm, open at one end, should be 11,304 cm²). The cube (13.5 m²), prism (90 m²) and pyramid (1,531.2 cm²) values check out.
15. **Printed p. 24, Activity 4 key:** "1. 133.33s" should be **111.11 s** (200 m ÷ 1.8 m/s); "2. 10.69m" should be **10.69 seconds** (139 m ÷ 13.0 m/s), not metres.
16. **Printed p. 25, Exercise 5 key Q4:** "27.5kg" is wrong — the two suitcases total 47.5 kg against a 40 kg combined limit, so they are **7.5 kg** over (the guide subtracted only one person's 20 kg limit).
17. **Printed p. 27, Exercise 5/6 key Q2 (°C→°F):** "d. 194°F e. 176°F" are misaligned — 65 °C = 149 °F and 90 °C = 194 °F; the guide's list (95, 68, 122, 194, 176) corresponds to 35, 20, 50, 90, 80 °C, skipping 65 °C.
18. **Printed p. 41, Activity 5 key d):** "SSP. 180y" should be **100p + 80y** (rice at SSP 100/kg, sugar at SSP 80/kg).
19. **Printed p. 61, Exercise 2 key row f:** marked 250, selling price 175, discount 25 — internally inconsistent (250 − 175 = 75, not 25).
20. **Printed p. 66, Exercise 5 key Q2:** "(change cash price to 30000)" — the guide rewrites the student book's cash price (80,000 → 30,000) instead of flagging that the printed question makes hire purchase cheaper than cash.
21. **Printed p. 72, Activity 6 key Q2:** "SSP. 5500" is the hire-purchase price, not the "how much more did he pay" the question asks (answer: **SSP 500**).
22. **Printed p. 55, probability formula:** "Probability = chance / Number of possible outcomes" — incomplete/wrong; it should be (number of favourable outcomes) ÷ (total number of possible outcomes).
23. **Printed p. 57, Exercise 2 key:** nine answers (a–i) for five questions — the extra four (39.33, 44.67, 36.67, 57.8) belong to nothing in the student book. Same pattern in Exercise 4 (p. 52): ten answers for five questions (the last five — 24, 14, 30, 30, 18 — belong to nothing).
24. **Printed p. 55, 5.6 Graph key:** Q4 (which movie received exactly 10 votes?) is skipped; all values are read off the bar-graph image and unverifiable from the text layer.
25. **Printed p. 31, Exercise 1 key:** "assess the learners understanding of temperature" under a geometry exercise — copy-paste error. Also p. 45: the Venn-diagram pages contain web copy ("Feel free to click on the image…") that makes no sense in print.
