## Grade 8, student — Pupil's Book 8 (© 2018; 115 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 4)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 20 passed, 2 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics, Pupil's Book 8 (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g08-student.pdf` — 115 PDF pages, 3,603,800 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 115 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full, plus visual reads of the figure-heavy pages whose text layer is thin (PDF pp. 22, 24, 26 — perimeter exercises; PDF p. 32 — area conversions; PDF p. 43 — volume conversions; PDF p. 3 — contents page, whose bold unit titles do not extract). No page skipped. The book ends at printed p. 111 (PDF p. 115), mid-exercise (Exercise 7 Q3, poultry account, part c) — there is no closing section and no answers section.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 4 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–4 are cover, foreword, contents pp. i–ii; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 5). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** cover (PDF p. 1) reads "Mathematics Pupil's Book 8", "Funded by:" (funder name not printed), "This book is the property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE", and "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" — so this book DOES carry a © line, unlike the Primary 7 books. **No ISBN and no individual author is printed anywhere.** Foreword (PDF p. 2, full text layer) is the standard series foreword signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, approval effective 4 February 2019, same credits (Longhorn and Mountain Top, Kenya; Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation UK).
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer plus the visual reads listed above. Every worked example's arithmetic was re-checked by hand/calculator; results are under Slips. Where a question depends on a diagram image, I say so and do not claim verification. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the book teaches, unit by unit

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 1–16)
- **1.1 Factors and multiples (pp. 1–3):** multiples (incl. negative multiples), factors (incl. negative factors), factor-pair method for 20. Activity 1 (p. 5): list factors of a chosen number; find squares (21, 453, 17, 27, 19, 221, 305, 41, 34, 635) and values of 23², 18², 51², 32², 65², 39², 47², 33², 36².
- **1.2 Squares and square roots of perfect squares (pp. 4–8):** square as A²; prime-factor method for √81; a Newton/Babylonian averaging method for √484 (estimate 20, 484 ÷ 20 = 24 r 4, average (20 + 24) ÷ 2 = 22) — the arithmetic is right though the instruction "Divide the estimated square root by the number given" is garbled phrasing. Exercise 1 (p. 6): area word problems (square side 156 cm; pond side 126 m; house side 27 m; product of 13² and 15²; garden 31 m). Exercise 2 (p. 7): fill-in roots (√144 = 12 × __; √169 = __ × 13; √225 = 25 × __; √196 = 14 × __; √289 = __ × 17 — note √225 = 15, so "25 × __" is wrong as printed, see Slips) and roots of 441, 576, 1,296, 2,209, 5,041, 2,025. Activity 2 (p. 8): twelve word problems, incl. square area 2,116 cm² → perimeter in metres (46 m side, 184 m perimeter); 5 cowsheds totalling 55,125 m² fenced with 4 strands (8.4 km of wire); √195 + 17² (see Slips — √195 is not whole); sum of 19² and √1,225; √9,216 and √256; √(4,498 + 263) = 69.
- **1.3 Squares and square roots of decimals and fractions (pp. 9–10):** squares of decimals via fractions (0.4² = 0.16); square roots of decimals via numerator/denominator roots (√0.36 = 0.6; √0.0196 = 0.14; √1.44 = 1.2). Exercise 3 (p. 9): squares of 0.5, 0.03, 0.035, 3.06, 0.16, 1.8, 0.25, 0.075, 0.15, 0.27, 4.5, 3.87, 0.23, 0.033, 0.025. Exercise 4 (p. 10): roots of 6.25, 2.25, 0.0169, 0.0144, 3.24, 26.01, 12.96, 3.61, 0.0081, 0.3136, 0.5625, 0.1225.
- **1.4 Conversion of fractions to percentage and percentage to fractions (pp. 11–13):** 3/8 = 37½%; 35% = 7/20; 33⅓% = ⅓. Exercise 5 (pp. 11–12): fractions to % (3/3, 1/3, 5/6, 13/20, 5/8 — see Slips re 3/3) and word problems (13/18 in Kiswahili; 18 girls of 45; 15 of 60 hens sold; 27 of 36 fruits ripe; 180 of 600 cattle dairy; 11 of 30 eggs rotten). Exercise 6 (p. 13): % to fractions (60%, 75%, 90%, 32½%, 27½%, 2¼%, 37½%, 66⅔%).
- **1.5 Conversion of decimals to percentage and percentage to decimals (pp. 13–14):** 0.05 = 5%; 88% = 0.88. Activity 4 (p. 14): decimals to % (0.567, 0.4, 0.036, 0.48, 0.135, 1.75, 0.23, 2.8, 0.25, 3.75). Exercise 7 (p. 14): % to decimals (77%, 135%, 265%, 1%, 857%, 13%, 175%, 8%, 19%, 9%).
- **1.6 Application of fractions, decimals and percentage (pp. 15–16):** real-life uses (chef, architect, pizza, germs) — the "percent" paragraph claims restaurants "use percent when they make a pizza so that they can cut it into equal pieces", which is a weak/confused example. Exercise 8 (p. 16): 50% off as a fraction (½); one in five as % (20%); ¼ as a decimal (0.25); 25% tax as a fraction (¼); 1/10 deposit as % (10%); ⅓ off as % (33⅓%); 1.75 m as a fraction (1¾).

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 17–46)
- **2.1 Perimeter (pp. 17–27):** perimeter of rectangle (P = 2(L + W); example 10 × 6 = 32 cm), square (P = 4L), triangle (P = a + b + c; example 12 + 13 + 5 = 30 cm), trapezium (example 15 + 27 + 14 + 10 = 66 cm), circle (C = πd; C = 2πr; π ≈ 22/7 or 3.14; example d = 7 → 22 cm; r = 7 → 44 cm), semicircle (C = ½πd + d; example d = 14 → 36 cm). Exercises 1–5 and Activities 1–5: rectangle perimeters (15×25, 18×12, 36×14 — read visually, p. 18); rugby field 90 × 75, seven laps; fencing 800 × 650; poles 50 m apart round a 150 × 100 plot; square perimeters (18, 25, 36 cm — read visually, p. 20); triangle perimeters (3+3+4, 3+4+5, 7+10+11, 8+7+4 — read visually, p. 22); circle radius/diameter/circumference table (p. 25, partly garbled in extraction); semicircle exercises; motorcyclist round a 49 m course five times; poles every 10 m round a 35 m field.
- **2.2 Area (pp. 28–36):** m²↔cm² (×/÷ 10,000), are (100 m²), hectare (10,000 m²); rectangle A = L × W; square A = L²; right-angled triangle A = ½bh ("The height and the base are the two sides which form a rich angle" — typo for "right angle", p. 30); circle A = πr² (example r = 14 → 616 cm²); semicircle A = ½πr² (example d = 14 → 77 cm²) — but the formula is mislabelled "Circumference" and written "A = ½ π2" (see Slips); trapezium A = (a + b)/2 × h (example (15 + 27)/2 × 10 = 210 cm²). Exercises 6–8 and Activities 7–9: triangle height from area 150 cm² and base 20 cm; triangular plot base 12 m, hypotenuse 13 m (needs Pythagoras, not yet taught); circle areas (r = 21, d = 30, semicircle d = 40; course r = 49; lake d = 7 km; r = 4 cm; field area 242 m² → circumference; semicircular disk d = 7 cm "in square metres"); trapezium field 280 m/160 m, 3.3 ha → width 150 m; ten composite-shape area problems (pp. 34–36) depending on diagrams — quarter-circle + triangle, flower garden, cow shed (semicircle + trapezium), potato garden (two semicircles), square + two semicircles, shaded figure with a circle in a semicircle, isosceles triangle + square + quarter-circle, cardboard with two opposite semicircles.
- **2.3 Surface area of a cube and cuboid (pp. 37–38):** closed cube 6S² (example side 8 m → 384 m²); cuboid formula as printed is WRONG (see Slips — "2(L×W) + 2(L×W) + 2(L×W)"). Activity 10 (p. 38): surface areas of four pictured cubes/cuboids (2 m × 5 m × 3 m closed; 8 cm × 7½ cm × 4⅕ cm open on top; 3½ m cube closed; 0.5 m cube closed); closed cuboid 4 m × 1.5 m × 2 m vs open cuboid 8 cm × 6 cm × 5 cm; open tin 12 × 9 × 7 cm painted outside; cuboid 4½ × 5½ × 7¾ cm with base and top unpainted (see Slips — "base … measures … by … by …"); open tank volume 48.6 m³, square base, height 5.4 m.
- **2.4 Converting m³ to cm³ (p. 39):** ×/÷ 1,000,000 in the worked examples (13.8 m³ = 13,800,000 cm³; 0.075 m³ = 75,000 cm³; 1,500 cm³ = 0.0015 m³; 28,450 cm³ = 0.02845 m³) — but the rule sentence for cm³→m³ says "divide the value given by 10000" (see Slips; verified visually on the printed page).
- **2.5 Volume of a cube and cuboid (pp. 40–43):** V = base area × height; cube 15 cm → 3,375 cm³; cuboid 12 × 11 × 8 → 1,056 cm³. Cylinder volume (V = πr²h; example r = 14, h = 15 → 9,240 cm³) appears on p. 42 with no section heading and no TOC entry. Exercise 9 (p. 41): volumes of pictured solids; two cubes 8 cm and 11 cm; juice container 350 m² × 20 m into 250 cm³ packets (28,000,000 — see Slips); tank 72.9 m³, square base, height 8.1 m → side 3 m; tank 45 × 25 cm, ¾ full, no height given (see Slips); cube tank 7.5 m, 14,600 cm³ removed, level "becomes 5cm high" (see Slips — impossible as stated); 0.09 m³ into 15 cm³ containers (6,000); two juice companies' October output (13.33 m³). Activity 11 (p. 43): cylinder volumes from diagrams (incl. halves of cylinders).
- **2.6 Problems involving Time, speed and distance (pp. 44–46):** T = D ÷ S, S = D ÷ T, D = S × T; m/s ↔ km/h (×36/10, ×10/36); worked conversion 15 m/s = 54 km/h (messy working, right answer). Exercise 10 (pp. 45–46): ten multi-step journey problems — cyclist 18 min at 36 km/h home→school, 20 min back (average speed homeward 9 m/s); motorist 510 km, 7:15 a.m. → 12:55 p.m. (90 km/h; town named B then D, see Slips); driver 450 km at 90 km/h, rest 25 min, then 60 km/h; relay race 100 m in 3 min + 100 m in 5 min + rest in 2 min (0.5 m/s); Ayesha 1⅓ h at 9 km/h, rest 20 min, 3 h at 4 2/15 km/h; Imran 75 km/h from R at 7:15, Saima 9 km/h from S at 8:00, meet 225 km from R (distance R–S = 245.25 km); motorist 80 km/h, 300 km, rest ¼ h after 2¼ h; two buses 48 min vs 40 min (16 km/h difference); motorbike 450 km at 90 then 60 (average 72 km/h); Abdallah 300 km, 8:15 a.m. → midday (80 km/h; destination named Juba then Kisumu, see Slips).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 47–64)
- **3.1 Inscribing and circumscribing circles of triangles (pp. 47–56):** incentre (angle bisectors), circumcentre (perpendicular bisectors); construction steps for both; Activity 1 (p. 49) and Activity 2 (p. 51) — construct inscribed/circumscribed circles, explain why incentre/circumcentre are equidistant. Exercise 1 (p. 52): food truck equidistant from three locations (circumcentre); school equidistant from three roads (incentre). Activity 3 (p. 53): construct triangles PQR (QR 4.5 cm, QP 6.9 cm, angle PQR 100°) and ABC (AB 6 cm, CA 9 cm, angle BCA 140°) with circumscribed/inscribed circles. Pythagoras' theorem (pp. 53–55): c² = a² + b²; examples 6-8-10 and 24-x-26 (x = 10). Exercise 2 (p. 56): find x in two pictured triangles; wheelchair ramp with "only 10 metres of space" and no height (see Slips); roof frame 9 m tall, 30 m wide (diagonal ≈ 31.6 m); which set makes a right-angled triangle — (A) 4.5, 6, 9 (no: 4.5² + 6² = 56.25 ≠ 81); (B) 3.75, 5.25, 6 (no); (C) 2.25, 3, 3.75 (yes: 5.0625 + 9 = 14.0625 = 3.75²); (D) 5.25, 9, 11.25 (no: 27.5625 + 81 = 108.5625 ≠ 126.5625); ladder against a 24 m wall, 7 m out (length 25 m — the question asks for the ladder's "height", see Slips).
- **3.2 Pyramids and prisms (pp. 56–59):** faces/edges/vertices; prism has two bases, pyramid one; type named by the base. Activity 4 (p. 57): count faces/vertices/edges of a "triangular square based pyramid" (see Slips) and a triangular prism. Exercise 3 (pp. 58–59): six net problems — square + 4 equilateral triangles (square pyramid); two more nets to identify; a net's edge count and pyramid-or-prism; which of four figures is a triangular prism net; vertex count of a folded net. All depend on diagrams.
- **3.3 Scale (pp. 60–62):** scale as a ratio; example: road 3 km drawn as 8 cm → 12 cm represents 4.5 km (the book's working says 4.4 km — see Slips). Activity 5 (p. 61): draw school compound, classroom, playing ground at 1:100. Exercise 4 (pp. 61–62): ten scale problems, incl. 8.5 km drawn as 17 cm (1:50,000); 5 cm = 75 km (1:1,500,000); 1:100,000, plot 70 m × 50 m, "area of the triangular plot on the map" (see Slips — rectangular plot called triangular); 7.5 cm = 90 km (1:1,200,000); 1:15,000, 13.5 cm → 2.025 km; 1 cm = 25 m, 1 ha on the drawing (16 cm²); 3 cm at 1:125,000 (3.75 km); 9 cm × 4 cm at 1:100,000 (9 km × 4 km); 1:800, 17 cm → 136 m; 72 km drawn as 18 cm (1:400,000).
- **3.4 Coordinates (pp. 62–64):** ordered pairs, negative values, four quadrants (table: I +/+, II −/+, III −/−, IV +/−; "numbered in a counterclockwise direction" — correct), point (12, 5), point A (3, 2) in Quadrant I, point C (−2, −1) in Quadrant III. No exercises on coordinates — the section ends at p. 64 with no activity or exercise.

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 65–75)
- **4.1 Simplifying algebraic expressions (pp. 65–66):** collecting like terms; worked examples incl. ½(5x + 7y) + ¼(3x − 6y) = 13/4 x + 2y ✓ and (15a + 12b − 6a + 8b)/(4(3a − 2b) + 13b) = (9a + 20b)/(12a + 5b) ✓. Activity 1 (p. 66): seven simplification items.
- **4.2 Word statements into algebraic expressions (pp. 67–68):** father five times daughter's age, sum 42 → daughter 7; Maryanne n mangoes, Winnie n/2, Debborah 2n, total 28 → n = 8; rectangle length = width + 12, perimeter 64 → width 4 cm, length 16 cm, area 64 cm² (the working writes "P = 2(l + l)" — typo for l + w). Activity 2 (p. 68): mother six times son, sum 63; square perimeter 36 cm; David's age ÷ 3 = David's age − 14 (age 21).
- **4.3 Sets (pp. 69–70):** set, element, ∈/∉; sets of numbers (ℕ, ℤ, ℤ₀⁺, ℚ). Exercise 1 (p. 70): list elements of 14 sets (even numbers < 12; primes 1–29; integers between −2 and 2; letters of LOYAL; vowels of CHOICE; factors of 36; {x ∈ N, 5 < x < 12}; multiples of 3 < 21; perfect cubes 27 < x < 216; {x = 5n − 3, n ∈ W, n < 3}; positive integers with x² < 40; divisors of 18; {x: x + 1 = 1}; colours of the rainbow). Then a second exercise mis-numbered "4." (p. 70): "Write each of the following sets" for 12 roster sets — incl. B = {l, 2, 3, 6, 9, 18} ("l" for 1) and I = {a, e, i, 0, u} ("0" for "o") — see Slips.
- **4.4 Finite Sets & Infinite Sets (pp. 71–75):** finite/infinite sets; cardinal number n(A); empty set ϕ, n(ϕ) = 0; equal sets; universal set U; Venn diagrams — the universal set is said to be "denoted by the symbol (epsilon)" (see Slips — it should be U or ξ); "Notice you don't use capitals within the brackets" for {tom, lucy, marie} (see Slips — misleading). Worked Venn example: A = {1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12}, B = {2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13}; intersection {6, 7, 9, 12} ✓; union 1–13 ✓. Exercise 3 (p. 74): list items in a pictured Venn diagram; intersection and union. Activity 3 (p. 75): three-exam Venn problem (150 learners; only E&M 15, only S&M 20, only S&E 18, all three 8; Science 65, English 55, Maths 72) → 27 sat none.

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 76–92)
- **5.1 Data collection process (pp. 76–77):** six steps (identify issues; select issues and set goals; plan approach and methods; collect data; analyse and interpret; act on results). A graph comparing 2010 and 2020 (p. 76) is pictured but its data is not given in the text.
- **5.2 Reading and interpreting tables and graphs (pp. 78–87):** attendance table (class of 50; most absentees Wednesday, 3); distance table D–F–H (88 + 140 = 228 km); bus-fare table (teacher + 3 pupils, A→C→F, child fare half adult — table values in the text layer are jumbled, the question is only answerable from the pictured table); football points table (3 for a win, 1 for a draw); sugar production/sales table (find the day sales were 1¾× production); bread sales by day (Monday vs Saturday takings at SSP 100/loaf); agricultural show attendance (adults vs children); bus timetable J–R (K→Q travel time 6 h 45 min); pupils present Mon–Fri (class of 70; Tuesday absentees 13); milk sales (total SSP 5,850 at SSP 50/litre → 117 litres; Friday vs Tuesday); primary 5–8 enrolment 2011–2014 (dropouts from the 2011 P5 cohort by 2014: 81 → 73, so 8). Pie-chart worked example (pp. 82–83): 10,800 animals; gazelles 240° = 7,200; wild beasts 50° = 1,500; difference 5,700 (the working multiplies by 500 instead of 50 — see Slips). Activity 2 (p. 83): colour preferences pie chart (14, 16, 12, 26, 22 — blue vs green angle difference); Taban's salary pie chart (spent SSP 8,000 on food); estate pie chart (600 girls; "how many more boys than men" — see Slips). Exercise 2 (pp. 85–86): exercise books pie chart (16, 14, 15, 18, 27); boys' angle for 210 girls/168 boys/336 men/126 women (72°); Kenyi's test scores (2nd + 5th angles = 96°); Nyandeng's farm income pie chart (maize 72° — the table is incomplete in the text layer, see Slips); 432 fruits circle graph (oranges vs pawpaw — image-only); Eunice's salary pie chart (SSP 2,400 on clothing; "how much less on transport than on rent" — see Slips, transport is the larger share). Activity 3 (p. 87): land use (homestead ¼ ha, tea 1 ha, maize 1¼ ha, grazing 1½ ha) as a bar graph. Exercise 3 (pp. 87–88): two travel graphs (motorist R→S and back vs cyclist; Akuol vs Kenyi) — image-only questions.
- **5.3 Probability (pp. 88–92):** chance vocabulary (impossible, certain, equally likely, even chance, unlikely, likely); card example (not a spade: 39 cards — the probability 39/52 = ¾ is never stated); two-dice odd-sum example ("18 combinations … so:" — the probability ½ is never stated); coin-flip prediction vs outcome discussion (Example 4, p. 90 — mis-numbered, a second "Example 4"); Activities 4–7: toss a coin 20 times; roll a die 48 times; toss a bottle top 20 times; two dice, product odd → player X wins, product even → player Y wins (unfair: ¼ vs ¾). Exercise 4 (p. 92): probabilities as fractions — red marble 2/8 = ¼; lollipop 5/9; bean 2/8 = ¼; white crayon 9/19; blue marble 11/26. (Note: "5 lollipops and 4 candies marbles" is garbled.)

#### Unit 6: BUSINESS ACCOUNTING (printed pp. 93–111)
- **6.1 Commissions (pp. 93–94):** commission = R% × value sold; example: 15% of SSP 25,000 = SSP 3,750. Exercise 1 (p. 94): Nadia (salary 25,200 + 9% of 150,000 = 38,700); Michael (27,000 + 6½% of (140,000 − 10,000) = 35,450); George (12.5% of (175,000 − 25,000) = 18,750); salesperson (5% of 600 × 1,500 = 45,000); Khamis (30,000 + 3% of nothing, since 125,000 < 150,000 = 30,000).
- **6.2 Discounts (p. 95):** discount = marked − selling; example: 15,000 − 13,910 = 1,090. Activity 1 (p. 95): dress 810 → 650 (discount 160); wardrobe 3,600 → 2,750.50 (discount 849.50); item bought for 750 after discount 150 (marked 900); radio 8,500 after discount 95, what if discount were 115 (20 less).
- **6.3 Hire purchase (pp. 95–97):** HP price = deposit + instalments; example: bicycle HP 7,500, deposit 1,000, 10 instalments of 650. Exercise 2 (pp. 96–97): sewing machine HP = 125% of cash, deposit 13,500 + 9 × 1,500 (cash price 21,600); radio deposit 45,000 + 18 × 850, total 25% more than cash (cash 48,240); laptop deposit 20,000, total paid 27,680, 8 instalments (960 each); dining table HP = 120% of marked, deposit 8,000 + instalment 5,000 "each" — number of instalments never stated (see Slips); TV deposit 120,000 + 12 × 850, HP 20% more than cash (cash 108,500; more paid 21,700) — the names change mid-question (Philip / She / Alai / Sylvia, see Slips); cooker cash 150,000, HP 10% more, deposit 90,000, 18 instalments (monthly 4,166.67); motorcycle marked 300,000, 8% cash discount (276,000), HP 120,000 + 10 × 18,000 = 300,000 (saves 24,000 by cash).
- **6.4 Profit and loss (pp. 97–98):** profit = SP − BP; loss = BP − SP. Example 4 (p. 97): basin bought SSP 175, sold SSP 208 — the solution uses "Buying price = SSP172" and gives profit SSP 36 (see Slips — correct profit is SSP 33). Example 5: radio 720 → 630, loss 90 ✓. Exercise 3 (p. 98): eggs (8 trays × 240, 8 broken, sold at 8/egg, 30/tray → loss 64); chickens (250 × 1.5 kg × 150 = 56,250 cost; 250 × 215 = 53,750 → loss 2,500, but the question asks "what profit" — see Slips); fruit bags (15 × 450 + 500 transport; 1½ bags spoilt; sold at 400/bag → loss 1,850); eggs (9 trays × 200; one tray broken; sold at 205/tray → loss 160).
- **6.5 Simple interest (pp. 99–102):** SI = PRT/100; amount = P + I; time in days ÷ 365. Example 6 (p. 100): 15,000 at 12% for 2½ years → SI 4,500, amount 19,500 ✓ (a garbled character "����" appears in the amount line — a font/encoding defect in the PDF). Example 7 (p. 101): loan 500,000, interest 25,000 in 6 months → rate 10% p.a. ✓. Exercise 4 (p. 101): Maryanne 325,000 at 18.5% for 5 years (amount 625,625); David 200,500 at 12.5%, interest 100,250 → time 4 years. Activity 2 (p. 102): eight rate/principal/time problems (incl. Richard deposits 5,400, gets back 6,000 after 2 years → 5.56% p.a.).
- **6.6 Compound interest (pp. 103–107):** CI = final amount − principal; year-by-year method. Example 8 (p. 103): 12,000 at 5% for 2 years → 13,230 ✓. Example 9 (p. 104): 10,000 at 12% for 2 years → 12,544 (interest 2,544) — but the "formula" block printed underneath ("CI = P × R1/100 × R2/100 × T", "CI = 10,000 × 112/100 × 112/100 × 1 = 12,544") is nonsense (see Slips; it computes the amount, not the CI). Exercise 5 (p. 105): nine CI problems — incl. Q3 ("deposited for 2 years … at the end of 3 years" — see Slips) and Q5 ("deposited … How much money did he pay back" — wrong verb). Example 10 (p. 106): 8,000 at 10% for 1½ years → 9,240, CI 1,240 ✓ (the heading says "for years" — the number is missing, see Slips). Exercise 6 (p. 107): nine harder CI problems — half-yearly compounding (12,000 at 10%, 30 months → 15,313.69); sum from two years' interest 2,652 at 10% (12,628.57 — not a clean number, see Slips); 2nd-year interest exceeds 1st by 96 at 8% (sum 15,000); 8,000 invested yearly at 10% (26,480 at the start of year 3); 13,500 at 16% for 5 years; TV-set debt (8,748 − 6,248 = 2,500); asset depreciating 20% yearly, value 10,240 after 2 years (original 16,000); sum amounting to 4,928 at 10% then 12% (4,000); Joan's account with yearly 4,000 deposits (37,440 on 1 Jan 2012).
- **6.7 Cash accounts (pp. 108–111):** two-column cash account (cash in / cash out; date, particulars, money); balancing procedure (total in = total out + balance). Example 10 (pp. 108–109): Mark's market account — capital 17,000; purchases 2,400 + 1,000 + 2,000; sales 5,000 + 2,400 + 4,000; transport 700; market fee 500; balance 21,800; profit 4,800 — all correct. Exercise 7 (pp. 110–111): shopkeeper's account (balance 1 May 2017: 121,760 — the weeks are listed out of order, 17 April before 14 April); carpenter's account (balance 1 Feb 2016: 20,440); poultry account (balance 90,300; "layers marsh" for "layers mash" throughout). The book ends mid-exercise (Q3 part c unfinished); no closing section, no answers.

### What the book does NOT teach (honest negatives)
- No HCF/LCM or divisibility tests (covered in Primary 5–7, not revised here); no prime factorisation beyond the square-root method; no cube roots; no indices/laws of exponents; no simultaneous equations; no quadratic expressions; no trigonometric ratios (Pythagoras only); no angle theorems beyond the transversal material carried from Primary 7; no construction of angles or bisectors (only inscribed/circumscribed circles); no translation/reflection/rotation; no bearings; no loci; no matrices; no statistics beyond reading tables/graphs and simple probability (no mean/median/mode in this book at all — those were Primary 7); no grouped data; no probability beyond single-event fractions (no combined events, no tree diagrams, no odds); no VAT/tax calculations (tax is mentioned only as a word problem in Unit 1); no bank statements or ledgers beyond the single cash-account format; no depreciation beyond one exercise question; no answers section and no unit revision exercises anywhere in the book.

### Held-copy defects
- The TOC's bold unit-title lines (UNIT 1: NUMBERS etc.) do not extract in the text layer — verified visually: they ARE printed (PDF p. 3), so this is a font/extraction artefact, not a print defect.
- PDF p. 32 (printed p. 28): the text layer misreads the fraction denominator as 100000; visually the printed fraction is 28,450/10,000 = 2.845 ✓ — extraction artefact only.
- A garbled character ("����") in the amount line of Example 6 (printed p. 100) — a font/encoding defect in the PDF.
- Otherwise no missing pages (printed pp. 1–111 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–115 complete), no blurred pages. The degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer in places (e.g. "3600" for 360°) — extraction artefact, not a print error. Many exercise tables and diagrams are images with values only in the figure (pp. 18, 20, 22, 25, 34–36, 43, 58–59, 74, 79–88, 91) — the questions are only fully answerable from the printed figures, which I read visually where the text layer was thin.

### Slips and wrong worked answers (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 39, §2.4 rule sentence:** "To convert cm³ to m³, divide the value given by 10000" — WRONG; it must be divided by **1,000,000**. The worked examples on the same page divide by 1,000,000 and are correct, so the rule contradicts its own examples. (Verified visually on the printed page.)
2. **Printed p. 37, cuboid surface-area formula:** "Surface area of a closed cuboid = 2(L×W) + 2(L×W) + 2(L×W)" — WRONG; it must be **2(LW + LH + WH)**. The formula repeats LW three times and never uses the height.
3. **Printed p. 97, Example 4 (profit):** basin bought for SSP 175, sold for SSP 208 — the solution writes "Buying price = SSP172" and gives profit **SSP 36**. Correct profit is **SSP 33** (208 − 175). The 172 is a typo that changes the answer.
4. **Printed p. 60, Example 3 (scale):** road 3 km drawn as 8 cm → 12 cm represents **4.5 km** (0.375 × 12), not the printed **4.4 km** (the book's own working, 1 cm = 375 m, gives 4,500 m).
5. **Printed p. 104, Example 9 (compound interest) formula block:** "CI = P × R1/100 × R2/100 × T" and "CI = 10,000 × 112/100 × 112/100 × 1 = 12,544" — nonsense as a formula: 12,544 is the AMOUNT, not the compound interest (CI = 2,544), and "The time (T) is multiplied by 1 since we are squaring the rate" is garbled. The step-by-step working above it (1,200 + 1,344 = 2,544) is correct.
6. **Printed p. 7, Exercise 2 Q1c:** "√225 = 25 × ____" — WRONG as printed; √225 = 15, so it should be "15 × ____" (or "√225 = ____ × 15"). The other four fill-ins are right (√144 = 12 × 1; √169 = 1 × 13; √196 = 14 × 1; √289 = 1 × 17).
7. **Printed p. 48, inscribing a circle:** "The segment connecting the incentre with the point of intersection of the triangle and the bisector is the radius of the circle" — WRONG; the radius is the **perpendicular distance from the incentre to a side**, not the segment to where the bisector meets the side. The book's own next step (construct a perpendicular from the incentre to a side) contradicts this sentence.
8. **Printed p. 32, semi-circle area:** the formula is headed "Circumference = ½ × pi × (Radius)²" — it is the AREA formula, mislabelled — and written "A = ½ π2" (missing r²). The worked example (½ × 22/7 × 7² = 77 cm²) is correct.
9. **Printed p. 41, Exercise 9 Q5:** "A rectangular tank 45cm long and 25cm wide was ¾ full of water. What is the volume of water required to fill the tank?" — NO HEIGHT/DEPTH is given; unsolvable as printed.
10. **Printed p. 41, Exercise 9 Q6:** "A cube-shaped tank of 7.5m was full of water. After removing 14600cm³ of water the level of water become 5cm high. What was the height of the container?" — impossible as stated: removing 0.0146 m³ from a 7.5 m cube (base 56.25 m²) lowers the level by 0.26 mm, not to 5 cm; the question is garbled.
11. **Printed p. 41, Exercise 9 Q3:** a "rectangular container with a base area of 350 m² and a height of 20 m" holding juice — 7,000 m³, giving 28,000,000 packets of 250 cm³. Arithmetically consistent but the scale is absurd for a juice container.
12. **Printed p. 11, Exercise 5 Q1a:** "Convert these fractions to percentages: a) 3/3" — 3/3 = 100% is trivial and almost certainly a misprint (the surrounding items are 1/3, 5/6, 13/20, 5/8).
13. **Printed p. 8, Activity 2 Q5:** "What is the value of √195 + 17²?" — √195 is not a whole number (≈ 13.96) in a perfect-squares section; almost certainly meant √196 (then the answer is 14 + 289 = 303).
14. **Printed p. 19, Activity 1 Q3:** "How many yards does she jog if she goes around the field two times?" — the field is given as 80 m by 120 m (metres), with no metres→yards conversion given; two laps = 800 m, not a number of yards.
15. **Printed p. 21, Activity 2 Q1:** "A cricket player makes four runs around a pitch of length 90m" — only the length is given, no width; unsolvable as printed.
16. **Printed p. 25, Exercise 4 Q2:** "a) What is the radius? b) Circumference? c) Radius?" — part (c) repeats part (a).
17. **Printed p. 56, Exercise 2 Q2:** "A wheelchair ramp is needed… There is only 10 metres of space available for the ramp. How long should the ramp be?" — no height is given; unsolvable as printed.
18. **Printed p. 56, Exercise 2 Q5:** "What was the height of the ladder?" — the ladder's LENGTH is wanted (25 m from a 24 m wall and 7 m out); "height" is the wrong word.
19. **Printed p. 57, Activity 4 Q1:** "The diagram below represents a triangular square based pyramid" — contradictory ("triangular" vs "square based").
20. **Printed p. 61, Exercise 4 Q3:** "an actual length of a right-angled plot of land measures 70m by 50m. What was the area of the triangular plot on the map?" — the plot is rectangular, not triangular; and at 1:100,000 the map drawing would be 0.07 cm × 0.05 cm, absurdly small.
21. **Printed p. 68, Example 4 working:** "P = 2(l + l)" — should be **P = 2(l + w)** (the substitution that follows uses w + 12 + w and is correct).
22. **Printed p. 70, Exercise "4." items (b) and (i):** B = "{l, 2, 3, 6, 9, 18}" — the letter "l" is printed where the digit **1** belongs; I = "{a, e, i, 0, u}" — the digit **0** is printed where the letter **o** belongs. (Also the exercise is mis-numbered "4." — it follows Exercise 1 with no 2 or 3.)
23. **Printed p. 73, Venn diagrams:** "A Venn diagram begins with a box called our universal set, which is denoted by the symbol (epsilon)" — WRONG: the universal set is denoted **U** (or ξ, xi), not epsilon. And "Notice you don't use capitals within the brackets" for {tom, lucy, marie} is misleading — set elements may be capitalised, and the book itself uses capitals for set names.
24. **Printed p. 83, pie-chart worked example:** "1° = 10800/360° × 500 = 1500 Wild beasts" — the working multiplies by **500** where it should be **50°** (the angle for wild beasts); the answer 1,500 happens to be right (30 × 50), but the printed working is wrong.
25. **Printed p. 84, Activity 2 Q3:** "If there were 600 girls, how many more boys than men were there?" — from the pictured angles (girls 60°, boys 40°, men 80°) there are 400 boys and 800 men, i.e. 400 FEWER boys than men; the question presupposes more boys than men.
26. **Printed p. 86, Exercise 2 Q6:** "If she spends SSP 2,400 on clothing every month, how much less does she spend on transport than on rent?" — from the pictured angles (clothing 40°, transport 150°, rent 30°) transport (SSP 9,000) is MORE than rent (SSP 1,800), not less; the question is backwards.
27. **Printed p. 89, Examples 4 and 5 (probability):** both end without stating the probability — "not drawing a spade" gives 39 cards but never 39/52 = ¾; "sum of two dice is odd" gives 18 combinations but never 18/36 = ½. (Also the second "Example 4" on p. 90 is mis-numbered — there are two Example 4s in Unit 5.)
28. **Printed p. 96, Exercise 2 Q4:** "The hire purchase price of a dining table is 120% of the marked price. The hire purchase price is a deposit of SSP 8 000 and an instalment of SSP 5 000 each." — the NUMBER of instalments is never stated; unsolvable as printed.
29. **Printed p. 97, Exercise 2 Q5:** names change mid-question — "Philip bought a TV set… She paid… How much more did Alai pay… Sylvia bought the same TV set on cash" — three names (Philip, Alai, Sylvia) for two buyers, and a pronoun change (Philip → She).
30. **Printed p. 98, Exercise 3 Q2:** "He then sold each chicken for SSP 215, what profit did Jacob make?" — there is no profit: cost 56,250, takings 53,750, a LOSS of SSP 2,500; the question asks for a profit that does not exist.
31. **Printed p. 105, Exercise 5 Q3:** "A trader deposited SSP18,000 for 2 years in a bank paying compound interest at the rate of 8% P.a. How much did she save in her account at the end of 3 years?" — 2 years vs 3 years mismatch. Q5: "Abdi deposited SSP 480,000… How much money did he pay back after 1½ years?" — "pay back" is the wrong verb for a deposit.
32. **Printed p. 106, Example 10 heading:** "Calculate the amount and the compound interest on SSP 8000 for years at 10% per annum compounded yearly for 1½ years" — the number of years is missing from "for years".
33. **Printed p. 107, Exercise 6 Q2:** "the interest for the first year plus the interest for the second year is SSP 2652" at 10% — the sum is 12,628.57, not a clean number (12,000 would give 2,520); the figure looks mistyped.
34. **Printed p. 45, Exercise 10 Q2:** the motorist leaves "for town B" but "arriving in town D" — the destination changes name mid-question. Same pattern in Q10 (p. 46): Abdallah travels "for Juba" but "reached Kisumu at midday".
35. **Printed p. 30:** "The height and the base are the two sides which form a rich angle" — "rich" for **right**. Printed p. 111: "layers marsh" (three times) for **layers mash**.
36. **Printed p. 20, Example 2 working:** "P = 4L = 8cm" — the working line equates the perimeter to 8 cm; it should read "P = 4 × 8cm". The final answer (32 cm) is right.

### Citation anchors (checked 2026-11-05 — see the check line at the top of this section)
1. Multiples of 3 include negative numbers: …, −9, −6, −3, 0, 3, 6, 9, … — printed p. 1.
2. Newton/averaging method for √484: estimate 20, (20 + 24) ÷ 2 = 22 — printed p. 7.
3. Squares of decimals: 0.4² = 0.16 — printed p. 9.
4. Square roots of decimals: √0.0196 = 0.14 — printed p. 10.
5. Fraction to percentage: 3/8 = 37½% — printed p. 11.
6. Percentage to fraction: 33⅓% = ⅓ — printed p. 13.
7. Perimeter of a rectangle: P = 2(L + W); 10 cm × 6 cm gives 32 cm — printed p. 18.
8. Circumference of a circle: C = πd; d = 7 cm gives 22 cm — printed p. 24.
9. Semi-circle circumference: C = ½πd + d; d = 14 cm gives 36 cm — printed p. 26.
10. Area of a circle: A = πr²; r = 14 cm gives 616 cm² — printed p. 31.
11. Area of a trapezium: (15 + 27)/2 × 10 = 210 cm² — printed p. 33.
12. Surface area of a closed cube: side 8 m gives 384 m² — printed p. 37.
13. Volume of a cylinder: V = πr²h; r = 14 cm, h = 15 cm gives 9,240 cm³ — printed p. 42.
14. Speed conversion: 15 m/s = 54 km/h — printed p. 44.
15. Pythagoras: 6² + 8² = 10² — printed p. 55.
16. Scale: a road 3 km drawn as 8 cm; 12 cm represents 4.5 km (book prints 4.4) — printed p. 60.
17. Coordinates: point (12, 5) is 12 along and 5 up; quadrants numbered counterclockwise — printed pp. 62–63.
18. Algebra: ½(5x + 7y) + ¼(3x − 6y) = 13/4 x + 2y — printed pp. 65–66.
19. Sets: A = {1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12}, B = {2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13}; intersection {6, 7, 9, 12} — printed p. 74.
20. Pie chart: 10,800 animals; gazelles 240° = 7,200 — printed p. 83.
21. Commission: 15% of SSP 25,000 = SSP 3,750 — printed p. 94.
22. Compound interest: SSP 12,000 at 5% for 2 years amounts to SSP 13,230 — printed p. 103.


## Grade 8, teacher guide — Teacher's Guide 8 (© 2018; 80 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 4)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found.

- **Book:** Primary Mathematics 8, Teacher's Guide (South Sudan series)
- **File read:** `g08-teacher.pdf` — 80 PDF pages, 8,549,083 bytes
- **Pages actually read:** all 80 PDF pages, in order. Text layer read in full, plus a visual read of the one text-empty page (PDF p. 47, printed p. 43 — a montage of two reduced student-book pages). No page skipped. The guide ends at printed p. 76 (PDF p. 80) with the poultry cash account; the student book's Exercise 7 Q3 part (c) (earnings from the poultry farm) has no key.
- **Printed vs PDF numbering:** printed page number = PDF page − 4 (front matter: PDF pp. 1–4 are cover, foreword, contents pp. i–ii; printed p. 1 starts on PDF p. 5). The last printed page is 76 (PDF p. 80). All page numbers below are PRINTED numbers unless marked "PDF".
- **Edition / imprint as printed:** cover (PDF p. 1) reads "Mathematics Teacher's Guide 8", "Funded by:" (funder name not printed), "This book is the property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction. THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE", and "©2018, THE REPUBLIC OF SOUTH SUDAN, MINISTRY OF GENERAL EDUCATION AND INSTRUCTION. All rights reserved…" — so this guide DOES carry a © line, unlike the Primary 7 books. **No ISBN and no individual author is printed anywhere.** The foreword (PDF p. 2, full text layer) is the series foreword adapted to the Teacher's Guide, signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai, approval effective 4 February 2019, same credits (Longhorn and Mountain Top, Kenya; Global Partnership for Education funding; UNICEF; Curriculum Foundation UK).
- **Method:** full read of the extracted text layer plus the visual read listed above. Every answer in the guide's keys was re-checked by hand/calculator against the student book's questions; the results are under Companion check and Slips. Where a key reads values off a graph/diagram image, I say so and do not claim verification. Short quoted fragments below are only to pin down slips; everything else is my own summary.

### What the guide contains, unit by unit

#### Front matter — Introduction (printed pp. 1–5, PDF pp. 5–9)
- Introduction (pp. 1–2): six units; unit overview table (Unit 1 Numbers: complex problems; Unit 2 Measurement: Volumes of solids; Unit 3 Geometry: co-ordinate geometry; Unit 4 Algebra: Algebraic expressions and sets; Unit 5 Statistics: Group data and probability (2); Unit 6 Business accounting).
- Teaching strategies (pp. 2–4): problem-based learning, open-ended questions (same rectangle-perimeter-160 cm example as the Primary 5–7 guides), group work, peer teaching.
- Classroom assessment (p. 5): observation / conversation / product ("triangulation").
- The TOC (printed pp. i–ii) matches the body's section numbering and page numbers throughout.

#### Unit 1: NUMBERS (printed pp. 6–17, PDF pp. 10–21)
- Unit opener (pp. 6–8): "Learn about" promises "multiples and factors of numbers expressed in fraction and decimal forms", "square roots of mixed numbers", and "terminal and recurring decimals" — **none of these are taught in the student book** (no multiples/factors of fractions, no recurring decimals; the only mixed-number root is one item in Activity 2). Outcomes (p. 7) repeat the same promises.
- 1.1 Factors and multiples (p. 8): one line ("Use example 1 and 2 to explain multiples"; "Use the number line to show learners on how to find multiples").
- 1.2 Squares and square roots (pp. 9–12): Activity 1 key (p. 10): squares 441, 205,209, 289, 729, 361, 48,841, 93,025, 1,681, 1,156, 403,225; 23²–36²: 529, 324, 2,601, 1,024, 4,225, 1,521, 2,209, 1,089, 1,296 — all correct. Exercise 1 key (p. 11): 24,336 cm²; 15,876 m²; 729 m²; 38,025; 961 m² — all correct. Exercise 2 key (p. 12): Q1 "a. 12 b. 13 c. 25 d. 14 e. 17" — the guide answers with the ROOTS, not the missing multipliers (see Slips; the student book's Q1c "√225 = 25 × ____" is itself unanswerable as printed); Q2 roots 21, 24, 36, 47, 71, 45 — all correct.
- Activity 2 key (p. 13): 46 cm; 49 m; "3.756594km"; 13; "22"; 396; 22; 13,754; "96 and 16"; 28; 69; 67/10. Q1 (46 cm), Q3 (3.756594 km) and Q5 (22) are wrong (see Slips); the rest are correct.
- 1.3 Squares/roots of decimals and fractions (p. 13): Exercise 3 key (squares of decimals) and Exercise 4 key (roots) — all 27 values correct (0.25, 0.0009, 0.001225, 9.3636, 0.0256, 3.24, 0.0625, 0.005625, 0.0225, 0.0729, 20.25, 14.9769, 0.0529, 0.001089, 0.000625; 2.5, 1.5, 0.13, 0.12, 1.8, 5.1, 3.6, 1.9, 0.09, 0.56, 0.75, 0.35).
- 1.4 Fractions↔percentages (pp. 14–15): Exercise 5 key (p. 14): 100%, 33.33%, 83.33%, 65%, 62.5%, 72%, 60%, 75%, 25%, 70%, 63.33% — all correct. Exercise 6 key (p. 15): 3/5, 3/4, 9/10, 13/40, 11/40, 9/400, 3/8, 2/3 — all correct.
- 1.5 Decimals↔percentages (pp. 15–16): the conversion method as printed is garbled ("write the figure in decimal without the decimal point and divide it by 100" — that would give the wrong answer; the key itself uses the correct method). Activity 3 key (p. 15): 56.7%, 40%, 3.6%, 48%, 13.5%, 175%, 23%, 280%, 25%, 375% — all correct. Exercise 7 key (p. 16): 0.77, 1.35, 2.65, 0.01, 8.57, 0.13, 1.75, 0.08, 0.19, 0.09 — all correct.
- 1.6 Application (p. 17): Exercise 8 key: ½; 20%; 0.25; ¼; 10%; 33.33%; 1 75/100 — all correct.

#### Unit 2: MEASUREMENT (printed pp. 18–33, PDF pp. 22–37)
- Unit opener (pp. 18–20): "Learn about" promises surface areas and volumes of "spheres, cylinders, cones, triangular prism and square based pyramid" — the student book teaches only cubes, cuboids and cylinders. Outcomes (p. 19) include "Solving problems involving; commissions and discounts, hire purchase, profit and loss, simple interest and compound interest" — that is Unit 6, not Unit 2.
- 2.1 Perimeter (pp. 20–23): formulas restated. Exercise 1 key (p. 21): 80 cm, 60 cm, 100 cm; 2,310 (no unit); 2,900 m; 10 poles — all correct. Exercise 2 key (p. 22): 72 cm, 100 cm, 144 cm — correct. Activity 2 key (p. 22): "1. 360m" — the student book's question is unsolvable as printed (pitch length only, no width); the guide just multiplies 4 × 90. "2. 3200m" — correct for one strand round an 800 m square field. "Exercise 5" key (pp. 22–23): 169.668 cm; 94.26 m; 125.68 m; 1,540 m; 46.278 m; "D = 24.507cm; R = 12.25cm" (see Slips — D should be 24.5 cm, and R is inconsistent with the guide's own D); 11 poles. Activity 4 key (p. 23): "63cm" — matches the second figure (18 + 25 + 10 + 10). Exercise 6 guidance (p. 23) is garbled ("choose two shapes… square and trapezium… decide which fact book provides the relevant information") and gives NO answers on that page; the answers (15 cm; 30 m² — both correct) appear on p. 26, split from their introduction.
- 2.2 Area (pp. 24–28): formulas restated correctly (trapezium (a + b) × h / 2). Activity 7 key (p. 25): 108 m²; 520,000 m²; 6,750 m² — correct. Activity 8 key (p. 25): 625 cm²; 324 cm²; 1,296 cm²; 13 m — correct. Exercise 6 key (p. 26): 15 cm; 30 m² — correct (see the split-layout note above). Exercise 7 key (p. 27): 1,386 cm²; 707.143 cm²; 628.57 cm²; 7,546 m²; 38.5 km²; 50.286 cm²; 55.15 m; 19.25 cm² — the values are correct, but Q6 answers in cm² where the student book asks "in square metres" (0.001925 m²). Activity 9 key (p. 27): "1. 150m²" — the question asks for the WIDTH (150 m), so the unit is wrong; "2. 820cm²" — image-dependent, unverifiable. Exercise 8 key (p. 28): ten composite-area answers (1.225 m² / 12,250 cm²; 307.1 m²; 431.44 m²; 471 m²; 134.375 m²; 227.5 m²; 37.44 m²; 19.25 cm²; 4,256 cm²; 7.735 m) — all read off diagram images, unverifiable from the text layer; Q8's 19.25 cm² is the same number as the semicircle area in Exercise 7 Q6, which is suspicious.
- 2.3 Surface area (p. 29): Activity 10 key: 1a) 62 m² ✓; 1b) "73,5m²" (see Slips — this is the answer for item c, the 3½ m cube); 1c) "225.75cm²" (matches no item — the open cuboid should be 190.2 cm²); 1d) 1.5 m² ✓; Q2 "339812cm²" ✓; Q3 "402cm²" ✓; Q4 "155 cm²" (see Slips — this is the LATERAL area, i.e. the painted parts, not the unpainted base + top = 49.5 cm² the question asks for); Q5 "45m²" (wrong — should be 73.8 m²).
- 2.4 Converting m³ to cm³ (pp. 30–31): the guide REPEATS the student book's wrong rule — "To convert cm³ to m³, divide the value given by 10000" — and prints "1m³ = 10000cm³" inside Example 14 Q2 while the working divides by 1,000,000 (see Slips).
- 2.5 Volume (pp. 31–32): Exercise 9 key (p. 31): 15 m³; 0.3 m³; 1,843 cm³; 28,000,000 packets; 3 m; "¼ xh (45 x 25) cm³" (see Slips — the question is unsolvable and the guide's formula fragment uses ¼ instead of ¾); "7.5m" (see Slips — the guide just repeats the given side); 6,000 containers; "1.288cm³ ;1288000m³" (see Slips — the units are swapped; should be 1.288 m³ and 1,288,000 cm³); "13 330" (see Slips — should be 13.33 m³). Activity 11 key (p. 32): cylinder volumes (27,720 m³; 616 cm³; 9,625 m³; 1,884 cm³; 31,400 cm³; 12,560 cm³; 2,772 cm³; 24,553.57 cm³) — all image-dependent; 3a's 2,772 cm³ looks like a full-cylinder value for a half-cylinder figure.
- 2.6 Time, speed and distance (p. 33): Exercise 10 key: 32.4 km/h; 94.44 km/h; 2.45 p.m.; 0.667 m/s; 4.207 km/h; 22.5 km; 96 km/h; 16 km/h; 72 km/h; 77.922 km/h. Only Q3, Q7, Q8 and Q9 are correct (see Slips — Q1 is the right number in the wrong unit; Q2, Q4, Q5, Q6 and Q10 are wrong).

#### Unit 3: GEOMETRY (printed pp. 34–43, PDF pp. 38–47)
- Unit opener (pp. 34–36): "Learn about" promises constructing an equilateral triangle and inscribing/circumscribing it, and "curved patterns from straight lines and nets… to make envelopes, pyramids and prisms" — none of this is in the student book. The introduction (p. 36) says "In this level they shall… delve further into determining surface area and volumes of common geometrical solids" — the student book's Unit 3 contains no surface area or volume.
- 3.1 Inscribing and circumscribing (pp. 36–39): Activity 1 and 2 guidance only (bisect angles / bisect sides); Exercise 1: "Answers may differ because the distance may differ from one learner to the other" — no key. Activity 3 (p. 39): no key. Exercise 2 key (p. 39): 1a) 3; 1b) 17.32 (both image-only); Q2 "(Hypotenuse)² = 10² + (height)²" — a formula restatement, not the length the question asks for (and the question is unsolvable as printed); Q3 "31.32m" ✓ (diagonal of a 9 × 30 frame); Q4 "C" ✓; Q5 "22m" — WRONG, should be 25 m (see Slips).
- 3.2 Pyramids and prisms (pp. 40–41): Activity 4 key (p. 40): "1. 5 faces; 5 vertices, 8 edges" ✓ (square-based pyramid); "2. 5 faces; 6 vertices; 6 edges" — WRONG: a triangular prism has **9 edges**, not 6. Exercise 3 key (p. 41): 1. Pyramid ✓; 2. Prism ✓; 3. Triangular prism ✓; 4. "5 edges; pyramid" (image-only); 5. "None" (image-only — the guide says none of the four figures is a triangular-prism net); 6. "6 vertices" (image-only).
- 3.3 Scale (pp. 41–42): Exercise 4 key (p. 42): 1:50,000 ✓; 1:1,500,000 ✓; "0.0035m²" (see Slips — should be 0.0035 cm², and the plot is rectangular, not triangular); 1:1,200,000 ✓; 2.025 km ✓; 16 cm² ✓; 3.75 km ✓; 9 km by 4 km ✓; 136 m ✓; 1:400,000 ✓.
- 3.4 Coordinates (pp. 42–43): the guide copies the student book's opening paragraph and then gives NO teaching guidance; printed p. 43 (PDF p. 47) is a montage of two reduced student-book pages (printed pp. 63–64: plotting points, the four-quadrants table, Examples 4–5) — read visually. The section has no activity, no exercise, no key.

#### Unit 4: ALGEBRA (printed pp. 44–52, PDF pp. 48–56)
- Unit opener (pp. 44–45): "Learn about" mentions "finding intersection and difference of two sets Q = [A, 3, x] and set R = [A, y, 13, z, 3]" — set difference is never taught in the student book, and these sets do not appear in it.
- 4.1 Simplifying (pp. 46–47): Activity 1 key (p. 47): i) 6d ✓; ii) 4x + 8y ✓; iii) "3a + 8b / 6a + 7b" — WRONG numerator (should be 3a + 16b); iv) 1.05m + 20 ✓; v) 20p − q + 6 ✓; vi) 5.5x + 14y ✓; vii) 20w − 6 ✓.
- 4.2 Word statements (pp. 47–48): Activity 2 key (p. 48): 54 years; 9 cm; 21 years — all correct.
- 4.3 Sets (pp. 48–49): Exercise 1 key (p. 49): (a)–(l) all correct EXCEPT (c) "{−1, 0, 2}" — should be {−1, 0, **1**} (2 is not between −2 and 2) — and (m) "{O}" — the letter O where the digit **0** belongs (repeating the student book's typo). The set-builder descriptions are correct except (k) "{x : x = 1/x, n ∊ N and 1 ≤ n ≤ 6}" — should be 1/**n**, not 1/x.
- 4.4 Finite & infinite sets (pp. 50–52): notes repeated from the student book. Exercise 3 key (p. 51): Set A and Set B lists, intersection {3, 7, 9, 20}, union {3, 7, 9, 10, 14, 15, 19, 20, 23, 24, 25, 26, 30} — all image-dependent (the student book's Venn diagrams), unverifiable from the text layer; note the guide's sets differ from the student book's worked Venn example on p. 73, because they key the Exercise 3 diagrams. Activity 3 key (pp. 51–52): the three-exam Venn worked in full — Science only 19, Maths only 29, English only 14, total 123, none 27 — all correct.

#### Unit 5: STATISTICS (printed pp. 53–63, PDF pp. 57–67)
- Unit opener (pp. 53–55): "Learn about" promises "drawing statistical graphs and determining the values of central tendencies" and the introduction (p. 55) says learners will be "taken through statistical graphs, the values of central tendencies" — the student book's Unit 5 teaches NO mean/median/mode; it reads tables and graphs and does probability. 5.1 Data collection (pp. 55–56): the six steps restated with research examples (poor performance in maths, corruption, road accidents) and sampling methods (simple random, stratified).
- 5.2 Tables and graphs (pp. 56–60): "Guide them through Bar graphs, pie charts, histograms, frequency polygons" — histograms and frequency polygons are not in the student book. Activity 1 key (p. 57): "525 South Sudanese pounds" — image-dependent (the bus-fare table), unverifiable from the text layer. Exercise 1 key (pp. 57–58): football table (Team C 14, Team B 13, Team A 8) ✓; Q2 "Thursday" (image-dependent); Q3 "SSP15000 and 9100" (Monday 15,000, Saturday 9,100 — the two takings, not the difference the question asks for); Q4 "2368" (image-dependent); Q5 "5hours 45 minutes" ✓ (K 9:30 → Q 3:15); Q6 "13 pupils" ✓; Q7 "8 litres" ✓; Q8 "8 pupils" ✓. Activity 2 key (p. 58): Q1 angles "(red 56°, blue 64°, 47°, 104°, 88°); 24°" — the 47° should be **48°** (12/90 × 360) and the difference should be **16°**, not 24° (see Slips); Q2 "SSP 2000" (image-dependent); Q3 "200" (see Slips — under the pictured angles the answer should be negative: 400 boys vs 800 men). Exercise 2 key (p. 59): Q1 angles 64°, 56°, 60°, 72°, 108° ✓; Q2 72° ✓; Q3 "82.3°" — WRONG, should be **96°** (32/120 × 360); Q4 "10.3°" (image-dependent); Q5 "12 oranges" (image-dependent); Q6 "SSP. 3200" (image-dependent; under the pictured angles the transport−rent difference is 7,200, and the question is backwards anyway). Activity 3 key (p. 60): Q1 "114.29km/h" — the question asks for a DISTANCE ("How far from R was the cyclist…"), so a speed is the wrong quantity (see Slips); Q2 "Bar graph" (the task type, not an answer). Exercise 3 key (p. 60): "1. 32km; 2. (a) 51km (b) 50km" — image-dependent (travel graphs), unverifiable.
- 5.3 Probability (pp. 61–63): coin ½, die ⅙, card 1/52, "one of the footballers… 1/22" — all reasonable. Activity 4 key (p. 62): ½, ½, No, No, "Yes" — Q5 ("Is getting an even score on a head as likely as getting a tail an odd score?") is garbled and the guide's "Yes" is meaningless. Activity 5 (die, p. 63) and Activity 6 (bottle top, p. 63) and the second "Activity 6" (two cubes — mis-numbered, should be Activity 7, p. 63): the tables are printed BLANK with no answers and no guidance for questions a–e. Exercise 4 key (p. 63): ¼, 5/9, ¼, 9/19, 11/26 — all correct.

#### Unit 6: BUSINESS ACCOUNTING (printed pp. 64–76, PDF pp. 68–80)
- Unit opener (pp. 64–66): promises business plans, spreadsheets and "break even point" — none of these are in the student book (only Activity 3's visit to a local businessperson).
- 6.1 Commissions (pp. 66–67): Exercise 1 key (p. 67): 38,700; 35,450; 18,750; 45,000; 30,000 — all correct.
- 6.2 Discounts (p. 67): Activity 1 key: 160; 849.50; 900; 20 — all correct.
- 6.3 Hire purchase (p. 68): Exercise 2 key: 1) 21,600 ✓; 2) 48,240 ✓; 3) 960 ✓; 4) "SSP 2166.67" (see Slips — the question is unsolvable as printed; the guide silently assumes ONE instalment); 5) "SSP 4508.33" — WRONG, should be **21,700** (130,200 − 108,500); 6) 4,166.67 ✓; 7) "SSP 60000" — WRONG, should be **24,000** (300,000 − 276,000; the guide appears to have taken 20% off instead of 8%).
- 6.4 Profit and loss (pp. 68–69): Exercise 3 key (p. 69): 1) Loss of SSP 64 ✓; 2) "SSP 6250" — WRONG (cost 56,250, takings 53,750 → LOSS of **2,500**; the guide gives 6,250 with no loss label); 3) SSP 1,850 ✓ (a loss, though the guide does not label it); 4) SSP 160 ✓ (a loss).
- 6.5 Simple interest (pp. 69–70): Exercise 4 key (p. 70): 625,625 ✓; 4 years ✓. Activity 2 key (p. 70): "1. SSP. 6,075; SSP. 51,075; 2. SSP. 27,000; 3. SSP 156,000; 4. SSP 3,000; 5. SSP. 7,500" — MISALIGNED: these are the answers to Q4–Q8, listed under labels 1–5, and Q1–Q3 (rate 4.44%; principal 2,000; rate 5.56%) have no answers (see Slips).
- 6.6 Compound interest (pp. 71–72): Exercise 5 key (p. 71): 233,280 ✓; 316,406.25 ✓; 22,674.816 ✓ (3-year reading of the inconsistent Q3); 46,225 — the AMOUNT, where the question asks for the INTEREST (6,225); 569,856 ✓; 172,800 ✓; 250,880 ✓; 38,160 ✓; "ZACHARIA, BY SSP 5355" ✓ (Zachariah by 5,355; "ZACHARIA" is a typo). Exercise 6 key (p. 72): 1) 15,315.30 (should be 15,315.38 — minor rounding); 2) "SSP 24109" — WRONG, should be **12,628.57** (the guide used only the second year's interest); 3) "SSP 1500" — WRONG, should be **15,000** (off by a factor of 10); 4) 26,480 ✓; 5) 2,160; 15,660; 2,505 ✓ (2,505.6 truncated); 6) 2,500 ✓; 7) "SSP 14745.60" — WRONG, should be **16,000** (the guide multiplied by 1.2² instead of dividing); 8) "SSP 21241.40" — WRONG, should be **4,000**; 9) 37,440 ✓.
- 6.7 Cash accounts (pp. 73–76): Exercise 7 keys given as full worked accounts. Shopkeeper's account (pp. 73–74): balance 121,760 ✓ (the guide keeps the student book's out-of-order weeks without comment). Carpenter's account (p. 75): balance **20,530** — WRONG: the guide mis-copies the bank deposit as 9,900 (student book: 9,990), so its balance is 90 too high; correct balance **20,440**. Poultry account (p. 76): balance 90,300 ✓; but the table writes "LAYERS MASH (63SACKS)" for 7,500 — the student book says 3 sacks (a typo, "63" for "3"); and part (c) (earnings from the poultry farm — 64,300) has no key.

### Companion check (guide vs its student book)
- **Same book, same order:** yes — six units in the same order, and the TOC matches the body's section numbering and page numbers throughout.
- **Guide covers things the student book lacks:** Unit 1 promises multiples/factors of fractions and decimals, mixed-number roots, terminal/recurring decimals; Unit 2 promises spheres, cones, prisms, pyramids and Unit 6 business topics; Unit 3 promises equilateral-triangle construction, curved patterns, envelopes; Unit 4 promises set difference; Unit 5 promises mean/median/mode, histograms, frequency polygons; Unit 6 promises business plans, spreadsheets and break-even points. None of this is in the pupil's book.
- **Student book content the guide does not key:** Exercise 1 (food truck/school, Q1–Q2 — "answers may differ"), Activity 3 (inscribing/circumscribing constructions), the coordinates section (§3.4 — only a montage of student pages), Activities 5–7 (die, bottle top, two cubes — blank tables, no answers), Exercise 7 Q3 part (c) (poultry earnings), the student book's Q1–Q3 in Activity 2 (simple interest), and the thermometer/bar-graph readings. The guide also never corrects any of the student book's slips — it repeats the cm³ conversion error (§2.4), repeats the "l"/"0" set typos, and in Exercise 2 Q4 (hire purchase) silently assumes one instalment instead of flagging the missing number.
- **Guide-only errors:** many and serious (see Slips) — the Exercise 10 speed key (6 of 10 wrong), the Exercise 6 compound-interest key (3 of 9 wrong), the hire-purchase Q5/Q7 answers, the profit/loss Q2 answer, the misaligned simple-interest Activity 2 key, the surface-area Activity 10 key, and the carpenter's account balance.

### What the guide does NOT teach / lacks
- No scheme of work, no lesson plans with timings, no pacing.
- No teaching guidance at all for §3.4 Coordinates (only a montage of student pages), for the inscribing/circumscribing activities, or for the probability experiments (Activities 5–7).
- No diagnostic/remedial guidance; no treatment of the student book's errors.

### Held-copy defects
- Printed p. 43 (PDF p. 47) is a reduced-size montage of two student-book pages (printed pp. 63–64) — legible as a layout reference; I read it visually. Otherwise no missing pages (printed pp. 1–76 run continuously, PDF pp. 1–80 complete), no blurred pages. The degree symbol renders as "0" in the text layer in places — an extraction artefact, not a print error. Many keys are numeric tables with very little prose (hence the thin text layer on 26 pages) — the content is present, just mostly numbers.

### Slips and wrong answers in the guide itself (must never be taught as fact)
1. **Printed p. 33, Exercise 10 key (time/speed/distance):** Q1 "32.4km/h" — the question asks for the average speed **in m/s**; 32.4 km/h = **9 m/s**. Q2 "94.44km/h" should be **90 km/h** (510 km ÷ 5 h 40 min). Q4 "0.667m/s" should be **0.5 m/s** (300 m ÷ 600 s). Q5 "4.207km/h" should be **5.23 km/h** (24.4 km ÷ 4⅔ h). Q6 "22.5km" should be **245.25 km** (the distance between towns R and S; 22.5 km is only Saima's distance from S). Q10 "77.922km/h" should be **80 km/h** (300 km ÷ 3 h 45 min). Only Q3 (2.45 p.m.), Q7 (96 km/h), Q8 (16 km/h) and Q9 (72 km/h) are correct.
2. **Printed p. 72, Exercise 6 key (compound interest):** Q2 "SSP 24109" should be **12,628.57** (the guide used only the second year's interest, 11%, instead of the sum of both years, 21%). Q3 "SSP 1500" should be **15,000** (off by a factor of 10). Q7 "SSP 14745.60" should be **16,000** (the guide multiplied 10,240 by 1.2² instead of dividing). Q8 "SSP 21241.40" should be **4,000** (4,928 ÷ (1.1 × 1.12)).
3. **Printed p. 68, Exercise 2 key Q5 (hire purchase):** "SSP 4508.33" should be **SSP 21,700** (HP 130,200 − cash 108,500). Q7 "SSP 60000" should be **SSP 24,000** (HP 300,000 − cash 276,000; the guide appears to have taken 20% off instead of 8%).
4. **Printed p. 69, Exercise 3 key Q2 (profit/loss):** "SSP 6250" — there is no profit: cost 56,250, takings 53,750, a LOSS of **SSP 2,500**. The guide's figure matches nothing and carries no loss label.
5. **Printed p. 70, Activity 2 key (simple interest):** the five answers (6,075; 51,075; 27,000; 156,000; 3,000; 7,500) are the answers to Q4–Q8, listed under labels 1–5. Q1 (rate 4.44% p.a.), Q2 (principal 2,000) and Q3 (rate 5.56% p.a.) have no answers.
6. **Printed p. 29, Activity 10 key (surface area):** 1b) "73,5m²" is the answer for item c (the 3½ m cube), not item b; the open cuboid (8 × 7½ × 4⅕ cm) should be **190.2 cm²**, which the guide never gives; 1c) "225.75cm²" matches no item. Q4 "155 cm²" is the LATERAL area (the painted parts); the question asks for the area of the parts NOT painted (base + top = **49.5 cm²**). Q5 "45m²" should be **73.8 m²** (base 9 m² + 4 sides of 16.2 m²).
7. **Printed p. 30–31, §2.4:** the guide repeats the student book's wrong rule — "To convert cm³ to m³, divide the value given by 10000" (should be **1,000,000**) — and prints "1m³ = 10000cm³" inside Example 14 Q2 while the working divides by 1,000,000.
8. **Printed p. 31, Exercise 9 key:** Q5 "¼ xh (45 x 25) cm³" — the question is unsolvable (no height) and the guide's formula fragment uses ¼ instead of **¾**. Q6 "7.5m" — the guide just repeats the given cube side; the question ("level becomes 5cm high") is garbled and unanswerable. Q8 "1.288cm³ ;1288000m³" — the units are swapped: should be **1.288 m³** and **1,288,000 cm³**. Q9 "13 330" — should be **13.33 m³** (0.43 m³/day × 31 days).
9. **Printed p. 12, Exercise 2 key Q1:** "a. 12 b. 13 c. 25 d. 14 e. 17" — these are the square roots, not the missing multipliers (√144 = 12 × **1**; √169 = **1** × 13; √196 = 14 × **1**; √289 = **1** × 17). Q1c as printed in the student book ("√225 = 25 × ____") is unanswerable; the guide's "25" does not fix it.
10. **Printed p. 13, Activity 2 key:** Q1 "46cm" is the SIDE of the square, not the perimeter in metres the question asks for (**184 m**). Q3 "3.756594km" should be **8.4 km** (the guide treated the total area of 5 cowsheds as one cowshed). Q5 "22" — √195 + 17² is ≈ 303 (or 303 if √196 was meant); 22 matches nothing.
11. **Printed p. 22, Activity 2 key Q1:** "360m" — the question ("four runs around a pitch of length 90m") gives no width and is unsolvable as printed; the guide just multiplies 4 × 90.
12. **Printed p. 23, Exercise 5 key Q4:** "D = 24.507cm; R = 12.25cm" — D should be **24.5 cm** (77 × 7/22), and R = 12.25 cm is inconsistent with the guide's own D (half of 24.507 is 12.2535).
13. **Printed p. 39, Exercise 2 key Q5:** "22m" should be **25 m** (√(24² + 7²)). Q2 "(Hypotenuse)² = 10² + (height)²" is a formula restatement, not the length asked for.
14. **Printed p. 40, Activity 4 key Q2:** "5 faces; 6 vertices; 6 edges" — a triangular prism has **9 edges**, not 6.
15. **Printed p. 42, Exercise 4 key Q3:** "0.0035m²" should be **0.0035 cm²** (and the plot is rectangular, not triangular as the question says).
16. **Printed p. 47, Activity 1 key iii:** "3a + 8b / 6a + 7b" — the numerator should be **3a + 16b** (9a + 12b − 6a + 4b).
17. **Printed p. 49, Exercise 1 key (sets):** (c) "{−1, 0, 2}" should be {−1, 0, **1**} (2 is not between −2 and 2); (m) "{O}" — the letter O where the digit **0** belongs; (k) "{x : x = 1/x, …}" should be 1/**n**.
18. **Printed p. 58, Activity 2 key Q1:** the third angle "47°" should be **48°** (12/90 × 360), and the blue-vs-green difference "24°" should be **16°** (64° − 48°). Q3 "200" does not match the pictured angles (400 boys vs 800 men — 400 FEWER boys than men).
19. **Printed p. 59, Exercise 2 key Q3:** "82.3°" should be **96°** ((17 + 15)/120 × 360). Q6 "SSP. 3200" does not match the pictured angles (transport 9,000 − rent 1,800 = 7,200, and the question is backwards).
20. **Printed p. 60, Activity 3 key Q1:** "114.29km/h" is a SPEED; the question asks for a DISTANCE ("How far from R was the cyclist…").
21. **Printed p. 27, Exercise 7 key Q6:** "19.25cm²" answers in cm² where the student book asks "in square metres" (**0.001925 m²**).
22. **Printed p. 27, Activity 9 key Q1:** "150m²" — the question asks for the WIDTH, so the unit should be **150 m**, not m².
23. **Printed p. 71, Exercise 5 key Q4:** "SSP 46225" is the AMOUNT; the question asks for the total INTEREST, which is **SSP 6,225**.
24. **Printed p. 75, Carpenter's cash account:** balance **20,530** is wrong — the guide mis-copies the bank deposit as 9,900 (student book: 9,990); correct balance **20,440**.
25. **Printed p. 76, Poultry cash account:** "LAYERS MASH (63SACKS)" for SSP 7,500 — the student book says **3 sacks** (a typo, "63" for "3"); part (c) (earnings — **64,300**) has no key.
26. **Printed p. 68, Exercise 2 key Q4:** "SSP 2166.67" silently assumes ONE instalment of 5,000; the question never states the number of instalments, so it is unsolvable as printed.
27. **Printed p. 15, §1.5 method:** "To convert decimals to percentages, write the figure in decimal without the decimal point and divide it by 100" — garbled/wrong as a method (the key itself uses the correct ×100 method).

## Band B cross-notes (for course building)

- **Ladder.** P5 six-digit numbers, divisibility for 3/4/6/9, primes, Roman numerals, HCF/LCM, fractions with LCM, decimals, cube/cuboid volume, temperature, parallel-line and angle construction, one-step equations → P6 numbers to a million, divisibility by 8/11, squares/roots, decimal–fraction–percent conversions, ratio/proportion, π and circle area, acres/hectares, triangle area, tonnes, two-step equations, expanding brackets, coordinates → P7 squares/roots of fractions and decimals, cubes, unitary method, percentage change, circumference, quadrilateral areas, surface area, speed, sets/Venn, mean/median/mode, probability, business accounting → P8 factors/multiples with negatives, squares/roots by prime factors and averaging, perimeter/area of all plane shapes, surface area and volume of cubes/cuboids/cylinders, time–speed–distance, inscribed/circumscribed circles, Pythagoras, pyramids/prisms, scale, coordinates, sets, probability, compound interest, cash accounts.
- **Guides over-promise.** Every P5–P8 guide's front-matter promises topics the student book never teaches (litres↔ml, acute/obtuse/reflex angles, scale drawing, coordinates, rotational symmetry, protractor use, translation, grouped-data statistics, business plans/spreadsheets, break-even). A lesson built from a guide promise alone would have no book behind it — build only from what the student books actually teach, and treat guide-only topics as absent.
- **First appearances.** Sets and Venn diagrams first appear in P7 (4.4–4.7); probability first appears in P7 (5.7) and is single-event only; compound interest and cash accounts first appear in P8; coordinates first appear in P6 (plotting only, no exercises set) and properly in P8 (3.4).
- **What to carry, corrected.** P5 student loss example 480,000 − 440,000 = 4,000 (correct 40,000); P5 pie-chart percentages (chicken 66.7%, not 58%); P6 student 1/3 = 0.3 (should be 0.333…); P7 student median "150" for 3, 9, 15, 17, 44 (correct 15); P7 guide temperature formulas without the 32 offset; P8 student cuboid surface-area formula and cm³→m³ "divide by 10,000" (correct 1,000,000); P8 guide speed key 6 of 10 wrong and compound-interest key 3 of 9 wrong.


# BAND C — Secondary 1, 2, 4 (8 files, 862 PDF pages)

All 8 books are in English; all 8 filings are in English. Same 2018 Ministry series, Mountain Top Publishers. There is no Secondary 3 (Grade 11) book in this set at all — the whole S3 set is link-only, and the S4 books assume S3 units (Trigonometry 1, Calculus 1–2, Complex Numbers 1) that are not in hand.


## Secondary 1, student — Student's Book 1 (© 2018; 132 PDF pp.; printed = PDF − 6)

Citation check 2026-11-05: 22 anchors — 22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found. (Spot-checks: 4 checked, 1 filing claim proved wrong — the p. 126 slip; see corrected bullet below.)

### Book identity

- **File read:** `g09-student.pdf` — 132 PDF pages, 9,323,838 bytes.
- **Title as printed:** Secondary Mathematics, Student's Book 1 (Secondary 1).
- **Publisher / imprint as printed:** "MOUNTAIN TOP PUBLISHERS. Published by: Secondary Mathematics has been written and developed by Ministry of General Education and Instruction, Government of South Sudan in conjunction with Subjects experts." Property of the Ministry of General Education and Instruction; "THIS BOOK IS NOT FOR SALE". Funded by the Global Partnership for Education (logo on the title page). Foreword signed Deng Deng Hoc Yai (Hon.), Minister of General Education and Instruction; the book was approved for use in all schools of the Republic of South Sudan except international schools, "with effect from 4th February, 2019". The foreword thanks Longhorn and Mountain Top publishers in Kenya, UNICEF South Sudan, the Curriculum Foundation (UK) and others.
- **Edition / year:** ©2018, The Republic of South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction (shown on the title page; see defect note below — in this copy the title page and foreword are scanned images, so the © line is visible in the rendered image but absent from the text layer).
- **ISBN:** NONE printed anywhere in the book (full-text search finds no ISBN). **No individual authors are named** — authorship is credited only to the Ministry collectively.
- **Pages actually read:** all 132 PDF pages (printed pp. 1–126). Printed page = PDF page − 6 throughout (PDF p. 7 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 132 = printed p. 126). No printed page is missing from the numbering sequence.
- **Language:** English throughout. Currency used in money problems is the South Sudanese Pound (SSP).

### Method (honest)

Read from the extracted text layer, page by page, using a per-page digest (headings, worked examples, exercise starts) checked against the full text of every unit, plus full-text reads of the worked examples I verified for arithmetic and of every page where an error is reported below. Two pages carry almost no text layer and were read from rendered images: PDF p. 4 (the Foreword, a scanned image) and PDF p. 66 / printed p. 60 (Exercise 6, which is diagrams only). Exercise question lists were counted and skimmed for content and errors, not read word-by-word; all exposition, definitions, worked examples and unit openings were read in full. Keyword searches over the whole text layer back the "does not teach" list. Where a formula is garbled in extraction I checked the rendered page before calling it an error.

### Contents, unit by unit (own-words summary; printed pages)

**Front matter (PDF pp. 1–6, unnumbered).** Series blurb page, "How to take care of your books" (do's and don'ts), title/imprint page, Foreword (image), Table of Contents (printed pp. 5–6 of the PDF). Note: the contents list omits the triangles section (printed pp. 49–55) from its Unit 3 listing.

**Unit 1 — Natural numbers, factors and proportion (printed pp. 1–27).** Counting/natural/whole numbers and place value (pp. 1–2); factors, even/odd, prime/composite numbers (pp. 2–4); highest common factor and lowest common multiple, including by prime factors (pp. 4–5); fractions — types, comparing, ordering, addition/subtraction, multiplication, division by reciprocals, mixed numbers (pp. 6–16); fractions, percentages and decimals, recurring decimals and converting decimals to fractions (pp. 16–19); standard form (p. 20); percentages (pp. 21–22); profit and loss, including percentage profit/loss and overheads (pp. 23–27); a group project on stocking a kiosk (p. 27). Exercises 1–16 run through the unit.

**Unit 2 — Measurement (printed pp. 28–47).** The metric system and conversion of units (pp. 28–29); perimeter and circumference of a circle (pp. 29–32); area of polygons and of a circle, by counting squares and by formula (pp. 33–36); surface area of solids with flat faces and of solids with curved surfaces — cylinders, cones, spheres (pp. 37–39); volume, volume formulae for prisms, pyramids, cones and spheres (pp. 40–44); capacity and the volume–capacity link, including litres (pp. 45–47). Exercises 1–10.

**Unit 3 — Geometry and trigonometry (printed pp. 48–89).** Angles and lines (pp. 48–49); triangles — classification by sides and by angles, angle sum, isosceles-triangle investigation (pp. 49–55); polygons and their angle sums (pp. 56–57); quadrilaterals — six special types, angle sum, and a diagram-only exercise (pp. 57–60); solids and polyhedra (pp. 61–62); transformation geometry — translation (with vectors), rotation, reflection, enlargement with scale factor and centre (pp. 63–73); bearings, clockwise from north as three figures (pp. 74–77); scale drawings (pp. 78–79); right-angled trigonometry — sine, cosine, tangent, navigation problems (pp. 80–85); angles of elevation and depression (pp. 86–89). Exercises 1–15.

**Unit 4 — Algebra: expressions, equations and inequalities (printed pp. 90–108).** Simplifying expressions and algebraic substitution, including substituting negative numbers (pp. 90–94); expansion of one and two brackets (pp. 95–98); factorising, including quadratics of the form x² + bx + c (pp. 99–102); solving equations in one unknown (p. 103); simultaneous equations by elimination/substitution, introduced with a coffee-and-cake money problem (pp. 104–105); forming and solving inequalities (pp. 106–108). Exercises 1–7.

**Unit 5 — Statistics and probability (printed pp. 109–126).** Mean, mode and median, with frequency tables and grouped data (pp. 109–113); probability — what chance means, experimental probability, sample space, theoretical probability, complementary events (pp. 114–120); using grids to find probabilities (pp. 121–122); tree diagrams for two-stage events (pp. 123–126). Exercises 1–6. The book ends mid-exercise on printed p. 126 (Exercise 6, question 7) with no closing section, summary or back matter.

### What this book does NOT teach (proven from a full read + full-text search)

No complex numbers, no matrices, no calculus, no derivatives or integrals, no surds, no logarithms, no quadratic equations as a solving topic (quadratics appear only as expressions to expand/factorise), no Pythagoras' theorem (right-angled triangles are used for trig ratios but the theorem itself is never stated), no sets or Venn diagrams, no functions, no probability distributions, no standard deviation, no loci, no circle theorems, no logarithms, no sequences/series. Discount and commission are also absent from this student book (they appear only in the Teacher's Guide).

### Slips and errors found (do not teach as fact)

- **Printed p. 23, Example 1:** "John bought a basin for SSP 172. He later sold it for 208 pounds." The currency changes mid-question — the solution treats it as SSP 208 (profit SSP 36, which is arithmetically right for 208 − 172). The word "pounds" should be SSP.
- **Printed p. 51:** "A triangle is right angled if the greatest angle is a right angle (900)" — the degree sign has collapsed into "900"; it should read 90°.
- **Printed p. 108, inequalities worked example:** "Divide both sides of 2(y + 5) < 16 by 2, to get y + 5 < 8. Therefore y = 3" — the conclusion is wrong: y + 5 < 8 gives **y < 3**, not y = 3.
- **Printed p. 120, Exercise 3, question 3(a):** "In a class of 32 students, eight have one first name, nineteen have two first names, and five have three first names… Determine the probability that the student has: (a) no first name" — the data accounts for all 32 students with at least one first name, so the answer is 0; the question only makes sense if a "no first name" group existed. Likely a data slip; treat the intended answer (0) with care.
- **Printed p. 126, Exercise 6, question 7 (last page):** the probabilities as printed are 2/3 (rain tomorrow) and 1/4 (carry umbrella) — both valid; there is no error in the fractions. [CHECK 2026-11-05: the filing's earlier claim that the book prints 3/2 and 4/1 was wrong — the rendered page clearly shows 2/3 and 1/4.] The page does misspell "tomorrow" as "tommorow" and "probability" as "probabality".
- The profit/loss worked examples on printed pp. 23–25 were checked and are arithmetically correct (e.g. p. 25: oranges at SSP 56 for 20, sold at SSP 35 per dozen → gain 4 1/6% — correct).

### Held-copy defects (this copy, not the text)

- **PDF pp. 3–4 are scanned images with no usable text layer:** the title/imprint page and the whole Foreword. They render cleanly and were read visually; the ©2018 line, the GPE logo and the minister's signature are all visible in the image. Any workflow that relies on the text layer will miss the imprint and foreword of this book.
- **PDF p. 66 / printed p. 60 (Exercise 6)** is diagrams only — nine quadrilateral angle diagrams plus a crossed-quadrilateral problem — with almost no text layer. Read visually; content is intact.
- **Series template errors carried into this secondary book:** PDF p. 1 says "All the courses in this primary series were developed…" and "designed to meet the primary school syllabus", and misspells "equipping" as "equiping". The title-page text layer also carries a stray token "PRI" (primary). The book itself is Secondary 1.
- The Table of Contents omits the triangles section (printed pp. 49–55) from Unit 3.

### S3 gap note

There is no Secondary 3 (Grade 11) book in this set at all, in hand or otherwise in Band C. Nothing in this filing describes S3 content, and no S3 material should be inferred from this S1 book.

### Citation anchors (checked 2026-11-05 — see the check line at the top of this section)

1. Natural numbers and place value — Secondary 1 Student's Book, printed p. 1–2: digits 0–9, counting numbers, place value of 3472.
2. Prime and composite numbers — printed p. 3: a prime has only two factors, itself and 1.
3. Highest common factor by prime factors — printed p. 4.
4. Lowest common multiple — printed p. 5.
5. Comparing fractions / LCM 60 ordering example — printed p. 8.
6. Division of fractions by reciprocals — printed p. 15.
7. Recurring decimals written with a dot over the repeating figure — printed p. 17.
8. Standard form for very large/small numbers — printed p. 20.
9. Percentages from fractions and decimals — printed p. 21.
